# Learning Grove · Little Sprouts (ages 3–6, read aloud): side stories and fun quests

## S1. The Wardens’ Walk

- `s1-step1`: Walk with Moss to the stream lantern. Go slowly! · _Stay right beside Moss. If you go too fast, he stops and waits._
- `s1-step-boats`: Float a leaf-boat down the stream to the pond. · _Tap the water behind the boat. Keep it away from the reeds. You can always try again._
- `s1-step2`: Walk with Moss to the lantern by the old wall. · _The old wall is on the north side of the Grove. Go on a quiet evening._
- `s1-step3`: Walk with Moss to his own lantern, by the pond. · _Go in the evening. Moss likes the evening light._
- `s1-step4`: Moss will tell you about the last lantern later. · _Keep playing the story. Moss will come and find you._

#### `s1-ask`

_Night. Three lanterns glow. One post is dark._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, do): count to 4; 3 and 1 more make 4  
> _(Count the posts together: one, two, three, four.)_  
> **Moss**: Three bright ones, and one dark one. That makes four.  
> Check: `edu:count:lantern-posts:4`
> _For grown-ups: Counted four lantern posts and saw that three and one more make four._

_Moss sits by your boot. He looks at the dark post._  
**Moss**: Not tonight. Ask me something else.  
**Moss**: Each lantern post keeps a little memory.  
**Moss**: Four posts. Four memories. Shall we walk to them?  
**Moss**: Slowly, mind. I am older than the trees.  

**Choices**

- **Walk slowly beside him.**
    - Step… step… step. Slow and steady.
    - **Moss**: Good. You didn’t huff. Hazel huffed for years.
- **Offer to carry him.**
    - **Moss**: No, thank you. I have legs.
    - **Moss**: Short ones. Old ones. But mine.
- **Ask which lantern comes first.**
    - **Moss**: Briar’s, by the stream. She hated waiting!

#### `s1-briar`

_The stream lantern hisses in the damp. Fssss._  
**Moss**: This was Briar’s post. Briar loved to laugh.  
**Moss**: She laughed at frogs. She laughed at rain. She laughed at me!  
**Moss**: When Briar was very, very old, she died.  
**Moss**: That means her body stopped working. It won’t start again.  
_The light turns gold. A happy voice whispers._  
**Briar**: If I’m not back by spring, plant something nice!  
**Moss**: A yellow flower grew where she sat. The very first one.  
**Moss**: When I see it, I remember her laugh.  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, notice): remembering someone we love who has died  
> **Moss**: We keep remembering the ones we love. With flowers. With stories.  
> _(Ask: what makes you laugh, like Briar? Wait for an answer.)_  
> _For grown-ups: Heard plainly what dying means, and that flowers and stories help us remember people we love._


#### `s1-boats-ask`

**Moss**: Briar had a game. Leaf-boats!  
**Moss**: A leaf, a candle a grown-up lights, and off down the stream.  
**Moss**: If yours reaches the pond, you must laugh out loud.  
**Moss**: That’s the rule. Briar made it up.  
_Moss folds a big green leaf. Fold, fold, fold._  
**Moss**: Mine always tips over. Every single year.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, do): wind pushes a sail  
> **Moss**: A sail catches the wind. The wind pushes the boat along.  
> _(Blow like the wind together. Whoosh!)_  
> Check: `edu:blow:wind:sail`
> _For grown-ups: Learned that wind pushes on a sail and moves a boat, and blew like the wind._


**Choices**

- **Make yours wide and flat.**
    - **Moss**: Briar’s way! Flat as a pancake.
- **Give yours a sail.**
    - **Moss**: A sail! Nobody ever thought of a sail!
    - **Moss**: Briar would have loved that.

#### `s1-boats`

_Splash! Moss’s boat tips over at once._  
**Moss**: Every year.  
_Your boat bobs past the stepping stones._  
_It spins at the reeds. Round and round!_  
_Then it slides onto the pond. The candle is still lit!_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.K.3, ask): first, next, last  
> **Moss**: Tell me the boat’s trip. Where did it go first?  
> - ✅ Past the stepping stones → Yes! First the stones. Next the reeds. Last, the pond!
> - Round the reeds → That came next. First it passed the stones. Then the reeds. Then the pond.
> - Onto the pond → That came last! First the stones, next the reeds, last the pond.
> _For grown-ups: Retold a short journey in order using first, next and last._

_Your Nubbin sits down, plop, in surprise._  
**Moss**: Well? Rules are rules!  
_(Laugh out loud together. Ha ha ha!)_  
_Your laugh rings out across the water._  
**Moss**: There. That’s Briar’s sound. I missed it.  

#### `s1-wall-ask`

_A few evenings later, Moss waits at the gate._  
**Moss**: The old wall next. Hush’s post.  
_Byte leans on the wall with a notebook._  
**Byte**: Is he really talking? Ask him something only he knows!  
**Moss**: Tell Byte his shoelace is undone.  
_Byte looks down. It is! Byte goes very quiet._  
**Moss**: Hush was the quiet one. Hush was a good listener.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): quiet, careful listening  
> **Moss**: Let’s listen like Hush. Shh.  
> _(Sit very still. Can you name one sound you hear?)_  
> Check: `edu:listen:sounds:quiet`
> _For grown-ups: Practised sitting still and listening carefully, then named a sound they heard._

**You**: What did Hush listen to?  
**Moss**: Everything. Come on. Mind the step.  

#### `s1-hush`

_The lantern by the old wall burns low and quiet._  
**Moss**: After the long white winter, some Nubbins couldn’t move.  
**Moss**: They sat very still, just where they were put.  
**Moss**: Everyone else was busy. Hush was not.  
**Moss**: Hush sat with them. Every single day.  
**Moss**: She didn’t try to fix them. She kept them company.  
**Moss**: So they were never on their own.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): helping a sad friend by staying with them  
> **Moss**: If a friend feels sad, what could you do?  
> - ✅ Sit beside them and stay → Yes. That’s what Hush did. Staying close helps.
> - Leave them on their own → They might feel lonely then. Hush would sit beside them. Staying helps.
> - Tell them to stop being sad → Sad feelings don’t stop when we’re told. Sitting with them helps more.
> _For grown-ups: Thought about how to help a sad friend: staying close and keeping them company._

**Moss**: Hush said that was a kind of keeping.  

#### `s1-pond-ask`

_Moss is by the pond, at his own post._  
**Moss**: My lantern. I like this one best.  
**Moss**: Sit beside me. Closer. There.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, do): position words: beside, on, behind  
> _(Where are you sitting? Beside Moss!)_  
> **Moss**: Beside me. Not on me. Not behind me. Beside.  
> Check: `edu:sit:beside:moss`
> _For grown-ups: Used the position words beside, on and behind._

**Moss**: That’s where Hazel sat. Long, long ago.  

**Choices**

- **Sit where Hazel sat.**
    - The grass is flat there. She sat here lots.
    - **Moss**: Just right.
- **Ask why he chose her.**
    - **Moss**: Patience! I’m getting to it.

#### `s1-hazel`

**Moss**: Hazel came here young. She was in a big hurry.  
**Moss**: She wanted the Nubbins to love her by Tuesday!  
_The light turns gold. It’s a memory. Snow falls._  
_A young Hazel sits by the frozen pond. Her coat is too big!_  

> **Lesson** (science, 2-ESS2-3, notice): water freezes into ice and melts back  
> **Moss**: In winter, the pond freezes. Cold turns water into ice.  
> **Moss**: When it warms up, the ice melts back into water. Splash!  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that cold turns water into ice, and warmth melts it back into water._

**Young Hazel**: I don’t know what to do. Do you?  
**Moss**: I sat beside her all winter. I said nothing.  
**Moss**: She wriggled and fidgeted. But she never asked me to talk.  
**Moss**: When the ice melted, I looked up and said, “You’ll do.”  
**Moss**: She laughed so hard, she fell in the pond!  
**Moss**: Sit next to them and wait. That’s Hazel’s third rule.  

#### `s1-wait`

_You look at the last post, by the long grass._  
**Moss**: Not that one. Not yet.  
_Down the lane, children skip and sing._  
_“Cinder held them close and tight, / kept them warm one winter night;”_  
_“when the morning came to call, / they did not move at all.”_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): hearing rhymes  
> _(Say the rhyme again together.)_  
> **Moss**: Which word sounds like “tight”?  
> - ✅ night → Yes! Tight, night. They rhyme!
> - cold → Cold and tight don’t sound alike. Tight… night! Those rhyme.
> - snow → Snow rhymes with go. Tight rhymes with night!
> _For grown-ups: Listened to a skipping rhyme and picked out a rhyming pair (tight, night)._

**Moss**: They have the words right. But they have her wrong.  
**Moss**: I’ll tell you about Cinder. Another day.  
**Moss**: Now: feed them, and off to bed.  

#### `s1-cinder`

_Winter now. Frost creeps out of the long grass._  
_Moss is at your door. He looks very faint._  
**Moss**: All four are home. You may carry me this time.  
_You carry him gently. He is light as a leaf._  
**Moss**: Let me tell you about Cinder’s last warm day.  
**Moss**: It was so hot! The Nubbins lay flat in the sun.  
**Moss**: Cinder carried them into the shade. One at a time.  

> **Lesson** (science, K-PS3-1, notice): shade is cooler than sunshine  
> **Moss**: The Sun warms the ground. Shade blocks some of the Sun.  
> **Moss**: So shade is cooler than sunshine. Cinder knew that.  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that sunlight warms the ground and shade is cooler._

**Cinder**: Out of the sun, you silly things! I’ve got you!  
**Moss**: That night, snow came. In summer. And it didn’t stop.  
**Moss**: Cinder loved them very much. But she held them too tight.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): gentle hugs, and asking first  
> **Moss**: How should we hug a small friend?  
> - ✅ Gently, and only if they want a hug → Yes. Gentle hugs, and we ask first.
> - As tight as we can → Tight hugs can hurt small friends. Gentle is best. And we ask first.
> _For grown-ups: Talked about gentle hugs and asking first, through Cinder’s love that held too tight._


#### `s1-complete`

**Moss**: There. Now two of us remember her warm.  
_Moss pushes something to your boot. An acorn on a string!_  
**Moss**: Hazel gave me that, long ago. Now it’s yours.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): an acorn is a seed that grows into an oak tree  
> **Moss**: An acorn is a seed. Plant it, and wait…  
> **Moss**: …a long, long time. It grows into a big oak tree!  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that an acorn is the seed of an oak tree._

_On the post hangs a soft hat with a tiny lantern._  
_The note says: “I MADE YOU THIS! —SPARKS”_  
_That night, you draw the four posts in your book._  
_Moss falls asleep on your windowsill. Shh._  

## S2. Wisp

- `s2-step1`: Sit up at night by Sable’s fruit. Keep very still. · _Leave fruit by the long grass. Then wait nearby, still as a post._
- `s2-step2`: Watch out for Wisp. When the big rain comes, find where he sleeps. · _This happens later, in the rain. Warm things went somewhere warm._
- `s2-step3`: Keep making friends with Sable. Wisp is never far away. · _Later, when Sable leans on you, visit Luna’s garden at night._

#### `s2-fruit`

_Morning. The fruit for Sable is gone._  
_But Sable looks thin. She didn’t eat it._  
_There is frost on the grass round her. In summer!_  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS2-1, ask): frost comes when it is very cold  
> **Luna**: Frost is tiny bits of ice. When do we see frost?  
> - ✅ When it is very cold → Yes! Frost comes when it’s very cold. That’s why summer frost is strange.
> - On a hot summer day → Summer is warm, so frost should melt. Frost comes when it’s very cold. That’s why this is strange!
> _For grown-ups: Learned that frost is ice that comes in cold weather, so frost in summer is strange._

**Luna**: Something else came in the dark. It took the fruit.  
**You**: Another Nubbin?  
**Luna**: A grey one. Even thinner than Sable.  
**Luna**: Sit up tonight. Keep still. Still as a post.  
**Luna**: Let’s find out what he wants.  

#### `s2-thief`

_Night. Sable creeps out. She takes one bite._  
_Then a thin grey Nubbin comes. One ear is torn._  
_He pushes Sable aside. He pulls the fruit away._  
_He sees your lantern. He presses his cheek on the warm glass._  
_He is shaking. Shiver, shiver._  
**Wisp**: Hsss! Hers! Not yours!  
_Then, very small, a second voice._  
**Wisp**: …she ate. she ate today. good.  

> **Lesson** (music, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): loud and quiet voices  
> _(Hiss like Wisp: hsss! Now whisper softly: she ate.)_  
> **Luna**: One loud voice. One quiet voice.  
> Check: `edu:voice:loud-quiet`
> _For grown-ups: Played with a loud voice and a quiet voice._

_Whoosh. He’s gone._  
**Luna**: Let’s call him Wisp.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, do): first sound w  
> _(Say it together: w-w-Wisp.)_  
> **Luna**: Wisp starts with a “w” sound. Like wind and water!  
> Check: `edu:say:first-sound:w`
> _For grown-ups: Heard and said the first sound “w” in Wisp, wind and water._


#### `s2-missing`

_Next day, more things go missing. Warm things._  
**Fern**: My left glove is gone! The right one is here.  
_A sock. A candle. The tea cosy. Gran’s warm egg stone._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5  
> _(Count the missing things on your fingers.)_  
> **Fern**: Glove, sock, candle, tea cosy, stone. Five!  
> Check: `edu:count:missing-things:5`
> _For grown-ups: Counted five missing things on their fingers._

**Clover**: He’s cold, that’s all. Can we feed him too?  
**Luna**: He hisses at Sable. And she listens to him.  
**Clover**: Somebody taught him to be scared.  
_Up in the fruit tree, Wisp watches you._  

**Choices**

- **Put down two piles of fruit. One each.**
    - Wisp eats his pile. Then most of hers!
    - **Clover**: Not quite fair. But he ate.
- **Clap and shoo him away.**
    - Clap! Wisp runs. Sable jumps too.
    - **Luna**: Claps scare small things. Use a soft voice next time.
- **Sit down and wait.**
    - You sit. Wisp hisses, and then he stops.
    - Sable eats. She watches you both.
    - **Luna**: Waiting works on him too!

#### `s2-rain`

_It rains all day and all night. Pitter-patter._  
_The stream is big and brown and loud._  
_Behind the cottage is a soggy nest._  
_Fern’s glove. The sock. The tea cosy. All wet._  
_Wisp sits on a log, shaking. He holds Gran’s egg stone._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): shivering helps a cold body warm up  
> **Clover**: Bodies shake when they’re cold. Shivering helps them warm up.  
> _(Shiver like Wisp. Brrr!)_  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that shivering is the body’s way of warming up when it is cold._

**Wisp**: mine. warm. mine… was warm.  
**Clover**: The bathhouse is warm. Please, let’s help him.  
**Luna**: He might wriggle. Hold him gently anyway.  

**Choices**

- **Pick him up. Hold on gently.**
    - He wriggles. Then he stops.
    - In the warm bathhouse, he bites your hand. Ouch!
    - **Wisp**: Hsss. Hands!
    - **Clover**: He’s scared of hands. But look. He’s warm now.
    - He is warm. But he is still shaking.
- **Get a blanket. Let Clover lift him.**
    - Clover wraps him up like a loaf of bread.
    - He bites her, right through the blanket. Ouch!
    - **Clover**: Ow. That’s fair. He’s scared.
    - He is warm now. But he is still shaking.

#### `s2-number`

**Clover**: Warm. Dry. Fed. And still shaking.  
**Luna**: So it was never the cold.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): naming a feeling: scared  
> **Luna**: Wisp is warm now. But he still shakes. How does he feel?  
> - ✅ Scared → Yes. Wisp is scared. Being scared can make us shake too.
> - Cold → He was cold before. He’s warm now. So it’s fear. Wisp feels scared.
> - Sleepy → Sleepy Nubbins go floppy, not shaky. Wisp feels scared.
> _For grown-ups: Named a feeling (scared) from clues, and learned that fear can make us shake._

_Rain drums on the roof. Drum, drum, drum._  
_On Wisp’s side is an old grey number. Painted on._  
**Luna**: I’ve seen paint like that before. I’ll ask around.  
_Outside, Sable sniffs your hands. She doesn’t run!_  

#### `s2-night`

_Night in Luna’s garden. The moonflowers are open._  
_Wisp sits close to Sable. He whispers to her._  
**Wisp**: hands are scary. come away. come to the dark.  
_Sable looks at Wisp. Then at your window._  
**Luna**: He wants her to leave the Grove. I want her safe.  
_Clover comes in her dressing gown, with fruit._  
**Clover**: We feed him because we’re kind. Not because he’s good.  
**Luna**: I care about Sable. Somebody has to.  
**Clover**: And Wisp has nobody at all.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): friends can disagree and both be kind  
> _Luna and Clover want different things._  
> _They are both being kind. Friends can disagree and stay friends._  
> _For grown-ups: Saw two friends disagree kindly, each caring about someone different._

_Luna and Clover both look at you._  

**Choices**

- **Keep feeding him. Every single day.**
    - Each morning, two piles of fruit. One for Sable. One for Wisp.
    - On the fourth morning, Wisp eats facing you.
    - **Wisp**: hands… maybe not hers. maybe not.
    - Sable sees. She comes one step closer.
    - **Luna**: I hope you’re right.
- **Let Sable decide.**
    - You put down one pile of fruit. You step back.
    - Sable pushes half the pile to Wisp. She shares!
    - **Wisp**: …she gave. hers. she gave.
    - **Luna**: That’s a kinder answer than mine.
- _(left out in this band; plays as `spared`)_

#### `s2-complete`

_Morning. Two little circles of frost by the long grass._  
_Two grey Nubbins slept there. Wisp has the egg stone._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.2, do): circle shape  
> _(Draw a circle in the air with your finger.)_  
> **Moss**: Round and round and back again. A circle!  
> Check: `edu:draw:shape:circle`
> _For grown-ups: Drew a circle in the air and named the shape._

**Moss**: He’ll never give that stone back.  
**Moss**: Feed them before you feed yourself. Hazel’s first rule.  
**Moss**: She never said “only the good ones”. I checked.  
**Moss**: Grey ones remember who fed them.  

## S3. Sun and Moon

- `s3-step1`: Ask a Sun friend and a Moon friend about the big race. · _Sun: Fern, Marina, Sparks, Clover, Pixel. Moon: Luna, Ember, Byte, Dusty, Nova. Ask one from each side._
- `s3-step2`: Help Sparks and Byte make the old race pictures. · _Go to Sparks’s workshop. Rock the tray when Byte counts._
- `s3-step3`: Show the pictures to Fern and to Ember. · _Fern is in her orchard. Ember is by the race fence in the evening._
- `s3-step4`: Call everyone to a big meeting at the pond. · _Later on, tap the pond and choose Call a Moot. All ten friends will come._

#### `s3-stands`

_The race stands. Sun friends sit on the left._  
_Moon friends sit on the right. An empty bench sits in the middle._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): left, right and middle  
> _(Point left. Point right. Now point to the middle.)_  
> **Fern**: So where is the empty bench?  
> - ✅ In the middle → Yes! Sun on the left, Moon on the right, the gap in the middle.
> - On the left → The left is full of Sun friends. The empty bench is in the middle.
> - On the right → The right is full of Moon friends. The empty bench is in the middle.
> _For grown-ups: Used left, right and middle to find a place._

**Fern**: Don’t sit there! That’s the gap.  
**Fern**: Hazel sat in the gap. Just to be cheeky.  
_Two big tea pots. One has a sun. One has a moon._  
**Sparks**: Long ago, at the Midsummer race, Bramble fell!  
**Sparks**: Sun says one thing. Moon says another. Now nobody races together!  
**Sparks**: Oops. I didn’t say that. That was the wind.  
_High up, Corvin watches the people, not the race._  

**Choices**

- **Sit in the gap, like Hazel.**
    - You sit in the middle. Everybody turns to look!
    - **Fern**: Oh, you’re Hazel’s, all right.
- **Sit with the Sun side.**
    - **Sparks**: Yes! Sun gets the new Keeper!
- **Sit with the Moon side.**
    - **Byte**: Welcome. Our biscuits are better. I checked.
    - **Fern**: Oh no! Lovely to see you anyway.

#### `s3-two-stories`

_You hear two stories. They are not the same._  
_Sun says: Ash bumped Bramble at the cliff bend._  
_Moon says: the flag was in the wrong place._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): listening to both sides  
> **Byte**: Two sides tell it two ways. What should we do?  
> - ✅ Listen to both sides → Yes. Hear everyone first. Then look for clues.
> - Only listen to one side → Then we’d miss half the story. Let’s listen to both sides.
> _For grown-ups: Learned to listen to both sides before deciding what happened._

**Byte**: I made a chart. Sun is yellow. Moon is blue.  
**Ember**: After that day, nobody would race with Ash.  
**Ember**: Only Corvin. He said he’d make her a champion.  
_Nobody talks about Bramble. Where did she go?_  

#### `s3-plates`

_Sparks is at your gate. She is fizzing!_  
**Sparks**: I have pictures from that race. I never looked at them!  
**Sparks**: I was scared of what they might show.  
**Sparks**: But my timer is broken. I need help.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): asking for help kindly  
> **Sparks**: How do I ask Byte for help?  
> - ✅ “Byte, please will you help me?” → Please. Right. I can say please. Probably.
> - “Give me your timer!” → He’d say no! Asking kindly works better. “Please will you help me?”
> _For grown-ups: Practised asking for help kindly, with please._

_She points up the lane, at Byte’s house._  
**Sparks**: Him!  

**Choices**

- **Fetch Byte. Say it’s for science.**
    - **Byte**: Science? I’ll bring three timers!
- **Fetch Byte. Say Sparks asked nicely.**
    - **Byte**: She did not.
    - You say, “No, she didn’t.”
    - **Byte**: I’ll come anyway.

#### `s3-flag`

_Sparks’s darkroom is red and dark. It smells of vinegar._  
**Byte**: Rock the tray on the count. One, and two…  

> **Lesson** (music, do): keeping a steady beat  
> _(Clap with Byte: one, and two, and one, and two.)_  
> **Byte**: Steady. Not fast, not slow. Like a clock.  
> Check: `edu:clap:beat:steady`
> _For grown-ups: Clapped a steady beat with Byte’s count._

**Sparks**: I rock on FEELING, Byte!  
_The first picture appears. It’s… a giant thumb._  
**Sparks**: It’s a GOOD thumb!  
_The next picture: the cliff bend, before the race._  
_A man in a grey coat is moving the flag!_  
**Byte**: He moved it two posts nearer the edge.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): near and far  
> **Byte**: Where did the flag go? Nearer the edge, or further away?  
> - ✅ Nearer the edge → Yes. Nearer the edge. That’s why the Nubbins went wide.
> - Further away → Look again. Old hole here. New hole there, nearer the edge.
> _For grown-ups: Compared positions using nearer and further._

**Sparks**: Nobody from Sun or Moon moved it. It was a race helper!  
**Sparks**: We fought about the wrong thing. For years!  

#### `s3-courier`

_Sparks wraps the pictures in a sun tea towel._  
_Byte wraps a moon tea towel round that._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds s and m  
> _(Say them slowly: Sun… Moon.)_  
> **Byte**: Which one starts with a “mmm” sound?  
> - ✅ Moon → Yes! Mmm-Moon. And Sun starts with “sss”.
> - Sun → Sun starts with “sss”, like a snake. Mmm-Moon starts with “mmm”.
> _For grown-ups: Heard the first sounds in Sun and Moon._

**Sparks**: You show them. Fern and Ember both like you.  
**Byte**: A helper from neither side. Good.  

**Choices**

- **Show them both at the same time.**
    - It takes a whole week to get them together!
    - **Fern**: Sneaky! Hazel did that to me once.
- **Show them one at a time.**
    - Fern first. Then Ember.
    - Each one asks: has the other one seen it?

#### `s3-truce`

_Fern looks at the pictures. Ember looks too._  
**Ember**: It wasn’t Ash. It wasn’t Fern. It was the flag.  
**Fern**: I was cross with you for years. Over a flag.  
**Fern**: I’m sorry, Ember.  
**Ember**: I’m sorry too.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): saying sorry  
> _Fern was cross with Ember for a long time._  
> _What can we say when we’ve been unkind?_  
> - ✅ “I’m sorry.” → Yes. Saying sorry is brave. It helps friends start again.
> - “It’s your fault.” → That keeps the cross feeling going. “I’m sorry” helps friends start again.
> _For grown-ups: Practised saying sorry, and saw how it helps friends make up._

**Ember**: Let’s have a big meeting. Everyone. Sun and Moon.  
**Fern**: And just one tea pot.  
**Ember**: One tea pot.  

#### `s3-invitations`

_Pixel makes the invitations. Gold ink or silver?_  
**Pixel**: Gold is for Sun. Silver is for Moon.  
**Clover**: What about green? Nobody is green.  
**Pixel**: Clover, that’s brilliant! Green!  

> **Lesson** (art, do): blue and yellow paint make green  
> **Pixel**: Mix blue paint and yellow paint, and you get green!  
> _(Find something green near you. Point to it!)_  
> Check: `edu:find:colour:green`
> _For grown-ups: Learned that blue and yellow paint mix to make green, and found something green._

_Dusty digs a hole for the one tea pot. Nobody asked._  
**Nova**: Am I Sun or Moon? I’ve decided I’m weather.  
**Luna**: Ember says she’s coming. That’s Ember for yes.  

#### `s3-moot`

_Evening at the pond. Ten friends. One tea pot._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 10; 5 and 5 make 10  
> _(Count the friends: five Sun, five Moon.)_  
> _(Five and five make ten!)_  
> Check: `edu:count:keepers:10`
> _For grown-ups: Counted ten friends as two groups of five._

_Dusty dug a hole. The pot sits in it. Dusty is proud._  
**Gran Hazel**: A Moot! I haven’t been to one in years!  
**Sparks**: It was the flag. I had the pictures all along.  
**Fern**: Bramble fell because a man moved a flag. Not because of us.  
**Byte**: I tasted both teas. Same tea. It always was.  
**Ember**: So. A race for everyone. And I check the flags.  
**Fern**: We check the flags.  
**Byte**: I’ll check the checking.  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.Civ.8.K-2, notice): a fair rule is the same for everyone  
> _A rule is fair when it’s the same for everyone._  
> _Everybody checks the flags. That keeps the race fair._  
> _For grown-ups: Talked about fair rules that are the same for everyone._

**Gran Hazel**: Now, who bangs the spoon? Someone must keep the Moot.  

**Choices**

- **Bang the spoon on the pot.**
    - Bong! Everybody stops talking.
    - Now one person talks at a time. Taking turns!
    - **Pixel**: Ribbons for every racer! Gold, silver and green!
    - **Gran Hazel**: Moot-Keeper. It suits you.
- **Let Fern and Ember share the spoon.**
    - Fern and Ember hold the spoon together. Bong! They both miss!
    - Everybody cheers.
    - **Pixel**: Ribbons for every racer! Gold, silver and green!
    - **Gran Hazel**: You’re still the Moot-Keeper, love.

## S4. The Waterfall

- `s4-step1`: Look at Gran’s white line by the waterfall. · _Go past the pond to the falls. Tap the white mark on the rock._
- `s4-step2`: Send a swimmer up the stream to look. · _Choose Swim at the top of the pool. Marina can teach a Nubbin first._
- `s4-step3`: Walk the path behind the waterfall with Marina. · _Meet Marina in the evening. Stay close behind her._
- `s4-step4`: Open the water gate at night. · _Go with Marina and a strong Nubbin. The gate sticks. Lift and push!_

#### `s4-pools`

**Marina**: Come and see something.  
_Marina’s rock pools step down like bowls._  
_Half of them are dry. No water at all._  
**Marina**: The stream fills my pools. This summer it gives less.  
**Marina**: And the water is cold. Cold, in July!  
**Marina**: Hazel painted a line by the waterfall. Go and look.  
**You**: Why don’t you look?  
**Marina**: I’ve looked lots. I want you to tell me I’m wrong.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): naming a feeling: worried  
> _Marina keeps looking and looking. How does she feel?_  
> - ✅ Worried → Yes. I’m worried. A friend checking helps.
> - Silly → Not silly. Worried. When we worry, a friend can help.
> _For grown-ups: Named the feeling worried, and heard that a friend’s help eases worry._

**Marina**: Mind the moss on the rocks. Not that Moss! The slippy kind.  

#### `s4-line`

_The falls are loud and white. Splash, splash!_  
_On the dark rock is a thick white line._  
_The water is a hand below the line._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): higher and lower; measuring with a hand  
> **Marina**: Is the water higher or lower than the line?  
> - ✅ Lower → Yes. Lower, by a whole hand. (Show a hand’s width.)
> - Higher → Look again. The line is up here. The water is down there. Lower!
> _For grown-ups: Compared heights (higher, lower) and measured a gap with a hand._

_Your Nubbin dips a paw in. Brr! It looks at you._  
**Marina**: A whole hand too low.  
**Marina**: Something up the stream is taking the water.  

#### `s4-lesson`

**Marina**: Hazel’s Nubbins learned to swim with me.  
**Marina**: Lesson one: Nubbins float better than you’d think.  
_Marina holds your Nubbin under its chin. Like a cup._  
_It kicks. It doesn’t sink!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, do): float or sink  
> _(Guess: does it float or sink? A leaf, a stone, an apple.)_  
> **Marina**: Leaf floats. Apple floats. Stone sinks. Plop!  
> Check: `edu:sort:float-sink:leaf-stone-apple`
> _For grown-ups: Guessed and sorted things that float and sink._

**Marina**: Now swim up the stream. Just a little way.  

**Choices**

- **Wade in after them.**
    - You wade in. Brrr! It’s very cold!
    - **Marina**: Lesson three: Keepers stay on the bank. Out you get!
- **Stay on the bank. Trust them.**
    - **Marina**: Good. Staying on the bank is the hardest part.

#### `s4-dam`

_Your swimmer comes back. Shake, shake! You get wet._  
_In its fur: a grey thread and a bit of new cement._  
_Up the stream is a new stone wall. It holds the water back._  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS2-2, notice): a dam holds water back; beavers build dams  
> **Marina**: A wall that holds water back is called a dam.  
> **Marina**: Beavers build dams too! With sticks and mud.  
> _For grown-ups: Learned what a dam is, and that beavers build dams that change their surroundings._

_Behind it is flat, still, blue water._  
_There are swimming lanes, and a sign: NO SPLASHING._  
**Marina**: No splashing? Splashing is what water is for!  
**Marina**: My pools need that water.  
**Marina**: I wrote three letters. Nobody helped.  

#### `s4-ledge-ask`

**Marina**: Before we do anything, I’ll show you something.  
**Marina**: Hazel knew this path. Now you should too.  
_By the falls, a thin ledge goes behind the water._  
**Marina**: Back to the rock. Eyes on my heels. Stay close.  

> **Lesson** (health, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, ask): staying close to a grown-up near water  
> **Marina**: Near water, where do we stay?  
> - ✅ Close to a grown-up → Yes. Close to me, all the way.
> - Run on ahead → Not near water. Wet rocks are slippy. We stay close to a grown-up.
> _For grown-ups: Talked about staying close to a grown-up near water._


#### `s4-ledge`

_Behind the falls, it’s all noise and spray. Whoosh!_  
_The rock is wet and soft with moss._  
_A warm breath of air comes from the dark._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, do): using our senses  
> **Marina**: Use your senses. Ears hear the roar. Skin feels the spray.  
> _(Point to your ears. Now your nose. What does a nose do?)_  
> Check: `edu:point:senses:ears-skin-nose`
> _For grown-ups: Named senses (hearing, touch, smell) and the body parts that use them._

**Marina**: That’s the warm spring. Where the first Nubbins came from.  
**Marina**: We stop here. Further is for when it’s needed.  
**Marina**: Now you know the way. That’s what a path is.  

#### `s4-sluice`

**Marina**: Three letters. Nobody listened. Now we do something.  
**Marina**: We won’t break anything. We’ll open a gate.  
_Night. The dam is a dark wall. The gate is wood._  
_In a hut, a guard sleeps in his chair._  
_He snores. In… out… whistle! In… out… whistle!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, ELOF: Mathematics Development, do): copying and continuing a pattern  
> _(Do the snore pattern together: in, out, whistle!)_  
> **Marina**: In, out, whistle. What comes next? In!  
> Check: `edu:copy:pattern:snore`
> _For grown-ups: Copied a repeating sound pattern and said what comes next._

**Marina**: Move on the snore. Freeze on the whistle.  
_You creep to the gate. You push. It’s stuck!_  

**Choices**

- **Lift, then push.**
    - Lift and push, like your gate at home. Creak! It opens!
    - **Marina**: Where did you learn that?
    - **You**: Home.
- **Let your strongest Nubbin try.**
    - Your Nubbin pushes. Nothing. Then it slips, and lifts!
    - The gate swings open.
    - **Marina**: Even the Nubbins know Hazel’s trick!
- **Pin a polite note on the gate first.**
    - You write: “Opened for the rock pools. Thank you!”
    - Then you lift and push. It opens.
    - **Marina**: Polite! Lovely.

#### `s4-water`

_Whoosh! Water rushes through the gate._  
**Marina**: Listen.  
_The waterfall sounds bigger now. Deep and full._  

> **Lesson** (music, ask): louder and quieter sounds  
> **Marina**: Is the waterfall louder now, or quieter?  
> - ✅ Louder → Yes! More water, more noise. Roar!
> - Quieter → Listen again. More water is falling. It’s louder!
> _For grown-ups: Listened and compared louder and quieter sounds._

_The guard mumbles, “Received.” Then he snores again._  
**Marina**: Someone may shut it again. Then someone opens it again.  
**Marina**: That’s most of looking after things.  

#### `s4-complete`

_A week later, the rock pools are full!_  
_The anemones open up, like little flowers._  
**Marina**: Look at them. Showing off!  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS3-1, notice): anemones are animals that live in water  
> **Marina**: Anemones look like flowers. But they’re animals!  
> **Marina**: They need water. When the water goes, they close up tight.  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that sea anemones are animals that need water to live._

_In the deepest pool, something round and white shines._  
_Your swimmer dives! Kick, kick, bubbles!_  
_It comes up with the thing on its head. Achoo!_  
_It’s an egg! A pearl-white egg!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): animals that hatch from eggs  
> **Marina**: Lots of animals hatch from eggs. Which one does?  
> - ✅ A duck → Yes! Ducklings hatch from eggs. So do Nubbins!
> - A kitten → Kittens grow inside their mum, not in an egg. Ducks hatch from eggs!
> _For grown-ups: Learned that some animals, like ducks, hatch from eggs._

**Marina**: Oh, you beauty! It’s yours. You opened the gate.  
**Marina**: Take some shells for the Grove, too.  

## S5. Fossil’s Walk

- `s5-step1`: Carry Fossil to the cliff where Dusty found her egg. · _The cliff is past the stream. Walk, don’t run. She likes to look at things._
- `s5-step2`: Take Fossil to Gran’s first tree. Shake it once. · _It’s the low, crooked tree over the stream. Just one shake!_
- `s5-step3`: Help Fossil float in the pond. · _Go to the edge of the pond and choose Swim. A swimmer or Marina can hold her._
- `s5-step4`: Sit with Fossil and your littlest Nubbin until dark. · _Stay still near them until night comes._

#### `s5-list`

_Dusty is at the gate with a very old Nubbin._  
_She is the colour of sand. She blinks very slowly._  
**Dusty**: This is Fossil. I dug her egg out of a cliff!  
**Dusty**: Fossil is very old now. She is fading.  
**Dusty**: Fading means her life is nearly over.  
**Dusty**: She has three wishes. But my back hurts.  
**Dusty**: Will you carry her for me? Please?  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, ask): being gentle with someone old and frail  
> **Dusty**: How do we carry a very old Nubbin?  
> - ✅ Gently, with both hands → That’s it. Slow and gentle.
> - Fast, swinging her about → Oh, that would hurt her. Gently, with both hands.
> _For grown-ups: Thought about being gentle and careful with someone old._

**Dusty**: One, the cliff. Two, Hazel’s first tree. Three, the water.  
_Fossil holds up her arms. Up, please!_  

**Choices**

- **Lift her with both hands, like an egg.**
    - You lift her gently. She snuggles in.
    - **Dusty**: That’s it. Just right.
- **Let her ride on your shoulder.**
    - She climbs up slowly. She sits like a queen.
    - **Dusty**: Oh, she likes you!

#### `s5-cliff`

_The cliff has stripes. Brown, red, grey, brown._  

> **Lesson** (maths, ELOF: Mathematics Development, ask): colour patterns: what comes next  
> _(Say the stripes together: brown, red, grey, brown.)_  
> **Dusty**: Which colour comes after red?  
> - ✅ Grey → Yes! Brown, red, grey, brown.
> - Brown → Brown comes first and last. After red comes grey.
> _For grown-ups: Read a colour sequence and said which colour came next._

**Dusty**: Here’s where I found her egg. I was twelve.  
_Fossil taps the rock. Tap, tap!_  
**Dusty**: Fine. I was eleven.  
_Tap!_  
**Dusty**: All right! I dug with a spoon, not a trowel!  
_Fossil rests her head on the rock. Everyone is quiet._  
_A baby fern grows in a crack. It is curled up tight._  
_Next to it, pressed in the stone, is a very old fern._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): a fossil is a very old plant or animal kept in stone  
> **Dusty**: That old fern is a fossil. A plant from long, long ago.  
> **Dusty**: Its shape got kept in the stone. That’s where Fossil got her name!  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that a fossil is the shape of a very old living thing kept in rock._


#### `s5-tree-ask`

**Dusty**: Next, Hazel’s first tree. The crooked one.  

> **Lesson** (art, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): straight and crooked lines  
> _(Make your arm straight. Now make it crooked, like the tree!)_  
> **Dusty**: Bendy and bumpy. That’s crooked.  
> Check: `edu:make:shape:straight-crooked`
> _For grown-ups: Made straight and crooked shapes with their body._

**Dusty**: Shake it once. Only once!  
_Fossil falls asleep on the way. Zzz._  
_She wakes up just as you get there!_  

#### `s5-tree`

_All your Nubbins follow. They sit in a row._  
_They all open their mouths. Ahhh!_  
**Dusty**: Once! Not twice.  
_You shake the tree, once. One fruit falls._  
_It misses every mouth. Plop! It lands in Fossil’s lap._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.C.6, ask): more and fewer  
> **Dusty**: Lots of mouths. One fruit. Is there one for everyone?  
> - ✅ No, not enough → Right. More mouths than fruit. Fewer fruit than mouths!
> - Yes, one each → Count them: lots of mouths, only one fruit. Not enough to go round!
> _For grown-ups: Compared groups to see that there were fewer fruits than mouths._

**Dusty**: Hazel gave her one of these when she hatched.  
_Fossil eats a little. She gives the rest to Dusty._  
**Dusty**: Tastes like being eleven.  

#### `s5-water-ask`

**Dusty**: Last wish: the water. She can’t swim alone now.  
**Dusty**: Marina taught her. She swam sideways!  
_Marina is at the pond, with a towel. What a surprise!_  
**Marina**: I was just passing. With a towel.  

> **Lesson** (health, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, notice): always swim with a helper nearby  
> **Marina**: Swimmers always have a helper close by. Even good swimmers.  
> _(Ask: who helps you near water? Wait for an answer.)_  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that swimmers always have a helper close by, and named who helps them._


**Choices**

- **Let one of your swimmers hold her.**
    - Your swimmer wades out. It looks very serious.
    - **Marina**: Paw under her chin. Like a cup.
- **Ask Marina to hold her.**
    - Marina wades in and holds out her hands.
    - **Marina**: Come on, old girl. Same as always.

#### `s5-water`

_Fossil goes into the water. She floats!_  
_Then, very slowly, she kicks._  
**Dusty**: Look! Her sideways swim!  
**Dusty**: To the reeds and back, Fos.  
_Slowly, slowly, she swims. Nobody hurries her._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, ask): opposites: slow and fast  
> **Dusty**: Fossil swims slowly. What’s the opposite of slow?  
> - ✅ Fast → Yes! Slow and fast are opposites.
> - Wet → She is wet! But the opposite of slow is fast.
> _For grown-ups: Named a pair of opposites: slow and fast._

_She comes out shivering, with bright eyes._  
_She looks at the Grove. She has a wish of her own._  

#### `s5-secret`

_Fossil walks across the Grove. Step… by… step._  
_She sits down by your littlest Nubbin._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): comparing: older and younger  
> _Who is older: Fossil, or your littlest Nubbin?_  
> - ✅ Fossil → Yes. Fossil is the oldest. Your littlest is the youngest.
> - My littlest Nubbin → Your littlest is brand new! Fossil is much, much older.
> _For grown-ups: Compared two characters by age using older and younger._

_Your littlest was falling over a fruit. Now it stops._  
_They look at each other. Then they just sit._  
**Dusty**: She did that for me once, on a bad day.  
**Dusty**: She didn’t fix anything. She just sat with me.  

#### `s5-gift`

_The Sun goes down behind the hill._  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-ESS1-1, notice): sunset, then night  
> _The Sun goes down in the evening. That’s called sunset._  
> _Then the sky gets dark. Night time!_  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that the Sun sets in the evening and then night comes._

_Nobody moves. Not Fossil. Not your littlest. Not you._  
_When it’s dark, Fossil touches noses with your littlest._  
_Your littlest sits up tall and proud. A gift from Fossil!_  

#### `s5-home`

_Dusty picks Fossil up to carry her home._  
_The little ball above her head is very pale now._  
**Dusty**: Fossil won’t be with us much longer. I’m glad we did her wishes.  
**Dusty**: Thank you for walking with us.  
_Fossil gives you a baby fern, curled up tight._  
**Dusty**: She wants you to plant it. A fossil-fern!  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, ask): what a plant needs to grow  
> **Dusty**: What does a little fern need to grow?  
> - ✅ Soil, water and light → Yes. Soil, water and light. And time.
> - A box in the dark → In a dark box it won’t grow. It needs soil, water and light.
> _For grown-ups: Learned that plants need soil, water and light to grow._

**Dusty**: And take my old trowel. For digging.  
_They go out of the gate. Dusty lifts and pushes._  
_On the hill, Moss watches them go._  

## S6. Ash’s Name

- `s6-s1`: Ember asked you to come. Go and see Ash. · _Ember’s house is on the Moon side. Knock on the door._
- `s6-s2`: Find what Gran wrote about Ash’s egg. · _In the cottage, tap the bookshelf by the stove. Look on the bottom shelf._
- `s6-s3`: Take the book to Ember. Practise the three notes by the pond. · _Pick the notes Gran wrote down. Try as many times as you like._
- `s6-s4`: Be in Ember’s kitchen when she whistles to Ash. · _Go to Ember’s house after dark. Stand quietly by the door._

#### `s6-kitchen`

_A note under your door: COME OVER. PLEASE._  
_Ember’s house is very, very tidy._  
_A grey Nubbin sits in the corner. She faces the wall._  
**Ember**: Ash. Ash, love. It’s me.  
_Ash does not turn round._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): understanding how a frightened friend feels  
> _Ash faces the wall. How might she feel?_  
> - ✅ Scared and lost → Yes. Scared and lost. She needs time.
> - Happy and silly → Happy Nubbins play and turn round. Ash feels scared and lost.
> _For grown-ups: Read clues to work out that a character felt scared and lost._

**Ember**: I’ve said her name so many times.  
**Ember**: She was away so long. She forgot her name.  

**Choices**

- **Sit on the floor near Ash, and wait.**
    - You sit for a long time. Ash relaxes, just a little.
    - **Ember**: Gran used to sit on the floor too. Like it was a chair!
- **Say “Ash” yourself, gently.**
    - Ash doesn’t turn.
    - **Ember**: See? It isn’t just me.
- **Ask what Corvin called her.**
    - **Ember**: Nothing. He never said her name.
    - **Ember**: If nobody says your name, you might forget it too.

#### `s6-egg`

_Ember puts the kettle on._  
**Ember**: When she was tiny, she answered to everything.  
**Ember**: Her name. My name. Even the kettle!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds in names  
> _(Say the names slowly: Ash, Ember, Fern.)_  
> **Ember**: Which name starts with an “eh” sound?  
> - ✅ Ember → Yes. Eh-Ember. That’s me.
> - Ash → Ash starts with “a”, like apple. Eh-Ember starts with “eh”.
> - Fern → Fern starts with “fff”. Eh-Ember starts with “eh”.
> _For grown-ups: Listened for the first sound in three names._

**Ember**: Go home now. Thanks for coming.  

#### `s6-fern`

_Fern leans on the fence, eating an apple. Your apple!_  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): asking before taking  
> _Fern took your apple without asking. What should she do next time?_  
> - ✅ Ask, “May I have one?” → Oops. You’re right. May I have an apple, please?
> - Just take it → Hmm. That’s what I did. Asking first is kinder. May I?
> _For grown-ups: Talked about asking before taking something that belongs to someone else._

**Fern**: You’ve been at Ember’s. You have that look.  
**You**: Ash won’t answer to her name.  
**Fern**: Oh, poor Ash. Poor Ember.  
**You**: Ash came from one of Gran’s eggs.  
**Fern**: Then she’s in Gran’s book! Gran wrote every egg down.  
**Fern**: And the first sound each baby made.  
**Fern**: Bottom shelf, behind the jam. Don’t eat the jam!  

#### `s6-register`

_Behind the jam: a fat old book._  
_On the front, Gran wrote: “EGGS.”_  
_And smaller: “Do not lend this to Byte.”_  
_Inside, a page says: “Given away.”_  
**Gran Hazel**: “Egg 7. Given to Sparks. First sound: a bang!”  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.4, do): show 7 on fingers  
> _(Egg 7! Hold up seven fingers together.)_  
> _(Five on one hand, and two more. Seven!)_  
> Check: `edu:show:fingers:7`
> _For grown-ups: Showed seven on their fingers as five and two more._

**Gran Hazel**: “Egg 23. Given to Byte. First sound: a hiccup.”  
**Gran Hazel**: “Egg 41. Given to Ember, aged fifteen. She promised to feed it first.”  
**Gran Hazel**: “First sound: three notes. Low, high, and a little one.”  
_Gran drew three dots. Low, high, and a tiny one going up._  

#### `s6-practise`

_Ember reads the page. Twice._  
**Ember**: What if I call her and she doesn’t come?  
_She comes to the pond anyway, frowning._  
**Ember**: I can’t sing. But I can whistle.  
**Ember**: You go first. Low, high, little one. Like a question.  

> **Lesson** (music, do): low and high notes  
> _(Sing a low note. Now a high note. Then a little one going up!)_  
> **Ember**: Low… high… little one? Like a question!  
> Check: `edu:sing:notes:low-high-up`
> _For grown-ups: Sang low and high notes and a little rising note, like a question._

_Your Nubbins sit on the bank to watch._  
**Ember**: If a duck answers, we’re stopping.  

**Choices**

- **Whistle: high, low, a little one.**
    - One of your Nubbins sneezes. Ember laughs.
    - **Ember**: Wrong way round! High comes second. Again.
- **Whistle: low, a little one, high.**
    - The last note goes up and up, like a kettle!
    - **Ember**: Nearly! The little one comes last. Again.
- **Whistle: low, high, and a little one.**
    - Low, high, and a little one, like a question.
    - Every Nubbin on the bank turns its head!
    - **Ember**: …Oh.
    - **Ember**: Again. Slower.

#### `s6-ember-tries`

_Ember whistles the three notes. Quietly._  
_From across the pond, a duck answers. QUACK!_  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-LS3-1, ask): animal babies: a baby duck is a duckling  
> _Ducks say quack. What is a baby duck called?_  
> - ✅ A duckling → Yes! A duckling. It looks like a small, fluffy duck.
> - A kitten → A kitten is a baby cat. A baby duck is a duckling.
> - A puppy → A puppy is a baby dog. A baby duck is a duckling.
> _For grown-ups: Named a duck’s baby (duckling) and other animal babies._

**Ember**: No.  
_Ember glares at the duck. The duck does not care._  
_But Ember heard herself do it._  
**Ember**: Tonight. You’re coming. Don’t say anything.  

#### `s6-whistle`

_Ember’s kitchen at night. One lamp is lit._  
_Ash faces the wall. Ember kneels a little way off._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, notice): giving a scared friend space  
> _Ember doesn’t go too close. She gives Ash space._  
> _Space helps a scared friend feel safe._  
> _For grown-ups: Saw how giving space helps a scared friend feel safe._

_Ember whistles. Low, high, and a little one._  
_Ash’s ear moves. Just a little._  
_Ember whistles again. Slower. Her hands shake._  
_Ash turns round._  
**Ash**: …pip. wirr. a?  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): sound words  
> _(Make Ash’s sound together: pip, wirr, a?)_  
> _Pip, wirr, a. That’s Ash’s very own sound._  
> Check: `edu:say:sound:ash`
> _For grown-ups: Copied a character’s sound word aloud._

_Step by step, Ash comes. She leans on Ember’s knee._  
**Ember**: Hello, you. It’s me. It was always me.  

**Choices**

- **Stay by the door, very quietly.**
    - Ember cries quietly. Happy tears.
    - Ash lets her.
- **Tiptoe out. This is their time.**
    - You lift the latch slowly. No click.
    - Behind you, Ember whistles. Ash answers every time.

#### `s6-complete`

_Next morning, Ember is at your gate. Ash walks close._  
_Ash is still grey._  
**Ember**: Clover says the grey stays. You can only make it lighter.  
**Ember**: She ate breakfast with me in the room. That’s lighter.  
**Ember**: Here. Ash’s first ribbon. Give it to one of yours.  
**Ember**: Now. Two fingers, like that. Blow!  
_Try one: pfff. Try two: pfff. Try three. Try four._  
_Try five: wheee! Three Nubbins come running!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5; keep trying  
> _(Count the tries on your fingers: one, two, three, four, five!)_  
> **Ember**: Five tries. You kept going. That’s how you learn.  
> Check: `edu:count:tries:5`
> _For grown-ups: Counted five tries and heard that practice is how we learn._

**Ember**: Don’t tell anyone I like you.  

## S7. Null and the Clock

- `s7-s1`: Find Byte after Moss’s talk. · _Byte is on the low wall by the long grass, writing._
- `s7-s2`: See the clock Pixel built for Null. · _Pixel’s bench is behind the lantern shed. Follow the tick-tock._
- `s7-s3`: After a week, go and see Null. · _Visit Byte’s house when the journal says a week has gone by._
- `s7-s4`: Spend an evening with Null. No clock. Wait for Null to come to you. · _Sit still near Null. Only pet Null when it comes close by itself._

#### `s7-unverified`

_After Moss’s talk, Byte sits on the wall, writing._  
_Beside him is a pale blue Nubbin. That’s Null._  
**Byte**: Null is four years old. And some weeks.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.4, do): show 4 on fingers; how old are you  
> _(Null is four. Hold up four fingers!)_  
> _(How old are you? Show me on your fingers.)_  
> Check: `edu:show:fingers:4`
> _For grown-ups: Showed four on their fingers and then their own age._

**Byte**: Moss says a Nubbin loved enough becomes an Eternal.  
**Byte**: “Enough”? How much is enough? I’ll use a clock!  
**Moss**: Your shoelace is untied.  
_Byte looks down. It is. It always is!_  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): checking our shoes are done up  
> _(Look at your shoes. Laces, buckles or sticky straps?)_  
> **Moss**: Done-up shoes stop you tripping. Always worth a look.  
> Check: `edu:check:shoes:fastened`
> _For grown-ups: Checked their own shoes and talked about laces, buckles and straps._

**Moss**: Things on a clock learn to wait for the bell, Byte.  
**Moss**: Safe is not the same as well.  
_Byte writes it down. Then he writes: NOT CHECKED._  

**Choices**

- **I’ll help. What do you need?**
    - **Byte**: Fruit, a bed, and a helper who watches.
- **Moss is usually right, Byte.**
    - **Byte**: “Usually” isn’t always!
    - Moss pretends to sleep. He is bad at it.
- **Pet Null.**
    - Null leans into your hand. Then leans more!
    - **Byte**: Fine. Once.

#### `s7-minder`

_Pixel’s bench: a clock, three little bells, and a tiny bed._  
**Pixel**: Bell one: fruit drops. Bell two: the bed warms.  
**Pixel**: Bell three: petting! Byte does the petting.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.K.3, ask): first, next, last  
> **Pixel**: Bell one, bell two, bell three. What happens first?  
> - ✅ Fruit drops → Yes! First fruit. Next the bed. Last, petting!
> - The bed warms → That’s next. First the fruit drops. Then the bed. Then petting.
> - Petting → That’s last! First fruit, next bed, last petting.
> _For grown-ups: Put three steps in order using first, next and last._

**Byte**: Exactly ninety seconds. I tested it.  
**Pixel**: We call it the Minder.  
_Tick. A plum shoots out. Bonk! It hits Byte._  
**Pixel**: That was a test.  
**Byte**: That was a plum.  
**Pixel**: A clock doesn’t care. I’m trying to care for it.  

**Choices**

- **What if Null is hungry before the bell?**
    - **Byte**: Null won’t be. The bell is set.
    - **Pixel**: I asked him that too.
- **Does it have an off switch?**
    - **Pixel**: Yes! A lever. I always add a lever.
    - **Pixel**: Thank you for asking!

#### `s7-first-bell`

_Byte puts Null in the little bed. Ding! Bell one._  
_Null looks at Byte. Then at the bell. Then eats._  
**Byte**: Day one. Come back in a week.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.A.1, do): a week is seven days; count to 7  
> **Byte**: A week is seven days.  
> _(Count to seven together. One whole week!)_  
> Check: `edu:count:days:7`
> _For grown-ups: Learned that a week has seven days and counted to seven._

**Pixel**: It’s just a clock. Clocks are fine.  

#### `s7-week`

_A week later. Null sits up in its bed, waiting._  
**Byte**: Best week ever! Null eats so neatly.  
**Pixel**: Watch this.  
_Pixel rolls a ball. Null doesn’t chase it._  
_Ding! Null eats. It only looks at the bell._  
_Null’s blue has gone pale at the edges._  
**Byte**: It used to chase that ball everywhere. Why won’t it play?  
**Pixel**: I think it’s waiting to be told.  

> **Lesson** (health, ask): we need food, rest, play and love  
> **Pixel**: What does a Nubbin need to be well?  
> - ✅ Food, rest, play and love → Yes. All of them. Not just the ones on a clock.
> - Just food and a bed → Food and rest keep it alive. Play and love make it well. It needs them all.
> _For grown-ups: Talked about needing play and love as well as food and rest._

**Byte**: Moss told me. And I didn’t listen.  

**Choices**

- **Pull the lever.**
    - Pixel pulls it at once. The ticking stops.
    - It is very, very quiet.
    - **Pixel**: I always add a lever.
- **Sit down by Null, Byte. And wait.**
    - **Byte**: For how long?
    - **You**: Until Null stops waiting for the bell.
    - Byte pulls the lever himself.

#### `s7-same-graph`

**Byte**: I wanted to keep Null safe. I kept it too still.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): saying “I was wrong”  
> **Byte**: I was wrong.  
> _Saying “I was wrong” is hard. It’s also brave._  
> _For grown-ups: Heard a character admit a mistake, and that this is brave._

_Null waits for a bell that doesn’t come._  
_It looks a little lost._  
**Pixel**: Tomorrow, no clock. We’ll just sit. We’re good at sitting.  

#### `s7-no-clock`

_Evening in Byte’s garden. No bells._  
_Byte holds his stopwatch. Just in case._  
_Null sits in the grass. For a long time, nothing._  
_Then a moth flutters past. Null’s head turns!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): moths fly at night  
> _Most moths fly at night. They like lights._  
> _Most butterflies fly in the day._  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that most moths fly at night and most butterflies fly by day._

**Byte**: Did you see that? That’s not on any list!  
_Null takes one step after the moth. Then it looks at you._  

**Choices**

- **Nod. “Go on.”**
    - Null runs. It falls into the hedge!
    - It comes out with a leaf on its head.
    - **Null**: …prrp!
- **Put the stopwatch down, Byte.**
    - Byte puts it face down in the grass.
    - **Byte**: It’s still ticking. But I can’t see it. That’s a start.

#### `s7-lap`

_Near dark, Null climbs into Byte’s lap._  
_It falls asleep at once._  
_Byte sits still for an hour. He doesn’t check the time._  
**Byte**: Its edges look bluer. Don’t tell me if I’m wrong.  

> **Lesson** (art, do): naming the colour blue  
> _(Find something blue near you. Point to it!)_  
> _(Is it pale blue, like Null? Or dark blue?)_  
> Check: `edu:find:colour:blue`
> _For grown-ups: Found something blue and compared pale and dark blue._


#### `s7-complete`

_A few days later, Pixel knocks. Pixel doesn’t speak._  
_Null is under Byte’s hedge. Null is very old now._  
_Null’s life is ending. Around it, a glowing cocoon grows._  
_Byte sits with his hand on it. He doesn’t count._  
_The cocoon glows gold. It opens. An egg!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): life cycles: caterpillar, cocoon, moth  
> _In real life, a caterpillar makes a cocoon._  
> _Later, a moth comes out! Living things change as they grow._  
> _For grown-ups: Heard about a real life cycle: caterpillar, cocoon, moth._

**Byte**: Null gets to start again. And I’ll remember it.  
**Byte**: Here. My stopwatch. Please keep it.  
**Pixel**: And the clock. It only counts lanterns now.  
_Byte’s shoelace is tied. Nobody told him to. It won’t last!_  

## S8. The Ring of Roots

- `s8-s1`: Follow Nova and the fireflies past Gran’s camp. · _Walk when the fireflies float. Stop when they stop._
- `s8-s2`: Leave fruit by the ring of roots, a little at a time. · _Bring twelve fruits over three evenings. Put them down, then step back._
- `s8-s3`: Bring Fern to the ring of roots. · _Fern is at her gate next door. Walk with her to the Hollow._
- `s8-s4`: Sit by the ring with Fern. Let the old Nubbin come to her. · _Sit down by the roots and wait. Don’t reach in._

#### `s8-further`

_Nova steps out of the trees._  
**Nova**: This is Gran’s camp. But it isn’t all of it.  
**Nova**: Gran wrote, “Fed nineteen today.” But there are only twelve bowls.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.C.6, ask): more and fewer  
> **Nova**: Nineteen Nubbins. Twelve bowls. Are there more Nubbins, or more bowls?  
> - ✅ More Nubbins → Yes. Nineteen is more than twelve. Not enough bowls!
> - More bowls → Twelve is fewer than nineteen. So there were more Nubbins than bowls.
> _For grown-ups: Compared nineteen and twelve to see which is more._

**Nova**: So she carried food further in.  
_A firefly lands on the notebook. Then it flies off._  
**Nova**: The fireflies go the same way every night.  
**Nova**: Bring fruit. Walk slowly. Slower than that.  

**Choices**

- **Lead the way.**
    - **Nova**: I don’t lead. I follow fireflies. You follow me.
- **Should my Nubbin wait here?**
    - **Nova**: No. Bring a Nubbin who trusts you. Then they’ll trust you too.

#### `s8-ring`

_The fireflies stop over a ring of old roots._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): fireflies make their own light  
> **Nova**: Fireflies are little beetles. They make their own light!  
> _(Open and shut your hands, like a firefly. Blink, blink!)_  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that fireflies are beetles that make their own light._

_Inside sit lots of grey Nubbins, in a circle._  
_In the middle sits an old grey Nubbin._  
_Her ear folds over at the tip. A faded ribbon is tied on it._  
**Nova**: They aren’t lost. They know where they are.  
**Nova**: That one’s in charge. I call her the Old Girl.  

#### `s8-edge`

_You step towards the roots. Every grey head turns._  
**Nova**: Don’t go in. This is their home.  
**Nova**: We don’t walk into a home without being asked.  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.Civ.8.K-2, ask): respecting other people’s homes  
> **Nova**: You visit a friend’s home. What do you do first?  
> - ✅ Knock, and wait to be asked in → Yes. Knock and wait. That’s respect.
> - Walk straight in → That might scare them. We knock and wait to be asked in.
> _For grown-ups: Talked about knocking and waiting to be invited into someone’s home._

**You**: How did Gran feed them?  
**Nova**: She left food at the edge and stepped back. Every night.  
_By the Old Girl sits a very thin grey Nubbin._  
_Its ear folds over at the tip too. Just like hers!_  
**Nova**: Her baby, I think. It doesn’t eat much.  

**Choices**

- **Leave the softest fruit near the thin one.**
    - **Nova**: That’s just what Gran did!
- **Put the fruit down. Sit a little way off.**
    - You sit. At last, one grey Nubbin takes a fruit.
    - **Nova**: Good. Now, three nights of this.

#### `s8-gap`

_On the third night, the Old Girl stands up._  
_She steps aside. There’s a gap, just for you!_  
**Nova**: I sit by these roots whenever I pass. She never did that for me!  
_Her old ribbon is brown. A leaf is sewn on it._  
_The leaf has five points and a curly stem._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5  
> _(Hold up one hand. Five fingers, like five leaf points!)_  
> _(Count them: one, two, three, four, five.)_  
> Check: `edu:count:leaf-points:5`
> _For grown-ups: Counted five points on a leaf using the fingers of one hand._

_You’ve seen that leaf before. On Fern’s gloves! On Fern’s gate!_  

#### `s8-fern-gate`

_Fern is at her gate, sewing a leaf on a glove._  
**Fern**: I put leaves on everything. Once I put one on a ribbon.  
**Fern**: That was for Bramble, my Nubbin.  
**Fern**: At a race, long ago, she fell at the cliff bend.  
**Fern**: I looked till dark. I never found her.  
**Fern**: I still miss her.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): missing someone, and talking about it  
> _Fern misses Bramble. Missing someone can last a long time._  
> _Talking about it, like Fern does, can help._  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that missing someone can last a long time and that talking helps._


**Choices**

- **Fern. I think I’ve found her.**
    - **Fern**: Don’t say that unless…
    - **You**: Bring soft fruit.
    - Fern runs through her gate. She still holds her needle!
- **Will you help me carry fruit tonight?**
    - **Fern**: The Hollow? At night? …Fine.
    - You don’t tell her why. Not yet.

#### `s8-arrive`

_The fireflies float ahead of you both._  
**Fern**: It’s so dark! I can’t see my own feet!  
_Bonk. Fern walks into a tree. “Sorry, tree.”_  
_Bonk. The same tree again!_  
**Fern**: I’m carrying the soft fruit. I sat on two.  
_At the stream, Fern takes her boots off and wades across._  
_She puts them back on the wrong feet!_  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): left and right  
> _(Wiggle your left foot. Now wiggle your right foot!)_  
> **Fern**: Left boot, left foot. Right boot, right foot. Oops!  
> Check: `edu:wiggle:feet:left-right`
> _For grown-ups: Practised left and right with their own feet._

_Then the fireflies stop. Fern goes very quiet._  

#### `s8-reunion`

_Fern stops at the roots. She looks at the middle._  
_The Old Girl stands up. Her brown ribbon hangs from her ear._  
**Fern**: Bramble? Oh, you’ve gone grey. That’s all right. So have I.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sound b  
> _(Say it slowly: b-b-Bramble.)_  
> _Which word starts with “b” too?_  
> - ✅ Ball → Yes! B-b-ball, b-b-Bramble.
> - Fish → Fish starts with “fff”. Ball starts with “b”, like Bramble!
> - Sun → Sun starts with “sss”. Ball starts with “b”, like Bramble!
> _For grown-ups: Matched the first sound “b” in Bramble and ball._

_Nobody moves._  
_Then Bramble walks out. Step by step. All the way to Fern._  
**Bramble**: …  
**Fern**: I’m sorry I stopped looking. I’m so sorry.  
_Bramble sits down on Fern’s boots. Both boots! And she stays._  
**Fern**: I’m going to cry now. Happy crying. Hold my gloves!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): happy tears  
> _People can cry when they’re happy, too._  
> _Big feelings sometimes come out as tears._  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that people can cry when they are happy._


**Choices**

- **Hold her gloves.**
    - You hold them. Fern sits in the mud with Bramble in her lap.
    - She cries a long time. Bramble lets her.
- **Give Fern a ribbon from your pocket.**
    - **Fern**: You carry ribbons? Of course you do!
    - Fern sews a leaf on it. She ties it under the old one.
    - **Fern**: Now she has two!

#### `s8-one-short`

_Later, Bramble goes back to the middle of the ring._  
**Fern**: She always did like being in charge.  
_Bramble looks up the dark path._  
**Nova**: Her thin baby isn’t here tonight.  
**Fern**: Then we leave soft fruit at the edge. Till it comes back.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): soft and hard  
> **Fern**: Soft fruit is easy for a little one. Which is softer?  
> - ✅ A ripe banana → Yes! Squishy and soft. Easy to eat.
> - A hard nut → A nut is hard. Crunch! A ripe banana is softer.
> _For grown-ups: Sorted foods by how they feel: soft and hard._


#### `s8-complete`

_Gran sits by the pond, holding a thin grey baby._  
**Gran Hazel**: I found her by a ring of roots. Too tired to stand.  
**Gran Hazel**: So I carried her. Her mother runs that place.  
**You**: Fern found her mother. Her name is Bramble.  
**Gran Hazel**: Did she? Oh, Fern.  
**Gran Hazel**: Take this little one home, love.  
_At the ring, the little one sees her mum. She wiggles with joy!_  
_Bramble washes her, ear to ear._  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-LS1-2, notice): animal parents look after their babies  
> _Many animal mums and dads look after their babies._  
> _They clean them, feed them and keep them warm._  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that many animal parents clean, feed and warm their young._

_Then Bramble drops her old ribbon at your feet. A thank-you._  
**Nova**: That’s you invited. Come any time. Bring soft fruit.  

## S9. The Gate That Sticks

- `s9-step1`: Take the old trowel to Gran. · _She is on the cottage step. Give it to her from your bag._
- `s9-step2`: Go to the gate at sunset tomorrow. · _Corvin comes when the Sun goes down. Just stand by the gate._
- `s9-step3`: Be at the gate first thing in the morning. · _When the new day starts, go to the gate before anything else._

#### `s9-dusk`

_Evening. The gate rattles. Rattle, rattle._  
**Gran Hazel**: That’ll be him.  
_Gran doesn’t get up. Her eyes are shut._  
**Gran Hazel**: Go on. It’s your gate now.  
_Corvin is at the gate. He did a wrong thing, long ago._  
_He doesn’t look grumpy now. He looks lonely and tired._  
**Corvin Vale**: My hands were cold for years. Now they’re warm again.  
**Corvin Vale**: Getting warm hurts a bit. Nobody tells you that.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, do): cold hands tingle as they warm; rubbing hands warms them  
> _When cold hands warm up, they can tingle._  
> _(Rub your hands together fast. Can you feel them get warm?)_  
> Check: `edu:rub:hands:warm`
> _For grown-ups: Rubbed their hands together to feel them warm up._

_He pushes the gate. It sticks. He doesn’t know the trick._  
**Corvin Vale**: This trowel is Hazel’s. Please give it back to her.  

**Choices**

- **Why now?**
    - **Corvin Vale**: Because I can feel my hands again.
    - He walks away down the lane.
- **Do you want to come in?**
    - **Corvin Vale**: Yes. But not tonight. I haven’t asked yet.
    - He walks away. He doesn’t look back.
- **Say nothing. Take the trowel.**
    - You take it. The handle is warm.
    - **Corvin Vale**: Thank you.

#### `s9-trowel`

_Gran turns the trowel over in her hands._  
**Gran Hazel**: My old trowel! I wondered where it went.  
**Gran Hazel**: Corvin was my helper, long ago. Clever, and in a hurry.  
**Gran Hazel**: He tried to make my Nubbins keep very still. That was wrong.  
**Gran Hazel**: I stopped him. That night, he took some Nubbins away.  
**Gran Hazel**: In the morning, I made the gate stick.  
**You**: So he couldn’t come back in?  
**Gran Hazel**: No, love. So I’d hear him if he ever came back.  
**Gran Hazel**: A sticky gate rattles. I always hear it.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): sound words  
> _(Say the gate sounds together: rattle, creak, clack!)_  
> **Gran Hazel**: Rattle means somebody new. Clack means somebody who knows.  
> Check: `edu:say:sound-words:gate`
> _For grown-ups: Said sound words (rattle, creak, clack) out loud._


**Choices**

- **Are you still angry with him?**
    - **Gran Hazel**: I was, for a long time. Angry is heavy to carry.
    - **Gran Hazel**: I had Nubbins to look after. So I put it down.
- **Did you find the Nubbins he took?**
    - **Gran Hazel**: Most of them. I’m still sad about the rest.
- **Sit next to her. Say nothing.**
    - You sit. Gran leans on you, just a little.
    - **Gran Hazel**: There. That’s better than any words.

#### `s9-yours`

_Gran puts the trowel down on the step._  
**Gran Hazel**: He’ll come back at the next sunset.  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-ESS1-1, do): the parts of the day in order  
> _(Say the day in order: morning, afternoon, evening, night.)_  
> **Gran Hazel**: Sunset is in the evening. Then comes night.  
> Check: `edu:order:times-of-day`
> _For grown-ups: Put the parts of the day in order and placed sunset in the evening._

**Gran Hazel**: I’m not going down there. I’ve done my part. It’s your gate now.  
**You**: What do I say to him?  
**Gran Hazel**: Nothing, to start with. Sit and wait. It works on people too.  

#### `s9-ask`

_Sunset. Corvin is already at the gate._  
**Corvin Vale**: I practised this in the lane. A sheep heard me.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, do): animal sounds: sheep  
> _(What does a sheep say? Baa!)_  
> _(A baby sheep is a lamb. Say it in a little voice: baa!)_  
> Check: `edu:say:animal-sound:sheep`
> _For grown-ups: Made a sheep sound and learned that a baby sheep is a lamb._

_He puts both hands on the gate. He doesn’t open it._  
**Corvin Vale**: Long ago, I opened this gate without asking.  
**Corvin Vale**: That was wrong. I am sorry.  
**Corvin Vale**: So now I’m asking.  
**Corvin Vale**: May I come in, please?  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.Civ.8.K-2, notice): asking before going into someone’s home  
> _Corvin says sorry. Then he asks before he comes in._  
> _We ask first. It’s their home, and their choice._  
> _For grown-ups: Saw a character say sorry and ask permission before entering a home._

**Corvin Vale**: I’ll stand right here till morning. If it’s still shut, I’ll understand.  
_He steps back into the dark lane. He doesn’t go far._  

#### `s9-word`

_By supper, everyone knows. Corvin asked to come in._  
**Ember**: He took Ash. I’m just telling you what he did.  
**Luna**: Sable came from his garden. But she got out.  
**Fern**: I’d let him in. Then I’d worry all night!  
**Clover**: The grey stays on him too. You can only make it lighter.  
**Byte**: I’d open the gate. Don’t tell anyone.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, notice): listening to different ideas  
> _Each friend thinks something different._  
> _It’s good to listen to everyone. Then you choose._  
> _For grown-ups: Heard several different opinions and that it helps to listen to all of them._

_Gran says nothing. But she leaves her door unlocked._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5  
> _(Count the friends who spoke: Ember, Luna, Fern, Clover, Byte.)_  
> _(Five friends. Five ideas!)_  
> Check: `edu:count:friends:5`
> _For grown-ups: Counted five characters who shared an idea._


#### `s9-gate`

_Morning. The pond is grey and still._  
_Gran sits by the pond with a cup of tea._  
_Corvin is at the gate. He stood there all night._  
_Frost sparkles on his coat._  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS2-1, notice): frost forms on very cold nights  
> _Frost is tiny bits of ice._  
> _It forms on very cold nights. When the Sun warms it, it melts._  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that frost is tiny bits of ice that form on very cold nights and melt in the sun._

**Corvin Vale**: Hello, Hazel’s grandchild.  
_In the long grass, a little grey ear goes flat._  
**Corvin Vale**: I know what I took from her. I’m asking anyway.  
_Gran drinks her tea. She is listening._  

**Choices**

- **Open it for him. Don’t show him how.**
    - You lift and push. The gate opens.
    - **Corvin Vale**: Thank you. I’ll knock every time.
    - By the pond, Gran gives a tiny wave.
- **Leave it shut. Not today.**
    - You stay still. Corvin nods.
    - **Corvin Vale**: All right. That’s fair.
    - **Corvin Vale**: Thank you for taking the trowel.
    - He walks away down the lane.
    - Gran sits by the pond a little longer.

#### `s9-complete`

_Sparks comes first, running. Then Fern. Then everyone!_  
_One by one, they lift and push. Creak, creak, creak._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): taking turns  
> _Everyone wants to come through the gate. What helps?_  
> - ✅ Take turns, one by one → Yes. One by one. Everyone gets through.
> - Everyone push at once → Bump! Squash! Taking turns, one by one, works better.
> _For grown-ups: Talked about taking turns when everyone wants the same thing._

**Gran Hazel**: Good gate, that. I never did unstick it.  
_That is how Gran says it’s all done._  

## S10. Gran’s Three Wishes

- `s10-step1`: Wish one: write your Nubbins in Gran’s big book. · _The book is on the cottage shelf. Add every Nubbin you have hatched._
- `s10-step2`: Wish two: run a race while Gran watches. · _Open Races from the top bar. Any race will do. Winning doesn’t matter._
- `s10-step3`: Wish three: walk with Gran to the other three lanterns. · _Go at Gran’s pace. Stop when she stops._
- `s10-step4`: Walk with Gran to the bottom of the hill. · _After the big choice, follow Gran across the Grove to the hill path._

#### `s10-step`

_Evening. Gran sits on the step. Her feet are in warm water._  
**Gran Hazel**: Sit. There. I like to see the pond.  
_Gran Hazel is very poorly. The doctors can’t make her better._  
**Gran Hazel**: The doctor said one year. I’ve had fourteen months!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.C.6, ask): a year has 12 months; 14 is more than 12  
> _A year has twelve months. Gran has had fourteen._  
> _Is fourteen more than twelve, or less?_  
> - ✅ More → More! Two whole months more. I’m very pleased with myself.
> - Less → Count on from twelve: thirteen, fourteen. Fourteen is more!
> _For grown-ups: Learned that a year has twelve months and compared fourteen with twelve._

_Soon Gran will die. That means her body will stop working._  
**Gran Hazel**: Don’t be brave at me. Sad is allowed.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): all feelings are allowed, and what helps  
> _Sad, worried, cross: all feelings are allowed._  
> _A hug or sitting close to someone can help._  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that every feeling is allowed and that closeness and hugs can help._

**Gran Hazel**: I have three little wishes. And one favour.  
**Gran Hazel**: This is my news. Can I share it my own way?  

**Choices**

- **All right, Gran. Share it your own way.**
    - **Gran Hazel**: Thank you, love.
    - **Gran Hazel**: I’ll write them a letter. I’m good at letters.
    - **Gran Hazel**: Wish one: write your Nubbins in my big book.
- **They love you. They should say goodbye.**
    - **Gran Hazel**: They’ll make a fuss. Clover will cry on me.
    - **Gran Hazel**: All right. Tell them.
    - **Gran Hazel**: Wish one: write your Nubbins in my big book.

#### `s10-register`

_Gran reads your pages slowly. A finger under each line._  
**Gran Hazel**: Oh, what a funny name. I love it!  
**Gran Hazel**: Write the weather too. You’ll want that, later.  
_Under your last line, Gran writes one more._  
_“Keeper from this page on.” And then your name._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): the first letter of your name  
> _(What letter does your name start with?)_  
> _(Draw it in the air with your finger!)_  
> Check: `edu:write:letter:first-of-name`
> _For grown-ups: Named and drew the first letter of their own name._

**Gran Hazel**: Now it’s in the book. Things in the book stay.  

#### `s10-wish2`

**Gran Hazel**: Wish two: one race. I’ll sit at the back with a pie.  
**Sparks**: Hazel is coming to a RACE?  
**Sparks**: I’ll save you a seat! Front row!  
**Gran Hazel**: Sparks, love. I want the back row.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): front and back  
> _Where does Gran want to sit?_  
> - ✅ The back row → The back row. Behind everyone. Fine!
> - The front row → That’s what I wanted! But Gran said the back row.
> _For grown-ups: Used the position words front and back._

**Sparks**: …Back row. Right.  
**Gran Hazel**: Bring any Nubbin. Fast or slow. I don’t mind about winning.  
**Sparks**: She does mind. She once threw a hat at the finish line!  
**Gran Hazel**: It was a very small hat.  

**Choices**

- **Bring the fast one!**
    - **Gran Hazel**: Oh, lovely! I’ll cheer till I’ve no voice left.
- **Bring the slow one. The one who stops to look.**
    - **Gran Hazel**: The slow one? Good! I’ll get a second pie.

#### `s10-cheer`

_The crowd roars! From the back row, one voice is loudest._  
_Sparks has lost her voice. She cheers with no sound!_  
_Win or lose, Gran stands up, beaming. Half a pie in her hand._  
**Gran Hazel**: That one runs for you. That’s love. Never spend that.  
_Then Gran sits down, all of a sudden. She is tired._  
_Nobody hurries her._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, notice): waiting kindly with someone who is tired  
> _When someone is tired, we can wait with them._  
> _Waiting kindly is a way to show love._  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that waiting patiently with someone who is tired is a kind thing to do._


#### `s10-wish3`

_Evening. Gran’s third wish has a little drawing: four posts._  
**Gran Hazel**: Wish three. Walk the four lanterns with me. Slowly.  
_Gran holds your arm at the pond lantern._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): counting on: 1 and 3 more make 4  
> **Gran Hazel**: Four posts. We’re at the first one. How many more to go?  
> - ✅ Three → Yes. One here, three more. Four in all.
> - Two → Let’s count on: this is one. Two, three, four. Three more!
> - Four → Four in all. But we’re at one already. So three more!
> _For grown-ups: Counted on from one to four to find how many more._

**Gran Hazel**: This is Moss’s post. When I was young, I came here.  
_A memory. Snow. A young Hazel sits by the frozen pond._  
**Young Hazel**: I don’t know what I’m doing.  
_A small green-grey Nubbin sits near her. Day after day. All winter._  
_When the snow melts, it looks up at her._  

> **Lesson** (science, 2-ESS2-3, notice): snow and ice melt when it gets warmer  
> _In spring, the sunshine gets warmer._  
> _Snow and ice melt into water. Drip, drip!_  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that snow and ice melt into water when the weather warms in spring._

**Moss**: …You’ll do.  
**Gran Hazel**: Rule three. Moss taught it to me. Just by sitting.  

#### `s10-lanterns`

_At the stream lantern, Gran laughs. Briar would have._  
_At the old wall, Gran puts her hand on the post. Quiet._  
_Then the long grass. The last post glows bright and steady._  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-PS4-2, notice): we need light to see  
> _Lanterns make light in the dark._  
> _We need light to see. No light, no seeing!_  
> _For grown-ups: Learned that we need light to see things._

**Gran Hazel**: For years it only flickered. I looked every night.  
**Gran Hazel**: I stopped hoping. But I never stopped looking.  
**Gran Hazel**: Can I ask you something? I never asked anyone.
