_The whole crowd follows you in one long, wobbly line._  
_You walk to the pond. They walk to the pond._  
_You turn round. They turn round. Bump!_  
_You stop. They stop, sit, and fall asleep in a heap._  
_Barty is on top. Snore._  
**Mother Brindle**: Thank you, love. Keep the gourd and the shoe.  
_Now, if you call “Follow me!”, every Nubbin parades behind you._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, do): taking turns  
> _(Play follow-the-leader together!)_  
> _Take turns. First you lead, then your grown-up leads._  
> Check: `edu:play:follow-the-leader`
> _For grown-ups: Your child played follow-the-leader and took turns being the leader._


## Q10. Haggle

- `q10-s1`: Offer Harrow less than he asks for the brass bell. · _Tap the bell and choose Haggle. Start with a small number._
- `q10-s2`: Haggle with Harrow for three rounds. · _Each time, offer a number in the middle._

#### `q10-intro`

_There’s a new stall, full of odds and ends._  
_Behind it sits Harrow, with a pencil behind each ear._  
**Harrow Tuppence**: Morning! Everything a Grove could want!  
_You spot a little brass bell. Your Nubbin wants it._  
**You**: How much is the bell?  
**Harrow Tuppence**: Twenty Shells.  
_You count out twenty Shells._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.A.1, do): count to 20  
> _(Count to twenty together. Use fingers and toes!)_  
> _One, two, three… all the way to twenty!_  
> Check: `edu:count:shells:20`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted all the way to twenty._

**Harrow Tuppence**: Twenty? Just like that? Where’s the fun in that?  
**Harrow Tuppence**: Haggling is a game. You say a small number. I say a big one.  

**Choices**

- **Sorry! How does haggling work?**
    - **Harrow Tuppence**: You say a number. I say a bigger one. We meet in the middle.
- **But the tag says twenty.**
    - **Harrow Tuppence**: The tag is just the start! Nobody listens to the tag.

#### `q10-first-offer`

**You**: …Eight Shells?  
**Harrow Tuppence**: EIGHT?  
_He clutches his heart. He clutches the bell._  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-PS4-1, notice): how a bell makes sound  
> _Ting! When a bell rings, it shakes very, very fast._  
> _That shaking makes the sound._  
> _Touch it gently, and you can feel the buzz!_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that a bell shakes very fast when it rings, and the shaking makes the sound._

**Harrow Tuppence**: Now we’re talking! Lovely! Go on!  

#### `q10-rounds`

**Harrow Tuppence**: Three rounds. I go down. You go up.  
**Harrow Tuppence**: Not too quick. Not too slow. Just right.  
_Your Nubbin climbs on the counter to watch._  
**Harrow Tuppence**: Twenty. Because I like your face.  
**You**: Ten.  
**Harrow Tuppence**: Ten? For a BELL? Eighteen!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.C.6, ask): comparing numbers  
> _Which is more: ten or eighteen?_  
> - ✅ Eighteen → Yes! Eighteen is more than ten.
> - Ten → Let’s count up: ten, eleven, twelve… eighteen! Eighteen is more.
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared ten and eighteen and found which is more._


**Choices**

- **Pretend to walk away.**
    - One, two, three steps…
    - **Harrow Tuppence**: Sixteen!
- **Say the bell is lovely, then go lower.**
    - **Harrow Tuppence**: Kind words AND a low number! Clever.

#### `q10-deal`

**Harrow Tuppence**: Fourteen. My last offer. Oh, my poor bell!  
**You**: Fourteen it is.  
_You shake hands. Harrow’s eyes are a bit wet._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, ask): happy tears  
> _Harrow is happy, but his eyes are wet._  
> _Can people cry when they are happy?_  
> - ✅ Yes → Yes! Some people cry happy tears.
> - No → They can! Happy tears are real. Harrow has some.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that people sometimes cry when they feel very happy._

**Harrow Tuppence**: It’s the wind. Just the wind.  

#### `q10-complete`

_You give Harrow fourteen Shells. He counts them twice._  

> **Lesson** (maths, 1.OA.A.1, do): counting on  
> _The tag said twenty. You paid fourteen._  
> _(Count on from fourteen, one finger each: fifteen… twenty!)_  
> _Six fingers. You saved six Shells!_  
> Check: `edu:count-on:shells:14-20`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted on from fourteen to twenty to find the Shells saved._

**Harrow Tuppence**: That was a proper bit of business!  
_He gives you a little gourd too. Free!_  
_Your Nubbin rings the bell. Ting-a-ling!_  
**Harrow Tuppence**: You can haggle at the shop now too. Just a little.  
_Now the shop lets you haggle, for a bit off._  

**Choices**

- **Give him the six Shells you saved.**
    - **Harrow Tuppence**: Don’t you dare!
    - **Harrow Tuppence**: A deal’s a deal. Put it away.
    - He looks very proud of you.
- **Shake hands again, for luck.**
    - He haggles you down to a shorter handshake. He’s delighted.

## Q11. One Thin Slice

- `q11-s1`: Pick fresh mint for Mr Crisp. · _Mint grows by the pond. Pick the top leaves for Mr Crisp._
- `q11-s2`: Stay and watch Gorbold with the last thin slice. · _Stay in the bathhouse. Gorbold waits for you if you go._

#### `q11-intro`

_The bathhouse is warm and steamy._  
_In the pool floats a very round Nubbin. Bob, bob._  
_This is Gorbold. Fruit stones float all around it._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds: g  
> _Gorbold starts with a g sound. G-g-g!_  
> _Which word starts with g too?_  
> - ✅ Grape → Yes! G-g-grape. Gorbold and grape!
> - Moon → Moon starts with mmm. Listen: g-g-grape starts like Gorbold!
> _For grown-ups: Your child listened for the “g” sound at the start of Gorbold’s name._

**Gorbold**: More.  
_Mr Crisp appears, with a shiny silver tray._  
**Mr Crisp**: Gorbold ate all the fruit. And the bowl. And the menu!  
**Mr Crisp**: I need fresh mint for one last thing. Could you fetch some?  
**You**: Gorbold looks very full.  
**Mr Crisp**: It is a VERY small thing.  

**Choices**

- **I’ll fetch the mint.**
    - **Mr Crisp**: Splendid. The patch by the pond. Top leaves only.
- **Ask Gorbold if that’s OK.**
    - **Gorbold**: More.
    - That seems to be a yes.

#### `q11-mint`

**Mr Crisp**: Perfect mint! One moment.  
_He vanishes into the steam._  
_Swish, swish. Very careful slicing._  
_The air smells fresh and minty._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): the sense of smell  
> _Mmm, minty! What do we smell with?_  
> - ✅ Our nose → Yes! Sniff, sniff. Our nose smells the mint.
> - Our knees → Knees can’t smell! We sniff with our nose.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named the nose as the part of the body we smell with._


#### `q11-slice`

_Mr Crisp comes back. On his tray: one thin, minty slice._  
**Mr Crisp**: For you, Gorbold. One tiny slice.  
**Gorbold**: Full.  

> **Lesson** (health, notice): listening to your tummy  
> _Gorbold says “full”. That is his tummy talking._  
> _When our tummy feels full, we can stop eating._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that when our tummy feels full, we can stop eating._

**Mr Crisp**: It is thinner than a leaf.  
_Gorbold looks at the slice. The slice looks at Gorbold._  
_The steam goes very still._  

**Choices**

- **Ask: Do you want it, Gorbold?**
    - **Gorbold**: …Just one tiny bit.
- **Take a big step back.**
    - Mr Crisp steps back too. He has done this before.

#### `q11-pop`

_Gorbold eats the slice. Hic!_  
_Then… POP!_  
_Sparkly confetti fills the bathhouse._  
_Gorbold bobs in the pool, normal-sized, blinking._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): bigger and smaller  
> _Is Gorbold bigger or smaller now?_  
> - ✅ Smaller → Yes! Before it was very round. Now it is smaller.
> - Bigger → Look again. Pop! It was very round. Now it is smaller.
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared Gorbold’s size before and after._

**Gorbold**: Light!  

#### `q11-complete`

**Mr Crisp**: Ah. That always happens. Lovely.  
_He sweeps the confetti into a neat pile._  
**Mr Crisp**: For you. My own recipe: thin slices.  
**Mr Crisp**: One slice fills a Nubbin to just full. Never too full.  
**Gorbold**: More?  
**Mr Crisp**: No.  
_Gorbold pushes one fruit stone over to your Nubbin._  
**Gorbold**: Yours.  
_Mr Crisp stops. Gorbold has never shared before!_  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): sharing  
> _Gorbold shared! How does your Nubbin feel?_  
> - ✅ Happy → Yes! Sharing makes friends feel happy. Gorbold feels good too.
> - Grumpy → Look at its smile! Being shared with feels happy.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about how it feels when someone shares with us._


## Q12. The Very Quiet Nubbin

- `q12-s1`: Find three clues that the shop’s Nubbin is a toy. · _Try feeding it, tickling it, and putting it in the pond._
- `q12-s2`: Show Mr Fenwick Mull your clues. · _Put all three clues on the shop counter. Be calm and clear._

#### `q12-intro`

_A parcel arrives! Inside is a Nubbin._  
_The note says: “One quiet Nubbin. Do not overfeed.”_  
_Your Nubbins poke it. It does not move._  
_One of its eyes is a button!_  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, ask): living things move and grow  
> _Living things eat, move and grow._  
> _Does this Nubbin move?_  
> - ✅ No → No, it doesn’t. Not a wiggle. That’s a clue!
> - Yes → Let’s watch… Nope. Not a wiggle. That’s a clue!
> _For grown-ups: Your child used “does it move?” as a clue to whether something is alive._

_You take it back to the shop._  
**You**: This Nubbin doesn’t move.  
**Mr Fenwick Mull**: Ah. Shh. It’s very, very sleepy.  
**You**: It has stitches down its back!  
**Mr Fenwick Mull**: Lovely stitches! Very rare!  

**Choices**

- **I want my Shells back.**
    - **Mr Fenwick Mull**: Shells back? We never do that. Nothing ever goes wrong!
- **I’ll wait for it to wake up.**
    - You wait. Mr Mull waits. The Nubbin waits the most.

#### `q12-evidence`

_Clue one: you offer it a heart fruit. It won’t eat._  
_Every Nubbin loves heart fruit!_  
_Clue two: you tickle its feet. Nothing._  
_Your own Nubbin giggles instead!_  
_Clue three: it floats in the pond. A price tag bobs up._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.4, do): count to 3  
> _(Count the clues on your fingers.)_  
> _Won’t eat. Won’t giggle. Price tag. Three clues!_  
> Check: `edu:count:clues:3`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted three clues on their fingers._


#### `q12-argument`

_You put the clues on the shiny counter._  
**Mr Fenwick Mull**: It didn’t eat? It’s shy!  
**You**: It didn’t giggle when I tickled it.  
**Mr Fenwick Mull**: Tired. Long journey.  
**You**: It floated in the pond.  
**Mr Fenwick Mull**: It loves a swim!  
_He pats it. It slowly topples off the counter. Flump._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, ask): honesty  
> _Is Mr Mull telling the truth?_  
> - ✅ No. It’s a toy. → Right. The clues say toy. The truth is always best.
> - Yes → Hmm. The clues say toy. He isn’t telling the truth yet.
> _For grown-ups: Your child noticed when someone wasn’t telling the truth, and why the truth matters._

**You**: This is a toy. It was never a real Nubbin.  

**Choices**

- **Say nothing. Just look at him.**
    - You look. He looks. Tick, tock. He blinks first.
- **Read the price tag out loud.**
    - **You**: “Toy. Stuffed. Not alive. Made with love.”
    - **Mr Fenwick Mull**: …Made with love, though.
- **Then I’d like a new one.**
    - **Mr Fenwick Mull**: None left! Try my brother’s shop, over the hill.
    - Over the hill: the same shop. The same man. In a stick-on moustache!
    - **Mr Fenwick Mull**: I’m his brother. Never met him.
    - The moustache falls off. You walk back together.

#### `q12-crack`

**Mr Fenwick Mull**: All right! It’s a toy. It was always a toy.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): rhyming words  
> _Toy… which word rhymes with toy?_  
> - ✅ Boy → Yes! Toy, boy, joy. They all rhyme!
> - Cat → Cat ends in “at”. Listen: t-oy, b-oy. Boy rhymes with toy!
> _For grown-ups: Your child found a word that rhymes with “toy”._

**Mr Fenwick Mull**: Here are your Shells back.  
**Mr Fenwick Mull**: And I’ll start a returns counter. The first in the valley!  
_He looks lighter now. Telling the truth helps._  

#### `q12-complete`

_Mr Mull counts the Shells into your hand._  
**Mr Fenwick Mull**: Keep the toy, though. I’ve grown fond of it.  
_You carry it home._  
_Your Nubbins take it to the nest and snuggle round it._  
_By night, they are all fast asleep, cuddling the toy._  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, do): alive and not alive  
> _Your Nubbins are alive. They eat, grow and move._  
> _The toy is not alive. But it’s lovely to cuddle!_  
> _(Point to something alive. Now something not alive.)_  
> Check: `edu:sort:alive:nubbin-toy`
> _For grown-ups: Your child sorted things into alive and not alive._

_Now the shop takes things back, if you say what’s wrong._  

## Q13. The Silly Walk Booth

- `q13-s1`: Show Mr Strutt a walk of your own. · _Tap a pattern of moves on the pad, then press Walk._
- `q13-s2`: Show Mr Strutt a really silly walk. · _Mix spins, hops, backwards steps and stops._

#### `q13-intro`

_A little booth stands at the crossroads._  
_The sign says: PRIZES FOR WALKS. VERY SILLY ONLY._  
_Inside is Mr Strutt, in a round black hat._  
**Mr Hollis Strutt**: Most walks here are so sensible. It keeps me awake.  
_He walks to his cupboard. Kick, sit, wiggle, twirl._  
_It takes four whole minutes!_  
**Mr Hollis Strutt**: Now, show me yours. Surprise me!  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): hopping on one foot  
> **Mr Hollis Strutt**: First, a warm-up. Can you hop on one foot?  
> _(Stand up and hop five times. Count them!)_  
> _Hop, hop, hop, hop, hop!_  
> Check: `edu:hop:one-foot:5`
> _For grown-ups: Your child warmed up by hopping on one foot._


**Choices**

- **I don’t think I have one.**
    - **Mr Hollis Strutt**: Everybody has one. Most people hide it.
- **I’ve been practising!**
    - **Mr Hollis Strutt**: Marvellous! Don’t tell me. Show me!

#### `q13-first`

_You do your walk. It has a hop in it._  
_You are proud of the hop._  
**Mr Hollis Strutt**: Hmm. That’s quite a sensible walk.  
**Mr Hollis Strutt**: The hop is good. The rest was just walking.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, notice): trying again  
> **Mr Hollis Strutt**: That’s all right! First tries are for learning.  
> _When something is tricky, we try again._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that first tries are for learning, and we can try again._


#### `q13-notes`

**Mr Hollis Strutt**: Let me give you some tips.  
**Mr Hollis Strutt**: More knees! Up, up, up!  
**Mr Hollis Strutt**: Then do something odd with your elbows.  

> **Lesson** (maths, ELOF: Mathematics Development, ask): patterns  
> **Mr Hollis Strutt**: Knee, elbow, knee, elbow… what comes next?  
> - ✅ Knee → Yes! Knee, elbow, KNEE! You found the pattern.
> - Elbow → Let’s say it slowly: knee, elbow, knee, elbow… KNEE!
> _For grown-ups: Your child spotted what comes next in a knee-elbow pattern._

**Mr Hollis Strutt**: And once, walk backwards. Look as if you mean it.  
_Your Nubbin tries. It falls over and stays lying down._  
**Mr Hollis Strutt**: Now THAT is promising!  

**Choices**

- **Copy your Nubbin.**
    - You try walking lying down. It doesn’t work. It IS silly!
- **Make up your own.**
    - **Mr Hollis Strutt**: Bold! I am reaching for my stamp.

#### `q13-approved`

_You walk. Hop! Crouch! Spin!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ask): balance  
> _Which is harder: standing on one foot, or two?_  
> - ✅ One foot → Yes! On one foot we wobble. Arms out help us balance.
> - Two feet → Try it! Two feet is steady. One foot wobbles. Arms out help!
> _For grown-ups: Your child noticed that balancing on one foot is harder, and arms out help._

_Backwards! Elbows! Ta-da!_  
_Mr Strutt stands up slowly. He takes off his hat._  
**Mr Hollis Strutt**: The silliest walk since Tuesday. Yes!  

#### `q13-complete`

_He stamps your form. Stamp! Stamp! STAMP!_  
**Mr Hollis Strutt**: Approved! Walk silly, often, and everywhere!  
_He gives you a spare round hat. It’s too big. It’s perfect._  
**Mr Hollis Strutt**: You don’t need a form to be silly, you know.  
**Mr Hollis Strutt**: But people do like being told they may.  
_Now you can walk silly whenever you like!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, do): measuring with steps  
> **Mr Hollis Strutt**: How many giant steps to the door? Now tiny steps?  
> _(Try it! Count both.)_  
> _Tiny steps take more. Giant steps take fewer!_  
> Check: `edu:measure:steps:giant-tiny`
> _For grown-ups: Your child measured a distance in giant steps and tiny steps and compared them._


## Q14. The Comfy Chair

- `q14-s1`: Bring the comfy chair from the Workshop. · _It’s the big red chair by the door. Carry it to the nest._
- `q14-s2`: Put your sleepiest Nubbin in the comfy chair. · _Look for the Nubbin that yawns. Put it on the chair._

#### `q14-intro`

_You are brushing a Nubbin. Bang! The gate flies open._  
_In sweep three Keepers in long red robes. One trips._  
**Brother Pouffe**: Surprise! We are the Nap Patrol!  
**Brother Pouffe**: Surprise is our best tool. Surprise, and cushions…  
**Brother Bolster**: And blankets.  
**Brother Pouffe**: Our THREE tools! Hmm. Out we go. We’ll do the door again.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, ask): count to 3  
> _Surprise, cushions, blankets. How many tools?_  
> - ✅ Three → Yes! One, two, three tools.
> - Two → Count again: surprise, cushions, blankets. Three!
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted the Nap Patrol’s three tools._

_Out they go. Bang! In they come. Nobody trips._  
**Brother Pouffe**: Your Nubbins look tired. Sister Tuft! The big red chair!  
**Sister Tuft**: We left it at your Workshop.  
**Brother Pouffe**: …Keeper, could you bring it here for us, please?  

**Choices**

- **OK. I’ll fetch it.**
    - **Brother Pouffe**: Splendid! We shall wait here, looking scary.
- **Can I hold a cushion?**
    - **Brother Bolster**: …Just for a minute.
    - It is very, very soft.

#### `q14-chair`

_You put the chair by the nest. It’s big, red and squashy._  
**Sister Tuft**: Behold! The comfy chair.  
_Bolster holds up the cushions. Tuft fluffs the chair._  
_Fluff, fluff!_  

> **Lesson** (science, 2-PS1-1, ask): soft and hard  
> _Which is soft: a cushion or a stone?_  
> - ✅ A cushion → Yes! Cushions are soft and squashy. Stones are hard.
> - A stone → Ouch! Stones are hard. Cushions are soft.
> _For grown-ups: Your child sorted things by how they feel: soft or hard._


#### `q14-sentence`

**Brother Pouffe**: Now! Bring us your sleepiest Nubbin!  

> **Lesson** (health, ask): signs of being tired  
> _How can you tell a Nubbin is sleepy?_  
> - ✅ It yawns and rubs its eyes → Yes! Yawning means a body needs rest.
> - It jumps about → Jumping means lots of energy! Yawning means sleepy.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named the signs that a body needs rest._

**Brother Pouffe**: It shall sit in the comfy chair until lunch.  
**Brother Bolster**: With a woolly blanket.  
**Sister Tuft**: And no getting up. Not even for a snack.  
**Brother Pouffe**: Unless it’s a very small snack.  
_They nod. Very serious. Very cosy._  

**Choices**

- **Bring the sleepiest Nubbin.**
    - It yawns so wide it nearly falls in!
- **Offer to sit in it yourself.**
    - **Brother Pouffe**: Ha! Not today. Bring us a sleepy Nubbin!

#### `q14-asleep`

_The Nubbin sinks into the chair._  
_Bolster tucks in a cushion. Tuft lays a blanket._  
_It is asleep before the blanket lands._  
_Snore… snore… like a tiny kettle._  

> **Lesson** (music, ask): loud and soft  
> _Shh! Should we be loud or soft now?_  
> - ✅ Soft → Yes. (Whisper “night night” in your softest voice.)
> - Loud → Loud would wake it! Let’s whisper softly: “night night”.
> _For grown-ups: Your child chose a soft voice for a quiet moment._


#### `q14-complete`

_Two hours later, the Nubbin wakes. Stretch! It glows._  
**Brother Pouffe**: Ha! Look at it. All better!  
**Brother Bolster**: The best nap we ever gave.  
**Sister Tuft**: Let’s go. Three more Groves to surprise before tea.  
_Out they sweep. One trips. Back they come for the chair._  
**Brother Pouffe**: Keep it! We have a whole room of them.  
**Brother Pouffe**: One last question. When did YOU last sit down?  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, ask): noticing when you are tired  
> _When you feel tired, what helps?_  
> - ✅ A rest or a cuddle → Yes! Rest helps our bodies feel better.
> - Running faster → Running makes us more tired! A rest or a cuddle helps.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about what helps when they feel tired._

_Now tired Nubbins nap better in the comfy chair._  

**Choices**

- **Sit down, just for a minute.**
    - You sit. Tuft tucks a blanket over your knees.
    - You wake an hour later with a cushion on your face!
    - **Brother Pouffe**: Ha! Our work is never done.
- **No time. Too much to do!**
    - **Sister Tuft**: That’s what they all say.
    - **Brother Pouffe**: Then keep the chair. It will wait. Chairs are good at waiting.

## Q15. The Empty Fruit Stall

- `q15-s1`: Ask Mr Dibble for six different fruits. · _Pick fruits from the list at the stall._
- `q15-s2`: Keep asking until you’ve tried all twelve fruits. · _Keep choosing from the list. Something happens at twelve._

#### `q15-intro`

_A smart green stall has opened by the bridge._  
_The sign says: EVERY FRUIT IN THE VALLEY!_  
_Mr Dibble beams, in a stripy apron and a straw hat._  
**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Good morning! Any fruit you like!  

> **Lesson** (science, ask): seeds inside fruit  
> _Fruit grows on plants. What is inside most fruits?_  
> - ✅ Seeds → Yes! Seeds. Plant a seed, and a new plant can grow.
> - Socks → Ha! No socks. Most fruits have seeds inside.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that most fruits have seeds inside, and seeds can grow new plants._

_The boxes are all under flat cloths. Very flat._  
**You**: A run fruit, please.  
**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Ah. All gone. They ran away!  
**You**: A swim fruit, then?  
**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: They went for a swim. Sorry!  

**Choices**

- **Keep asking, politely.**
    - **Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Please do! Asking is half the fun. For me.
- **Lift a cloth.**
    - Mr Dibble gently puts the cloth back down.
    - **Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Let’s not spoil the surprise.

#### `q15-six`

**You**: Fly fruit?  
**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Flew away.  
**You**: Power fruit? Stamina fruit? Sun fruit?  
**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Lifted. Eaten. And the sun fruit set. They do that.  
_That’s six fruits. And still no fruit!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 6  
> _(Count the fruits you asked for on your fingers.)_  
> _Run, swim, fly, power, stamina, sun. Six!_  
> Check: `edu:count:fruits-asked:6`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted the six fruits asked for on their fingers._


#### `q15-twelve`

_You keep asking. Mr Dibble keeps smiling._  
_Behind the stall, a Nubbin plays three notes. Plink, plonk, plunk._  

> **Lesson** (music, do): copying a pattern of sounds  
> _(Clap the tune: plink, plonk, plunk.)_  
> _Now very slowly. Plink… plonk… plunk._  
> _Now fast! Plinkplonkplunk!_  
> Check: `edu:clap:pattern:plink-plonk-plunk`
> _For grown-ups: Your child copied a three-note pattern, slow and then fast._

**You**: Moon fruit?  
**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Only at night. And at night I’m closed.  
**You**: Heart fruit?  
**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: A Nubbin loved it so much, I gave it away.  
**You**: Could the music stop?  
**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Stop? It makes the stall feel special!  
_Plink… plonk… plunk. Even slower._  

#### `q15-confess`

_By fruit number twelve, Mr Dibble stops smiling._  
_He takes off his hat and holds it._  
**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: I have to tell you something. There’s no fruit. Not one pip.  
**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: I just liked having a stall.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, ask): telling the truth is brave  
> _Mr Dibble told the truth. Was that brave?_  
> - ✅ Yes → Yes! Telling the truth can be hard. He did it.
> - No → It took courage! Saying “I have to tell you something” is brave.
> _For grown-ups: Your child talked about how telling the truth can take courage._

**You**: A fruit stall with no fruit?  
**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Yes. It’s a very nice stall, though.  

**Choices**

- **Let’s fill it with real fruit!**
    - **Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Real fruit? But people would buy it! Then it’d be gone!
    - **Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: …I’ll put out one run fruit. On a cushion.
- **Don’t change a thing.**
    - **Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Really? You don’t mind?
    - **You**: It’s the friendliest stall in the valley.
    - **Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: That’s the nicest thing anyone has said to me.

#### `q15-complete`

**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: I have no fruit. But I know lots about fruit.  
_He brings out a big page. Every fruit, drawn beautifully._  

> **Lesson** (art, ask): naming colours  
> _On the page, the sun fruit is yellow. What else is yellow?_  
> - ✅ A banana → Yes! Bananas are yellow, like the sun fruit.
> - Grass → Grass is green. Bananas are yellow, like the sun fruit.
> _For grown-ups: Your child matched things that share a colour._

**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Where it grows. What it does. Who likes it best.  
_It goes in your Collection book. It fits perfectly._  
**Mr Pomeroy Dibble**: Take the straw hat too.  
_As you go, he calls to the next customer: “Sorry! All gone!”_  

## Q16. The Argument Booth

- `q16-s1`: Pay Byte 5 Shells for an argument. · _Tap Byte’s booth and choose Pay._
- `q16-s2`: Win the argument before the sand runs out. · _Don’t just say “no” back. Ask Byte for reasons._

#### `q16-intro`

_Byte has a booth. The sign says: ARGUMENTS. 5 SHELLS._  
_On the counter is a sand timer._  
_Next door is a booth called COMPLAINTS. Also Byte, in another hat._  
**Byte**: Good afternoon. Would you like an argument?  
**You**: What kind?  
**Byte**: A good one. Fair, and timed. One minute.  
**Byte**: Five Shells, please. First.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.4, do): count to 5  
> **Byte**: Count them onto the counter, please.  
> _(Count together: one, two, three, four, five.)_  
> Check: `edu:count:shells:5`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted five Shells, one at a time._


**Choices**

- **Pay the five Shells.**
    - **Byte**: Thank you. Sit down to begin.
- **Four Shells?**
    - **Byte**: No. That one was free. Don’t do it again.

#### `q16-flip`

_Byte turns the sand timer over._  
_The sand starts to fall. Shhh._  

> **Lesson** (science, notice): things fall down  
> _Watch the sand. It always falls down, never up._  
> _When the top is empty, one minute is over._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child watched sand fall down through a timer and heard how it measures a minute._

**Byte**: Your minute starts now.  
**Byte**: Please begin.  

#### `q16-minute`

**You**: Your booth is very nice.  
**Byte**: No, it isn’t.  
**You**: It’s a lovely day. It rained, and now there’s a rainbow!  

> **Lesson** (science, ask): rainbows  
> _A rainbow comes when the sun shines through raindrops._  
> _What colours are in a rainbow?_  
> - ✅ Red, orange, yellow, green, blue… → Yes! And purple too. So many colours!
> - Only grey → Rainbows are bright! Red, orange, yellow, green, blue and purple.
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that sunshine and raindrops make a rainbow, and named its colours._

**Byte**: That is a smudge.  
**You**: You just say no to everything!  
**Byte**: No, I don’t.  
_The sand is half gone._  

**Choices**

- **Agree with Byte. See what happens.**
    - **You**: You’re right. It’s a smudge.
    - **Byte**: It is clearly a rainbow!
- **Take a deep breath.**
    - You breathe in. Byte breathes in too, just in case.

#### `q16-win`

**You**: Byte, you never say why.  
**Byte**: Yes, I do.  
**You**: Saying “no” isn’t a reason. Tell me why!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, SL.K.1, ask): giving reasons with “because”  
> _Which one gives a reason?_  
> - ✅ “It’s a rainbow, because I can see colours.” → Yes! “Because” tells us why.
> - “No.” → Just “no” has no reason. “Because” tells us why!
> _For grown-ups: Your child picked the answer that gives a reason using “because”._

_Byte opens his mouth. Then he stops._  
_He looks at the sand timer for help._  
**Byte**: …That’s fair.  

**Choices**

- **Go next door to Complaints.**
    - You go next door. Byte swaps hats and follows.
    - **Byte**: Complaints. Go on.
    - **You**: The argument man just kept saying no.
    - **Byte**: Did he? I shall talk to him.
    - He swaps hats and tells himself off.
- **Shake hands. Good game.**
    - **Byte**: It wasn’t a game. It was… yes. Fine. Good game.

#### `q16-complete`

_The last grain of sand falls._  
**Byte**: Nobody has won before. They just get cross.  
**Byte**: Saying no is easy. I called it thinking.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): disagreeing kindly  
> _If you think something different from a friend, what can you say?_  
> - ✅ “I think something different, because…” → Yes. Kind, and with a reason. Very good.
> - “You’re wrong, wrong, wrong!” → That can hurt feelings. Try: “I think something different, because…”
> _For grown-ups: Your child practised a kind way to say they think something different._

**Byte**: Keep the timer. I have lots of sand.  
**Byte**: You’re in my Talking Club. The first member!  
**You**: Is there a club?  
**Byte**: There is now. No arguing.  

## Q17. The Dock Dance

- `q17-s1`: Learn the dock dance. · _Open Karate on the dock. Tap in time with the beat._
- `q17-s2`: Dance the whole dance on the dock. · _Hit every beat. The last beat isn’t yours to win!_

#### `q17-intro`

_At the end of the dock stand two Keepers in white shirts._  
_Each holds a shiny rubber fish. Squeak!_  
**Mr Pilchard**: Good morning. I’m Pilchard. This is Grampus. We dance.  
**Mr Grampus**: Just us two.  
_They show you. Step, bow, boop! A rubber fish on the nose._  
_Then the other way. Step, bow, boop!_  

> **Lesson** (music, do): moving to a pattern  
> _(Stand up! Step… bow… boop your nose!)_  
> _Step, bow, boop. Step, bow, boop!_  
> Check: `edu:dance:pattern:step-bow-boop`
> _For grown-ups: Your child danced a three-step pattern: step, bow, boop._

_Nobody smiles. It’s strangely beautiful._  
**Mr Grampus**: Will you dance with us?  

**Choices**

- **I’d love to!**
    - **Mr Pilchard**: Splendid. You’ll need a fish.
- **Do the fish mind?**
    - **Mr Grampus**: They’re rubber fish. They love it. Listen: squeak!

#### `q17-learned`

_Step, bow, boop. Step, bow, boop._  
_Your rubber fish squeaks every time._  

> **Lesson** (science, 4-LS1-1, notice): how fish breathe  
> **Mr Grampus**: Real fish live in the water.  
> **Mr Grampus**: They breathe with gills, on the sides of their heads.  
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that real fish live in water and breathe with gills._

**Mr Pilchard**: Good. Very good. Your wrist is a bit too keen.  
**Mr Grampus**: Keen wrists end up in the water.  

#### `q17-performance`

**Mr Pilchard**: Now the whole dance! In front of everyone!  
_Nubbins sit on the bank to watch. One has a snack._  
**Mr Grampus**: Keep to the beat. Whatever happens.  
_Grampus brings out a second rubber fish. It is VERY big._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): bigger and smaller  
> _Which fish is bigger?_  
> - ✅ The big finish fish → Yes! It’s much bigger than your little fish.
> - Your little fish → Yours is little. The big finish fish is much bigger!
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared the sizes of two fish._

**You**: That’s not the same fish!  
**Mr Grampus**: It is the big finish fish.  

**Choices**

- **Keep to the beat.**
    - You plant your feet. Your little fish squeaks.
- **What happens at the end?**
    - **Mr Pilchard**: Water, mostly.
    - **Mr Grampus**: Gently, though.

#### `q17-splash`

_Step, bow, boop. Every beat just right!_  
_Then Grampus swings the big fish. Slowly. Gently._  
_Boop! You wobble… and topple into the pond. SPLASH!_  
_The water is warm and shallow. A duck looks at you._  

> **Lesson** (health, notice): water safety  
> _This pond is warm and shallow, and grown-ups are right here._  
> _We only go in water with a grown-up watching._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that we only go in water when a grown-up is watching._

_The Nubbins cheer. That was the plan!_  

#### `q17-complete`

_You climb out. Pilchard hands you a towel._  
_Grampus hands you the big finish fish._  
**Mr Grampus**: Whoever falls in keeps the big fish. That’s the rule.  
**Mr Pilchard**: It’s great for knocking fruit out of trees.  
**Mr Grampus**: We dance every morning, usually for nobody.  
**Mr Grampus**: It’s nice to be watched. But we’d dance anyway.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, ask): doing what you love  
> _They dance even when nobody watches. Why?_  
> - ✅ Because they love it → Yes! When we love something, we do it for joy.
> - Because they have to → Nobody makes them. They dance because they love it!
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about why people do things they love, even with nobody watching._

_Now your Power Nubbins can knock fruit down with the big fish!_  

## Q18. Fruit Class

- `q18-s1`: Peel the banana before the Nubbin can wave it. · _When the banana goes up, tap Peel._
- `q18-s2`: Catch every raspberry in the bowl. · _Slide the bowl left and right under the berries._
- `q18-s3`: Toss the pineapple to your Nubbin. · _Aim at your Nubbin and tap Throw. Gently!_

#### `q18-intro`

_In the clearing, a Keeper with a bushy moustache marches about._  
_Three Nubbins sit on logs. They look a bit nervous._  
**Sergeant Grimble**: YOU! Late! Sit on a log!  
**Sergeant Grimble**: Sergeant Grimble. Tonight: what to do when someone has fruit!  
_A Nubbin puts up its paw._  
**Sergeant Grimble**: NO. Not sticks. Tonight is FRUIT.  
_The Nubbin puts its paw down. Very slowly._  
**Sergeant Grimble**: First, the banana. Someone waves a banana at you. What do you do?  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, do): clapping syllables  
> _Let’s clap “banana” first._  
> _(Clap it together: ba… na… na!)_  
> _Three claps!_  
> Check: `edu:clap:syllables:banana`
> _For grown-ups: Your child clapped the three beats in “banana”._


**Choices**

- **Duck!**
    - **Sergeant Grimble**: DUCK? Bananas are curved, Keeper! It’ll find you!
- **Talk to them nicely.**
    - **Sergeant Grimble**: With a BANANA in their hand?
- **Peel it!**
    - **Sergeant Grimble**: …Correct! Who told you?

#### `q18-banana`

_A Nubbin waves a banana at you._  
_You peel it. Zip, zip, zip!_  
_The Nubbin looks at the bare banana. Oops._  
_It sits back down, a bit embarrassed._  
**Sergeant Grimble**: PERFECT! A peeled banana is a beaten banana!  

> **Lesson** (health, notice): fruit is good food  
> **Sergeant Grimble**: And a peeled banana is a tasty snack.  
> **Sergeant Grimble**: Fruit helps our bodies grow strong. Eat up!  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that fruit like bananas helps our bodies grow strong._


#### `q18-raspberry`

**Sergeant Grimble**: Next, the raspberry. Small. Fast. Red.  

> **Lesson** (art, ask): the colour red  
> _Raspberries are red. Which of these is red too?_  
> - ✅ A strawberry → Yes! Strawberries are red, like raspberries.
> - A leaf → Most leaves are green. Strawberries are red, like raspberries!
> _For grown-ups: Your child found another red thing to match the raspberries._

**Sergeant Grimble**: There is never just one. There are lots!  
_A Nubbin stands up with a bowl of raspberries._  
**Sergeant Grimble**: Hold out a bowl. Catch them all. None on the floor!  
**You**: Why not on the floor?  
**Sergeant Grimble**: They STAIN, Keeper! Red, red, red!  

#### `q18-raspberry-done`

_Plop, plop, plop! Every raspberry lands in the bowl._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 10  
> _(Count the last ten into the bowl.)_  
> _One, two, three, four, five, six, seven, eight, nine, ten!_  
> Check: `edu:count:raspberries:10`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted ten raspberries into the bowl._

_The floor is clean._  
_The Nubbin with the empty bowl eats one of yours._  
**Sergeant Grimble**: Good enough. Next!  

#### `q18-pineapple`

**Sergeant Grimble**: And now. The big one.  
_Under a cloth sits a huge, spiky pineapple. Wearing sunglasses!_  
**Sergeant Grimble**: The pineapple. Spiky. Heavy. A bit of a show-off.  
**Sergeant Grimble**: Don’t fight it. Make it your friend.  
**You**: How?  
**Sergeant Grimble**: Throw it gently to your Nubbin. Let it catch!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, ask): playing safely with others  
> _How do we throw something to a friend?_  
> - ✅ Gently, when they are looking → Correct! Gentle, and ready. Nobody gets bonked.
> - Hard, when they’re not looking → No! That could hurt. Gently, when they’re looking.
> _For grown-ups: Your child chose the safe, kind way to throw to a friend._


**Choices**

- **Just toss it.**
    - **Sergeant Grimble**: No fuss. I like it.
- **Take its sunglasses off first.**
    - **Sergeant Grimble**: Bold! It respects that.

#### `q18-catch`

_You toss the pineapple, nice and gently._  
_Your Nubbin runs, jumps and catches it. Oof!_  
_It holds it up like a prize._  
_The pineapple looks happy about it._  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): throwing and catching  
> _(Find a soft toy. Toss it gently to each other!)_  
> _Throw… catch! Throw… catch!_  
> Check: `edu:throw-catch:soft-toy`
> _For grown-ups: Your child played gentle throw-and-catch with a soft toy._


#### `q18-complete`

**Sergeant Grimble**: CLASS IS OVER!  
**Sergeant Grimble**: You can now handle fresh fruit. And throw it to friends.  
**Sergeant Grimble**: Between you and me? Forty years. Nobody ever waved fruit at me.  
**Sergeant Grimble**: I’ve been ready the whole time. So tiring!  
_He gives you his old brass whistle._  
**Sergeant Grimble**: Blow it, and your Nubbins look up. Throw, and they catch.  
**Sergeant Grimble**: Dried fruit is next week. Bring a hat.  

> **Lesson** (science, notice): dried fruit  
> **Sergeant Grimble**: Dried fruit is fruit with the water taken out.  
> **Sergeant Grimble**: A raisin is a dried grape!  
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that dried fruit has had its water taken out, like a raisin from a grape._

_Now your Nubbins can catch fruit you throw!_  

## Q19. The Grand Cup Race

- `q19-s1`: Sign up for the Grand Cup race in the meadow. · _Write your name in Mrs Thackery’s book._
- `q19-s2`: Win the Grand Cup race. · _Jump the matchboxes. Go round the sleeping Keeper. Don’t wake them!_

#### `q19-intro`

_A banner says: THE GRAND CUP RACE. EVERYONE WELCOME!_  
**Mrs Thackery**: Roll up! Three runners so far. We need one more!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.2, ask): adding one more  
> _Three runners, and you join. How many runners now?_  
> - ✅ Four → Yes! Three and one more makes four.
> - Three → Count again: Bunty, Piers, Algernon… and you! Four.
> _For grown-ups: Your child added one more to three to make four._

_The three runners stand at the start. One faces the wrong way._  
**The Hon. Bunty Fairweather-Flute**: I say. Is this the start or the end?  
**Piers Tumbledown-Hoop**: It’s the middle, Bunty. Everything is the middle.  
**Lord Algernon Spoon**: Nine years I’ve entered. I’ve never found the start line!  

**Choices**

- **Sign up!**
    - **Mrs Thackery**: Splendid! Write your name. Any spelling you like.
- **Turn Bunty round first.**
    - You turn Bunty round. Bunty says thank you… and turns back.

#### `q19-ready`

**Mrs Thackery**: Four runners! A record!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): the first letter of your name  
> **Mrs Thackery**: Write your name in the book!  
> _(What letter does your name start with? Draw it in the air.)_  
> Check: `edu:write:own-name:first-letter`
> _For grown-ups: Your child found and drew the first letter of their own name._

**Mrs Thackery**: Jump the matchboxes. Climb the low wall. Go round the sleeping Keeper.  
_At the end, a Keeper snores in a deckchair._  
_Nobody knows who put them there._  

#### `q19-race`

**Mrs Thackery**: Ready… steady… GO!  
_You run. Behind you, nobody else moves._  
**Mrs Thackery**: Bunty is checking which way is forwards!  
**Mrs Thackery**: Piers is tying his lace to Algernon’s lace!  
_Piers jumps the matchboxes. Way too high!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): positions: over and round  
> _We go OVER the matchboxes. What about the sleeping Keeper?_  
> - ✅ Round them → Yes! Round them, quietly, so they can sleep.
> - Over them → Not over! We go round them, so they can sleep.
> _For grown-ups: Your child used the position words over and round._

**Lord Algernon Spoon**: I found the start line! Hello, start line!  
_Bunty reaches the sleeping Keeper and taps their shoulder._  
**The Hon. Bunty Fairweather-Flute**: So sorry. Would you like to come? It’s fun!  

**Choices**

- **Keep a straight face and run.**
    - You keep a straight face. It’s SO hard!
- **Laugh all the way.**
    - You laugh so much you have to walk. You’re still way ahead!

#### `q19-win`

**Mrs Thackery**: The Keeper wins!  
_Far behind, Algernon waves goodbye to the start line._  
_Bunty comes last, arm in arm with the sleeping Keeper._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, notice): kindness matters more than winning  
> _Bunty came last. But Bunty stopped to help._  
> _Being kind matters more than winning._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that helping someone matters more than winning a race._

_The Keeper is still asleep!_  

**Choices**

- **Bunty should get a prize for helping!**
    - **Mrs Thackery**: There isn’t a prize for that.
    - **You**: There should be!
    - **Mrs Thackery**: …There is now. A smaller hat.
    - Bunty cries happy tears and puts it on the sleeping Keeper.
- **Say thank you nicely.**
    - You bow. Piers bows. Algernon bows to the start line.

#### `q19-complete`

_The runners come to say well done._  
_Two of them say it to a fence post first._  
**Piers Tumbledown-Hoop**: Jolly fast! We didn’t know people went that way!  
**The Hon. Bunty Fairweather-Flute**: Here’s your prize. A tall top hat!  
**The Hon. Bunty Fairweather-Flute**: And a monocle from me. A round glass for one eye.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.2, ask): circles  
> _What shape is the monocle?_  
> - ✅ A circle → Yes! A round circle, just for one eye.
> - A square → A square has four corners. The monocle is round: a circle!
> _For grown-ups: Your child named the round monocle as a circle._

_It only stays in if you lift one eyebrow._  
**Lord Algernon Spoon**: Same time next year? I’ll look for the start line tonight.  
_The sleeping Keeper wakes, sees your hat, and goes back to sleep._  

## Q20. The Pointy Tree

- `q20-s1`: Find and tap one of every kind of tree in your Grove. · _One tree for each fruit, and the palm by the sea._
- `q20-s2`: Take a photo of Mr Arbour’s board from the meadow. · _Use Photo mode. Zoom in on the far hill._

#### `q20-intro`

_By the orchard stands Mr Arbour, with a picture of a tree._  
**Mr Arbour**: Today we learn the trees of the Grove. From close up.  
**Mr Arbour**: Tree number one…  
**Mr Arbour**: Look! A larch!  
_The tree in the picture is tall, thin and pointy._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.2, ask): triangles  
> _The larch is tall and pointy. What shape is it like?_  
> - ✅ A triangle → Yes! Pointy on top, wide at the bottom. A triangle!
> - A circle → A circle is round. The larch is pointy, like a triangle!
> _For grown-ups: Your child matched the pointy tree to a triangle._

**You**: There aren’t any of those here.  
**Mr Arbour**: Correct. Tree number two…  
_He flips the board. It’s the same tree!_  
**Mr Arbour**: Look! Another larch!  
**Mr Arbour**: Your job: find every tree you DO have. Tap each one.  

**Choices**

- **Is tree three a larch?**
    - **Mr Arbour**: …Let’s not rush ahead.
- **Off I go!**
    - **Mr Arbour**: I shall wait here. With my larch.

#### `q20-trees`

_Run, swim, fly, power, stamina, sun, moon, heart._  
_And the palm tree by the sea._  
_Each tree rustles when you tap it. It feels noticed!_  

> **Lesson** (science, do): parts of a tree  
> _Trees have roots under the ground, a trunk and leaves._  
> _Most have branches too. The palm tree doesn’t!_  
> _(Point to the trunk. Now the leaves!)_  
> Check: `edu:tap:tree-part:trunk`
> _For grown-ups: Your child named the parts of a tree: roots, trunk, branches and leaves._

_There is no larch. You check twice._  

#### `q20-far`

**Mr Arbour**: Now the hard class. A tree from far away.  
_He picks up his board and walks off. And off. Over the hill._  
_A tiny Mr Arbour pops up on the far hill._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): near and far  
> _Mr Arbour is far away. Does he look big or small?_  
> - ✅ Small → Yes! Far-away things look small. Close things look big.
> - Big → Look again: he’s tiny now! Far-away things look small.
> _For grown-ups: Your child noticed that faraway things look small and close things look big._

**Mr Arbour**: Can you see it?  
_The board looks tiny. It shows… a larch, of course._  
**Mr Arbour**: Take a photo! I brought a sandwich!  

#### `q20-photo`

_Click! The photo is mostly sky._  
_And one small, clear larch._  
_Far away, Mr Arbour takes a big bite of sandwich._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, ask): waiting calmly  
> _Mr Arbour waited a long time. What helps when we wait?_  
> - ✅ Something calm, like humming or a snack → Yes! Humming, counting or a snack makes waiting easier.
> - Shouting “Hurry up!” → Shouting makes waiting harder. A calm hum helps.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about calm ways to wait._

_Munch._  

#### `q20-complete`

_Mr Arbour comes back, puffing._  
_He looks at your photo for a long time._  
**Mr Arbour**: Yes. That is a larch.  
**Mr Arbour**: Most people never look up at their trees. You tapped them all.  
**Mr Arbour**: The larch just got you looking. Here. Grow your own!  
_A small larch seed: a new tree for your Grove!_  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, ask): what a seed needs to grow  
> _What does a seed need to grow into a tree?_  
> - ✅ Soil, water and sunshine → Precisely. And a lot of patience.
> - A hat → Ha! No hat. Soil, water and sunshine.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named what a seed needs to grow into a plant._

**Mr Arbour**: And a new button for your camera. Very surprising.  
_A very big foot comes down from the sky…_  
_…and goes back up. Nobody mentions it._  

## Q21. Bear Comes Too

- `q21-s1`: Look for a clue by the pond. · _Search the reeds by the pond. Clues sparkle._
- `q21-s2`: Follow the little wild Nubbin up the hill. · _It nests in the long grass on the western hill. Walk slowly!_
- `q21-s3`: Bring Bear back to Mr. Tweed. · _He’s on his rock by the pond. Use Give._

#### `q21-intro`

_A man in a tweed jacket pats all his pockets._  
_He looks in his hat. He looks under a sleeping duck!_  
_This is Mr. Tweed. He hardly ever says a whole sentence._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Hnn.  
_He holds his hands apart. Then he hugs the empty air._  
**You**: Something is missing?  
**Mr. Tweed**: Bear.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): naming feelings  
> _Mr. Tweed has lost his bear. How does he feel?_  
> - ✅ Sad → Yes. Losing a special toy feels sad. We can help.
> - Happy → Look at his droopy face. He feels sad. We can help him.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named Mr. Tweed’s feeling when he lost his special toy._

_He makes two little ears with his fingers. A bear!_  
_Then he points down to the pond._  

**Choices**

- **I’ll help you look!**
    - **Mr. Tweed**: Mm!
    - He shakes your hand with both hands. For a long time.
- **What does the bear look like?**
    - He shows you: small, brown, one chewed ear.
    - And a very proud face. Somehow he shows that too.
- **Ask your Nubbin to sniff for it.**
    - Your Nubbin sniffs his sleeve. Achoo! Off it trots.
    - **Mr. Tweed**: Ah!

#### `q21-scarf`

_In the reeds, you find a tiny knitted scarf._  
_It’s bear-sized. And a bit damp._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.1.4, ask): describing words  
> _The scarf is damp. What does damp mean?_  
> - ✅ A little bit wet → Yes! Damp means a little bit wet. Like a towel after a bath.
> - Very hot → Hot is warm, warm, warm. Damp means a little bit wet.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned the describing word “damp”._

_Mr. Tweed hurries over and hugs it._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Ohh.  
_Then he sulks. His whole face folds up._  

#### `q21-sulk`

_Mr. Tweed sits on a rock and sulks._  
_Bottom lip out. Arms folded. A big sulk._  
_He peeks up the hill. Then away. Then up again._  
_A wild Nubbin is waddling uphill, holding something brown!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): positions: up and down  
> _Is the wild Nubbin going up the hill, or down?_  
> - ✅ Up → Yes! Up, up, up the hill. Let’s follow!
> - Down → Look: it’s climbing. Up the hill!
> _For grown-ups: Your child used the position words up and down._

**Mr. Tweed**: Hm!  
_He stands up… remembers he’s sulking… and sits down._  
_He waves you on. This is your job now._  

#### `q21-nest`

_At the top of the hill is a nest of grass._  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS2-2, notice): animals build nests  
> _Lots of animals make nests to sleep in._  
> _Birds build nests from grass, twigs and moss._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that many animals build nests, changing the place around them._

_A wild Nubbin is fast asleep, cuddling a small brown bear._  
_The bear has one chewed ear._  
_Shh. Tiptoe, tiptoe._  

**Choices**

- **Ease the bear out gently.**
    - The Nubbin sighs and lets go.
    - You leave it a fruit to cuddle instead.
- **Swap the bear for the little scarf.**
    - The Nubbin puts the scarf on. Very smart! A fair swap.
    - Mr. Tweed may have feelings about that.

#### `q21-carry`

_The bear is very light._  
_Your Nubbin carries it, very carefully._  
_Down by the pond, Mr. Tweed is still sulking._  
_Your Nubbin hides the bear behind its back._  
_The bear is wider than its back!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): wider and narrower  
> _The bear is wider than the Nubbin’s back. Is it hidden?_  
> - ✅ No, we can still see it! → Yes! Wider things poke out. Peekaboo, bear!
> - Yes, all hidden → Look: the bear sticks out on both sides. Wider things poke out!
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared widths to see if the bear could hide._

**You**: Ready? Surprise!  

#### `q21-complete`

_Mr. Tweed sees the bear. His face opens up like a deckchair._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Bear! Oh, Bear!  
_He hugs it. He checks one ear. Then the other._  
_He tucks it in his top pocket. Only its head peeks out._  
_Your Nubbin looks at the bear, wishing._  
_Mr. Tweed digs in his pockets… and his sock!_  
_He finds a teeny bear and puts it on your shoulder._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Bear.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): kindness goes round  
> _You helped Mr. Tweed. Then he was kind back._  
> _Kindness goes round and round!_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard how one kind act led to another._

_That night, he sets two plates. One very small._  

## Q22. The Armchair Ride

- `q22-s1`: Help Mr. Tweed tie the armchair on top of his cart. · _Use rope from your bag. Tap each corner of the chair._
- `q22-s2`: Clear the path before the cart gets there. · _Move the branch. Carry the sleepy Nubbin aside. Unhook the washing line._

#### `q22-shop`

_At the market, Mr. Tweed buys a big green armchair._  
_His hand cart is tiny. About the size of the cushion._  
_He lifts one end. Then the other end. Still too big!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): will it fit? big and small  
> _The chair is big. The cart is small._  
> _Will the chair fit inside the cart?_  
> - ✅ No, it’s too big → Right! Big things don’t fit in small spaces. So… on top!
> - Yes → Look: the chair is much bigger than the cart. It won’t fit inside!
> _For grown-ups: Your child worked out that a big thing won’t fit inside a small space._

**Mr. Tweed**: Hnn.  
_Then, an idea! He points at the cart top and mimes a knot._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Rope!  
_He looks at you. You look like someone with rope._  

**Choices**

- **Give him some rope.**
    - He bows. He ties a knot… round himself.
- **Is this a good idea?**
    - **Mr. Tweed**: Mm.
    - He nods firmly. You fetch rope.
- **We could just carry it.**
    - He looks at the long road. Then he shakes his head, kindly.

#### `q22-tied`

_The chair sits on the cart, tied at all four corners._  
_And once, by mistake, to a lamp post!_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): rhyming words  
> _Which word rhymes with rope?_  
> - ✅ Soap → Yes! Rope, soap, hope. They rhyme!
> - Chair → Chair rhymes with bear! Rope rhymes with soap.
> _For grown-ups: Your child found a word that rhymes with “rope”._

_You untie the lamp post. Mr. Tweed climbs into the chair._  
_He pushes the ground with a long stick. Off they roll!_  

#### `q22-road`

_The cart wobbles down the lane._  
_Mr. Tweed steers by leaning. He waves at the birds._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Ooh.  
_Ahead: a branch, a sleeping Nubbin, and a low washing line!_  
_Run ahead and clear the way!_  
_Now the lane goes downhill. The cart goes faster. Whee!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ask): rolling down slopes  
> _Why does the cart go faster now?_  
> - ✅ It’s going downhill → Yes! Things roll faster down a slope.
> - It’s hungry → Ha! Carts don’t eat. Going downhill makes things roll faster.
> _For grown-ups: Your child noticed that things roll faster down a slope._

_Mr. Tweed lifts his feet. As if that helps!_  

**Choices**

- **Run ahead!**
    - You run. Mr. Tweed hums a little driving song.
- **Send your Nubbin to wake the sleeper.**
    - Your Nubbin pokes it. It rolls over. Still asleep!

#### `q22-arrive`

_The cart stops at his gate. Bonk!_  
_His hat flies off. He catches it with his foot!_  
_Nothing fell off. He checks himself, the chair and the stick._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): position: beside  
> _Where did you put the sleepy Nubbin, to keep it safe?_  
> - ✅ Beside the path → Yes! Beside the path, out of the way. Still snoring.
> - In the middle of the path → The cart would bump it! We put it beside the path.
> _For grown-ups: Your child used the position word “beside”._

**Mr. Tweed**: Ha!  

#### `q22-complete`

_Mr. Tweed looks at the chair. Then at his little door._  
_The chair won’t fit through! He measures with his hands. Three times._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Hnn.  
_He pats the chair and pushes it to you. It’s yours!_  
_He pats the cart too. Yours as well._  
_Then he sits outside on a small kitchen chair, in the sun._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, notice): coping with disappointment  
> _Mr. Tweed was disappointed. That’s OK._  
> _Then he made a new plan: a small chair in the sun._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard how Mr. Tweed felt let down, then made a new plan._

_He pats the space beside him. There is no space._  
_You sit on the wall. You watch the lane together._  

## Q23. Pumpkin Head

- `q23-s1`: Calm the wandering pumpkin. · _Tap the pumpkin and choose Pet._
- `q23-s2`: Free the Nubbin with three gentle wiggles. · _Tap when the pumpkin glows. Tap, tap, tap. No pulling!_

#### `q23-patch`

_It’s harvest time. The pumpkins are big and orange._  

> **Lesson** (science, notice): how pumpkins grow  
> _Pumpkins grow on long, twisty vines, along the ground._  
> _Inside, they’re full of seeds!_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that pumpkins grow on vines and are full of seeds._

_One pumpkin is moving! Left. Right. Bump! “Mff.”_  
_Mr. Tweed knocks on it. Knock, knock._  
_The pumpkin knocks back!_  
**Mr. Tweed**: Oh!  
_Two little feet stick out. A Nubbin climbed in, and it’s stuck!_  
_Mr. Tweed grabs the stalk, ready to pull hard._  

**Choices**

- **Stop! Not like that.**
    - He freezes. He lets go of the stalk.
    - **Mr. Tweed**: Mm.
- **Watch which way it wiggles.**
    - Left, right, left. The Nubbin is trying to wiggle out!
- _(left out in this band; plays as `stop`)_

#### `q23-calm`

_You pat the pumpkin. Pat, pat._  
_It stops wandering and sits. Thump._  
_From inside comes a happy hum._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): gentle hands  
> _The Nubbin was stuck and scared. What helped?_  
> - ✅ Gentle pats and a calm voice → Yes! Gentle and calm helps someone feel safe.
> - Pulling hard → Pulling could hurt and scare it. Gentle and calm helps.
> _For grown-ups: Your child chose gentle pats and a calm voice to help a scared creature._

_Mr. Tweed listens with his ear. He nods._  

#### `q23-wiggle`

_Mr. Tweed holds up three fingers._  
_He mimes: wiggle, wiggle, wiggle… pop!_  
_You put both hands on the pumpkin._  
_Inside, the Nubbin goes still and listens._  
**Mr. Tweed**: One…  
_He waves his arms like a band leader. The Nubbin hums along._  
**You**: Gently. On the beat.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.4, do): count to 3  
> _(Wiggle together! Count each one.)_  
> _One… two… three!_  
> Check: `edu:count:wiggles:3`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted three gentle wiggles._


#### `q23-pop`

_On wiggle three… pop! Out slides the Nubbin._  
_Seeds fly everywhere!_  
_It is orange up to its elbows. It smells lovely._  

> **Lesson** (art, ask): colour mixing: orange  
> _What colour is the Nubbin now?_  
> - ✅ Orange → Yes! Orange. Red and yellow paint mixed together make orange.
> - Purple → Purple is like grapes. The Nubbin is orange, like the pumpkin!
> _For grown-ups: Your child named orange and heard that red and yellow mix to make it._

_It looks at the empty pumpkin. Its lip wobbles._  

#### `q23-complete`

_The Nubbin climbs straight back into the pumpkin. Head first!_  
_Mr. Tweed lifts it off and scoops it clean with a spoon._  
_He finds a butter knife in his sock. He cuts two eyeholes._  
_He pops it on the Nubbin, like a hat._  
_It fits! The Nubbin spins round, delighted._  
_Mr. Tweed tries one on too. Bonk! Into a fence post._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): bigger and smaller  
> _The pumpkin hat fits the Nubbin, but not Mr. Tweed. Whose head is bigger?_  
> - ✅ Mr. Tweed’s → Yes! His head is bigger, so the little hat is too tight.
> - The Nubbin’s → The Nubbin is small! Mr. Tweed’s head is bigger.
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared head sizes to see why one hat fitted and one didn’t._

**Mr. Tweed**: Mff.  
_It takes three tugs to get it off. He’s orange to the eyebrows!_  
_He gives you a smaller pumpkin hat with a curly stalk._  
_Some creatures just like a snug place to be._  

## Q24. Test Day

- `q24-s1`: Move your Nubbin’s paper three times, so Buttons can’t copy. · _When Buttons leans over, tap your Nubbin’s paper to move it._
- `q24-s2`: At break, help Buttons learn shapes. Then both Nubbins pass! · _Practise circles with Buttons on the chalkboard._

#### `q24-desks`

**Mrs Inkwell**: Test day! Shapes, colours, and counting to five!  
_Your Nubbin sits at one desk. Buttons, Mr. Tweed’s Nubbin, at the other._  
_Buttons wears a tiny tweed waistcoat._  
**Mrs Inkwell**: Grown-ups may stay. Quietly. I’m looking at you, Mr. Tweed.  
_He zips his lips and puts the key in his pocket._  
_Your Nubbin draws a perfect circle._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.2, ask): circles  
> _Your Nubbin drew a circle. Which of these is a circle?_  
> - ✅ A round plate → Yes! A plate is round, like a circle.
> - A square window → A window has four corners. A plate is round, like a circle.
> _For grown-ups: Your child picked out a circle from everyday shapes._

_Buttons draws nothing. Buttons leans… slowly… to the left._  

**Choices**

- **Help your Nubbin cover its paper.**
    - Your Nubbin curls an arm round its work. Buttons leans more. Creak!
- **Let Buttons look. Sharing is nice.**
    - **Mrs Inkwell**: Eyes on your own paper, please, Buttons.
    - Buttons sits up and tries to whistle. Nubbins can’t whistle.
- **Look at Mr. Tweed.**
    - He looks at the ceiling. As if he’s never met Buttons.

#### `q24-shuffled`

_Left. Right. Under the desk!_  
_Buttons follows the paper with its whole body…_  
_And falls off its chair. Bump!_  
_Mr. Tweed falls over too._  
**Mrs Inkwell**: Buttons! Are you all right?  
_Buttons is fine. But its paper is still empty._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, notice): doing your own work  
> **Mrs Inkwell**: In a test, we each do our own work.  
> **Mrs Inkwell**: If something is tricky, we can ask for help after.  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that in a test we each do our own work, and ask for help afterwards._


#### `q24-break`

_Buttons looks at its empty paper. Its ears droop._  
_Mr. Tweed’s ears droop too, somehow._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Hnn…  
**Mrs Inkwell**: Break time! You may help each other now. That’s learning.  
**You**: Right, Buttons. Circles first.  
_Buttons looks up at you. It has never drawn a circle._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.2, do): drawing a circle  
> _(Draw a circle in the air with your finger.)_  
> _Round and round, back to the start. A circle!_  
> Check: `edu:draw:shape:circle`
> _For grown-ups: Your child drew a circle in the air, going round and back to the start._


#### `q24-pass`

**Mrs Inkwell**: Two passes! Well done!  
**Mrs Inkwell**: Buttons, that’s the roundest circle I’ve ever seen. Nearly.  
_Buttons holds its paper up to Mr. Tweed._  
_Both arms. Very proud._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, ask): feeling proud  
> _How does Buttons feel?_  
> - ✅ Proud → Yes! Proud means happy about something you worked hard on.
> - Bored → Look at those arms up high! Buttons feels proud.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named the feeling of being proud after working hard._


#### `q24-complete`

_Mr. Tweed lifts Buttons up high and walks round the room._  
**Mrs Inkwell**: Mr. Tweed. Please put the Nubbin down.  
_He shakes everyone’s hand. Even Mrs Inkwell’s._  
**Mrs Inkwell**: These two learn better together.  
_Buttons and your Nubbin draw circles on each other’s papers._  

> **Lesson** (art, do): colour patterns  
> **Mrs Inkwell**: Let’s colour our circles. Red, blue, red, blue…  
> _(What comes next? Red!)_  
> Check: `edu:colour:circles:red-blue`
> _For grown-ups: Your child coloured circles in a red, blue pattern._

_Mr. Tweed holds up one finger. Hopeful._  
**Mrs Inkwell**: …One paper. Just one.  
_He squeezes into a tiny desk. He draws a circle, tongue out._  
_It’s a wobbly circle. Buttons claps anyway._  

## Q25. The High Ledge

- `q25-s1`: Help Mr. Tweed down from the high ledge. · _Talk him down step by step, or ask a Swim Nubbin to show him._
- `q25-s2`: Build a low diving board at the pond. · _Put the plank on the flat rock. Tap three times to hammer it._

#### `q25-ledge`

_Mr. Tweed is at the waterfall in a stripy swimsuit._  
_He points at the high ledge. He mimes a perfect dive._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Hup!  
_He climbs up, up, up. At the top, he looks down._  
_He freezes like a statue. A bird lands on his head._  
_Three little Nubbins squeeze past his ankles. Splash, splash, splash!_  
**Mr. Tweed**: Hnn.  
_He wiggles two fingers backwards. He wants to come down._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, ask): feeling scared is OK  
> _Mr. Tweed is scared. Is it OK to feel scared?_  
> - ✅ Yes, everyone feels scared sometimes → Yes. Everyone does. A friend nearby helps.
> - No → It is OK! Everyone feels scared sometimes. A friend nearby helps.
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that everyone feels scared sometimes, and a friend helps._


**Choices**

- **Talk him down, one step at a time.**
    - **You**: You’re OK! One foot back. Just one.
    - **Mr. Tweed**: Mm…
- **Ask a Swim Nubbin to show him.**
    - Your Nubbin looks at the water. Then at you. Yes!

#### `q25-down`

_Step by step, or splash by splash, he gets down._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): high and low  
> _The ledge is high. The water is…_  
> - ✅ Low → Yes! High up top, low down below.
> - Higher → The water is down at the bottom. It is low!
> _For grown-ups: Your child used the words high and low._

_The last bit is mostly sliding. Wheee!_  
_He lands in the shallow water. Splosh!_  
_The bird is still on his head._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Mm!  

#### `q25-board`

_Mr. Tweed dries off with a tiny towel._  
_He looks at the high ledge. Then at the low rock by the pond._  
_He mimes: saw, hammer, bounce. A low diving board!_  

> **Lesson** (health, notice): water safety  
> _Before jumping in, a grown-up checks the water._  
> _Is it deep enough? Is it safe? Then splash!_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that a grown-up checks the water before anyone jumps in._

**Mr. Tweed**: Small.  
_Your Nubbin bounces on the spot. It wants to go first._  
_Mr. Tweed pulls a plank out of his towel!_  

#### `q25-complete`

_The diving board is low and bouncy. A bit crooked. Perfect!_  
_Your Nubbin goes first. Bounce, bounce, SPLASH!_  
_Mr. Tweed walks to the end. He looks down. He freezes._  
_The water is just below his toes._  
_You wait. Your Nubbin waits. The bird comes back._  
_He holds his nose, shuts his eyes… and steps off. Plop!_  
**Mr. Tweed**: Ha!  
_He climbs out and does it again, and again, and again!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.A.1, do): count to 10  
> _(Count his jumps! Clap for each one.)_  
> _One, two, three… all the way to ten!_  
> Check: `edu:count:jumps:10`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted ten jumps, clapping for each one._


## Q26. The Bench Sandwich

- `q26-s1`: Hand Mr. Tweed the right things, in the right order. · _Watch his hands. Sawing means bread. Shaking means pepper._
- `q26-s2`: Spread the picnic blanket and share the sandwich with every Nubbin. · _Put the blanket on the grass, then feed each Nubbin._

#### `q26-bench`

_Mr. Tweed sits on his bench by the pond._  
_He opens his coat. Out comes lunch!_  

> **Lesson** (health, notice): washing hands before eating  
> _Wait! Before lunch, we wash our hands._  
> _(Pretend to wash: soap, rub, rinse, dry!)_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that we wash our hands before we eat._

_Bread from his sleeve. Lettuce from his pocket._  
_Jam from near his knee. A tomato from under his hat!_  
_Butter from his top pocket. Of course._  
_He spreads jam… on the lettuce._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Hm?  
_Something has gone wrong. He doesn’t know what._  

**Choices**

- **Offer to pass him things.**
    - He nods and holds out his hand, like a doctor.
- **Try the jam lettuce.**
    - Crunchy, sweet… and wrong!
    - Mr. Tweed nods. He knew it.
- **Let your Nubbin have the jam.**
    - Your Nubbin puts its whole face in the jar.
    - Mr. Tweed finds another jar near his other knee.

#### `q26-sandwich`

_Bread. Butter. Lettuce. Tomato. Pepper. Bread on top._  
_Pat, pat. A sandwich!_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.K.3, ask): first, next, last  
> _What goes on first, and what goes on last?_  
> - ✅ Bread first, bread last → Yes! Bread first, fillings next, bread last.
> - Pepper first → Pepper would fall off! Bread first, fillings next, bread last.
> _For grown-ups: Your child put the steps of making a sandwich in order._

_Mr. Tweed holds it up, like treasure._  
_A duck lands on the bench. It looks at the sandwich._  
_The duck flies off with the jam lettuce. Fair enough._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Ahh.  

#### `q26-share`

_He’s about to bite. Then he sees: you have no sandwich._  
_Your Nubbin has none. Wild Nubbins behind the bench have none._  
_Mr. Tweed sighs, all the way from his boots._  
_He takes out a ruler. He cuts the sandwich into neat squares._  

> **Lesson** (maths, 1.G.A.3, ask): quarters  
> _One cut down, one cut across. How many squares?_  
> _(Draw it in the air: down… across!)_  
> - ✅ Four → Yes! Four squares the same size. Each one is a quarter.
> - Two → Let’s count: one, two at the top. Three, four at the bottom. Four!
> _For grown-ups: Your child found that two cuts make four equal squares, called quarters._

**Mr. Tweed**: Picnic.  
_He pulls a checked blanket out of his coat. It’s bigger than the coat!_  

#### `q26-complete`

_Everyone gets a square._  
_Mr. Tweed takes the last one. Mostly crust. He loves it._  
_He has never shared his lunch before._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): sharing fairly  
> _Everyone got a square. Was that fair?_  
> - ✅ Yes! Everyone got some → Yes. Sharing so everyone gets some is fair.
> - No → Everyone got one square each. That’s fair sharing!
> _For grown-ups: Your child talked about why sharing so everyone gets some is fair._

_The Nubbins snuggle in a sleepy pile on the blanket._  
_Mr. Tweed goes to fold it. Then he stops._  
_He leaves it there, for the sleeping Nubbins._  
_The blanket is yours now. The Nubbins decided._  

## Q27. The Smudged Painting

- `q27-s1`: Bring three Nubbins to the hall and line them up like the painting. · _Mr. Tweed’s chalk marks show where each one sits._
- `q27-s2`: Take a photo that matches the painting. · _Use the self-timer. Sit in the empty chair._
- `q27-s3`: Hang the frame back up before the Warden comes. · _Tap the hook to hang it. Tap again to make it straight._

#### `q27-hall`

_The Keepers’ Hall smells of polish._  
_On the wall: a painting of two old Keepers and three Nubbins._  
**Warden Pryce**: Mr. Tweed is cleaning the painting. Touch only the frame!  
_The Warden leaves. Mr. Tweed dabs it. His nose twitches._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Ah… ah… ACHOO!  

> **Lesson** (health, ask): catching sneezes  
> _When we sneeze, what should we do?_  
> - ✅ Sneeze into a tissue or my elbow → Yes! That keeps germs in. Bless you!
> - Sneeze on the painting → Oops! Into a tissue or your elbow keeps germs in.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned to sneeze into a tissue or elbow to keep germs in._

_The wet cloth flies onto the painting. Splat!_  
_Now the two Keepers are one wide Keeper. With four ears!_  
_He tries to fix it. Now there are glasses too._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Oh.  

**Choices**

- **We can fix this. Probably.**
    - He grabs your hand. Thank you!
- **We should tell the Warden.**
    - He nods slowly. Then he points: try first, then tell.

#### `q27-lined`

_Three Nubbins in a row. Sort of._  
_One faces the wrong way. One is asleep._  
_Mr. Tweed draws a plan in chalk: two chairs, three Nubbins._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.2, ask): adding within 5  
> _Two Keepers and three Nubbins. How many in the picture?_  
> - ✅ Five → Yes! Two and three make five.
> - Four → Count on your fingers: two… then three more. One, two, three, four, five!
> _For grown-ups: Your child added two Keepers and three Nubbins to make five._

_And one camera. Yours!_  

#### `q27-pose`

_Mr. Tweed hangs a dark curtain. He sets out two chairs._  
_He sticks on a grey moustache. He gives you half!_  
_He sits very still, looking far away. Very serious._  
_The Nubbins copy him. One falls asleep._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Shh.  
**You**: Everybody, hold still…  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): body control: keeping still  
> _(Let’s practise! Freeze like a statue.)_  
> _Hold it… hold it… now wiggle!_  
> Check: `edu:freeze:statue`
> _For grown-ups: Your child practised freezing like a statue and then relaxing._


#### `q27-shot`

_Click!_  
_The photo looks serious, grand, and nearly right._  

> **Lesson** (art, notice): paintings and photos  
> _A painting is made with brushes and paint._  
> _A photo is made with a camera. Click!_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard the difference between a painting and a photo._

_One Nubbin is yawning._  
_But if you squint, so was one in the painting!_  

#### `q27-swap`

_Footsteps! The Warden is early!_  
_Mr. Tweed hides the smudged painting up his jacket._  
_You slide the photo in the frame. It’s a bit too small._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.2, ask): rectangles  
> _What shape is the frame?_  
> - ✅ A rectangle → Yes! Four sides, four corners. A rectangle.
> - A circle → A circle is round. The frame has four corners: a rectangle!
> _For grown-ups: Your child named the frame’s shape as a rectangle._

_He stuffs the gap with his hankie._  
_He hangs it. It’s crooked. He fixes it. Now the other side drops!_  
**Mr. Tweed**: Quick!  
_The door handle turns… slowly…_  

#### `q27-complete`

_The Warden looks at the picture. For a long, long time._  
**Warden Pryce**: It looks… brighter. And that Keeper has your chin.  
_Mr. Tweed looks at the ceiling. Half a moustache is still on his lip._  
_Behind his back, he gives you a pile of empty frames._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, ask): owning up to accidents  
> _Something went wrong by accident. What is the best thing to do?_  
> - ✅ Tell the truth → Yes. Accidents happen. Telling the truth helps fix them.
> - Hide it → Hiding feels worse inside. Telling the truth helps fix it.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about telling the truth after an accident._


**Choices**

- **Tell her: it’s a photo. There was a sneeze.**
    - **Warden Pryce**: A sneeze. Of course.
    - **Warden Pryce**: You know what? I like this one better. It stays.
    - Mr. Tweed gives her the old painting, rolled up.
- **Say nothing. Just smile.**
    - **Warden Pryce**: Well. Good work, both of you.
    - The door closes. Mr. Tweed breathes out at last.

## Q28. The Yellow Room

- `q28-s1`: Carry every Nubbin, and anything precious, out of the cottage. · _Three Nubbins are napping inside. His bear is on the shelf._
- `q28-s2`: Stand far back, behind the hedge. · _Run to the far side of the hedge before the fizz runs out._

#### `q28-plan`

_Mr. Tweed’s room is the colour of old porridge._  
_He has a tin of yellow paint and a tiny brush._  
_He paints one stroke. He looks at four big walls._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Hnn.  
_He picks up a firework. A big idea!_  

> **Lesson** (health, notice): fireworks are for grown-ups  
> _Fireworks are only for grown-ups._  
> _Even grown-ups stand far, far back._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that fireworks are only for grown-ups, and everyone stands far back._

_He mimes: firework in the paint… BOOM! Paint everywhere!_  
**Mr. Tweed**: Boom.  
_Your Nubbin hides behind your legs._  

**Choices**

- **Absolutely not!**
    - He nods kindly… and keeps unwrapping.
    - But first, he mimes carrying Nubbins outside. Safety first!
- **OK. But everyone goes outside first.**
    - He shakes your hand. He wants to keep his eyebrows too.
- _(left out in this band; plays as `outside`)_

#### `q28-cleared`

_Three sleepy Nubbins on the grass. One has a yellow stripe._  
_The bear sits on the gate post, watching._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.2, ask): adding one more  
> _Three Nubbins, and one bear. How many are safe outside?_  
> - ✅ Four → Yes! Three and one more makes four. All safe!
> - Three → Don’t forget the bear! Three and one more makes four.
> _For grown-ups: Your child added one bear to three Nubbins to make four._

_The room is empty. Just Mr. Tweed, the paint and the firework._  
_He tucks his trousers into his socks. Safety clothes!_  

#### `q28-fuse`

_He puts the firework in the tin. He lights it. Fizz!_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.1.4, ask): sound words  
> _Fizz! That word sounds like the noise. Which is a sound word too?_  
> - ✅ Boom → Yes! Boom, fizz, splash. Words that sound like noises!
> - Chair → A chair doesn’t make a noise. Boom is a sound word!
> _For grown-ups: Your child picked out a sound word like “fizz”._

_He watches it fizz. And fizz._  
_You wave through the window. He waves back._  
_You point at the fizz. He looks at it. Then at the door._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Oh!  
_He runs. You run. Your Nubbin is already behind the hedge!_  

#### `q28-complete`

_FWUMP! Every window turns yellow._  
_A little yellow cloud puffs from the chimney._  
_You peek inside. Walls, ceiling, clock: all yellow!_  

> **Lesson** (art, ask): naming colours  
> _What colour is the room now?_  
> - ✅ Yellow → Yes! Yellow, like the sun. (Can you see something yellow near you?)
> - Green → Not green! Yellow, like the sun and bananas.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named yellow and found other yellow things._

_One porridge square is left, where a picture hung._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Ha!  
_He gives you a big, wide brush for one big swish._  
_Then he shakes his head at the firework. No more booms._  
_On Saturday, half the lane comes for tea in the yellow room._  
_Mr. Tweed beams in the doorway, holding the teapot._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, ask): mixed feelings  
> _Lots of guests! How does Mr. Tweed feel?_  
> - ✅ Happy, and a bit shy → Yes! We can feel two things at once. Happy and shy.
> - Cross → Look at his big smile. Happy, and a little bit shy.
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that we can feel happy and shy at the same time._


## Q29. Mind the Egg

- `q29-s1`: Put Odette’s egg down somewhere flat. · _Find flat ground near your cottage. Not on the slope!_
- `q29-s2`: Catch the rolling egg before it reaches the stream. · _Get in front of it and wait. Mr. Tweed’s hat can catch it._
- `q29-s3`: Give the egg back to Odette at sunset. · _Odette waits at your gate. Carry the egg and use Give._

#### `q29-handover`

**Odette Pennant**: Hello! I’m Odette, from over the hill.  
**Odette Pennant**: Could you mind my egg this afternoon?  
_She gives you a speckled egg. It’s warm._  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-LS1-2, notice): parents keep eggs warm  
> _Bird mums and dads sit on their eggs to keep them warm._  
> _Warm eggs can hatch into babies!_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that bird parents sit on eggs to keep them warm._

**Odette Pennant**: It likes to roll. Keep it on something flat. Back by sunset!  
_Off she goes. The egg sits still… then wobbles._  
_Mr. Tweed leans over the fence. He mimes rocking a baby._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Hm.  

**Choices**

- **I’ll take great care of it.**
    - Mr. Tweed salutes the egg. The egg wobbles.
- **Mr. Tweed, will you help?**
    - He takes off his hat and holds it like a nest. Ready!

#### `q29-roll`

_You put the egg down. It sits._  
_Mr. Tweed lies flat to check. Is the ground level? Yes!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ask): rolling on slopes and flat ground  
> _Where does an egg roll more: on a slope, or on flat ground?_  
> - ✅ On a slope → Yes! Round things roll down slopes. Flat ground keeps them still.
> - On flat ground → Flat ground keeps it still. On a slope, it rolls away!
> _For grown-ups: Your child worked out that round things roll more on a slope than on flat ground._

_Thumbs up!_  
_Then a Nubbin sneezes. ACHOO! And the egg rolls away!_  

#### `q29-chase`

_The egg rolls down the path. Faster and faster!_  
_It bumps a post. It goes left. Over the bridge!_  
_Mr. Tweed jumps the fence. Mostly._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Egg!  
_Past the dojo. Three Nubbins bow as it rolls by._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.K.3, do): first, next, last  
> _(Say where the egg went, in order.)_  
> _First the post. Next the bridge. Last the dojo!_  
> Check: `edu:order:egg-route:post-bridge-dojo`
> _For grown-ups: Your child retold the egg’s journey in order: first, next, last._

_Mr. Tweed takes a shortcut through a hedge. He comes out ahead!_  
_He is wearing most of the hedge._  

#### `q29-caught`

_You dive. Mr. Tweed dives._  
_The egg bounces off your hands… off his head…_  
_…and lands in his hat. Plop!_  
**Mr. Tweed**: Ha!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): teamwork  
> _You and Mr. Tweed did it together._  
> _That’s teamwork!_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard how working together caught the egg._


#### `q29-afternoon`

_All afternoon, nobody puts the egg down._  
_Mr. Tweed knits it a woolly hat. It’s lumpy. The egg likes it._  
_You put a blanket in a fruit box, and add wheels._  
_A stone at each end stops it rolling._
