_The egg tries the box. It stays!_  
_The sun goes down. Odette comes back over the hill._  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-ESS1-1, ask): sunset and evening  
> _The sun is going down. What time of day is it?_  
> - ✅ Evening → Yes! The sun goes down in the evening. Soon it’s night.
> - Morning → In the morning the sun comes up. Going down means evening.
> _For grown-ups: Your child connected the sun going down with evening._


#### `q29-complete`

**Odette Pennant**: There it is! Safe! And wearing a hat!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, ask): the letter O  
> _Odette starts with the letter O._  
> _Which letter is round, like an egg?_  
> - ✅ O → Yes! O is round, like an egg. O for Odette!
> - T → T is straight lines. O is round, like an egg!
> _For grown-ups: Your child found the letter O at the start of Odette’s name._

_Mr. Tweed bows. His letter is knitted on it, upside down._  
**Odette Pennant**: Keep the box. Two eggs, side by side, on wheels? That’s a pram!  
_Mr. Tweed holds the egg a moment longer. Pat, pat. He lets go._  
**Odette Pennant**: So. Was it good?  

**Choices**

- **It went for a little roll.**
    - **Odette Pennant**: They always do! That’s why I asked a good Keeper.
- **It was very good!**
    - Behind you, Mr. Tweed picks a twig out of his hair.
    - Odette sees. She smiles and says nothing.

## Q30. The Three-Wheeler

- `q30-s1`: Meet Rufus at the start line and enter the race in your hand cart. · _Ride your cart to the ridge track and talk to Rufus._
- `q30-s2`: Win the race. Pass the Bluebell so it tips over three times. · _Catch up on a bend, and Mr. Tweed waves. Three times!_

#### `q30-meet`

_A bright blue cart rattles up the lane. It has three wheels._  
**Rufus Crankshaw**: Hello! I’m Rufus. This is the Bluebell. Fast!  
_Mr. Tweed sits in your hand cart. It has four wheels._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.C.6, ask): more and fewer  
> _The Bluebell has three wheels. Your cart has four._  
> _Which has more wheels?_  
> - ✅ My cart → Yes! Four is more than three.
> - The Bluebell → Let’s count. Bluebell: three. Your cart: four. Four is more!
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared three wheels and four wheels._

_He looks at the Bluebell’s one front wheel. He smiles a tiny smile._  
**Rufus Crankshaw**: One lap of the ridge. Ready?  
_Mr. Tweed holds up three fingers. Then he tips his hand over._  
**Mr. Tweed**: Three.  

**Choices**

- **You’re on!**
    - **Rufus Crankshaw**: Splendid! Mind your four wheels, show-off!
- **Is the Bluebell wobbly?**
    - **Rufus Crankshaw**: Steady as a table!
    - Behind him, the Bluebell tips over. All by itself.
    - **Rufus Crankshaw**: …It does that.

#### `q30-grid`

_Two carts at the start line._  
_Rufus says “Brrm!” very loudly._  
_Mr. Tweed polishes your cart with his sleeve._  
_Then he polishes the Bluebell too. To be fair._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): fair play  
> _Mr. Tweed polished both carts. Why?_  
> - ✅ To be fair to everyone → Yes! Fair means everyone gets the same chance.
> - Because he likes polishing → Maybe! But mostly to be fair. Everyone gets the same chance.
> _For grown-ups: Your child talked about why treating both sides the same is fair._


#### `q30-race`

_The flag drops! Rufus zooms off, waving._  
_Each time you catch up, Mr. Tweed waves at Rufus._  
_Rufus waves back. The Bluebell leans… and tips over. Bump!_  

> **Lesson** (science, do): a wide base is steady  
> _(Stand with your feet close together. Now wide apart.)_  
> _Which way is steadier? Wide feet!_  
> _Wide is steady. Narrow tips more easily._  
> Check: `edu:stand:feet:wide-narrow`
> _For grown-ups: Your child felt that standing with feet wide apart is steadier than feet together._

**Rufus Crankshaw**: Don’t worry! It does that!  
_He puts it back up with one hand. Off again!_  
**Mr. Tweed**: Ha!  

**Choices**

- **Ask Mr. Tweed to stop waving. Win it fairly.**
    - Mr. Tweed sits on his hands.
    - Next bend, Rufus waves anyway. Tip!
    - **Rufus Crankshaw**: Don’t worry! I just like waving!
    - Mr. Tweed looks very relieved. It wasn’t his fault.
- **Let him wave. It’s friendly.**
    - He waves so warmly that a Nubbin waves back… and tips over too!

#### `q30-complete`

_You cross the line first! The Bluebell slides in second, on its side._  
**Rufus Crankshaw**: What a race! Three tips!  
_Mr. Tweed shakes his hand. The parked Bluebell tips over again._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.2, ask): adding one more  
> _Three tips in the race, and one more after. How many tips?_  
> - ✅ Four → Yes! Three and one more makes four.
> - Three → Don’t forget the one at the end! Three and one more is four.
> _For grown-ups: Your child added one more tip to three to make four._

**Rufus Crankshaw**: I have a little old one at home. Your Nubbins can have it!  
_Next morning, a little blue cart sits in your garden._  
_A Nubbin bumps it. It tips. The Nubbin is thrilled!_  

## Q46. Not the Cabbages!

- `q46-s1`: Find the 8 cabbages. Help fix the wheel. · _Cabbages roll downhill. Look by the stream and under the bushes._
- `q46-s2`: Shake the big tree. Then fix the cart. · _Tap the tree until nothing else falls. Planks and rope are in the shed._
- `q46-s3`: Build a cart that cannot tip over. · _Make it low and wide. Tuk’s drawing is in your journal._

#### `q46-crash`

_Creak, creak! A cart comes up the path._  
_It is full of round, green cabbages._  
**The cabbage merchant**: Hello! Crunchy cabbages! Lovely cabbages! Cabbages for everyone!  
_Here comes a Nubbin. It is racing. Zoom!_  
_It does not look where it is going. Bump!_  
_The cart tips over. Wobble, wobble, CRASH!_  
**The cabbage merchant**: Oh no! Not the cabbages! Not again!  
_The cabbages roll away down the hill. Roll, roll, roll!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): round things roll  
> **The cabbage merchant**: Which one rolls away: a round cabbage or a square box?  
> _(Point to the round cabbages.)_  
> - ✅ The round cabbage → Yes! Round things roll. Boxes sit still.
> - The square box → A box has flat sides. It sits still. The round cabbage rolls away. Round things roll!
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared shapes and found that round things roll and flat-sided things stay put._

**The cabbage merchant**: And the wheel came off. Oh dear.  

**Choices**

- **Say sorry for your Nubbin**
    - **You**: Sorry! My Nubbin is still learning to look.
    - **The cabbage merchant**: Thank you. A sorry helps a lot.
- **Offer to buy the bumpy ones**
    - **The cabbage merchant**: Bumpy cabbages are still yummy cabbages!
- **Point to the race track sign**
    - **You**: Look. This is the race track.
    - **The cabbage merchant**: Oh! There is a sign. I am standing on it.

#### `q46-crash-done`

_You find the cabbages by the stream and under the bushes._  
_You put the wheel back on. Click!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 8  
> **The cabbage merchant**: Let’s count them back into the cart.  
> _(Count together on your fingers, one to eight.)_  
> **The cabbage merchant**: One, two, three, four, five, six, seven, eight!  
> Check: `edu:count:cabbages:8`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted eight cabbages, one for each number._

**The cabbage merchant**: Every single one! A bit muddy. That’s fine!  
**The cabbage merchant**: Thank you for helping me.  

#### `q46-fruit`

_The man parks his cart under a big tree._  
**The cabbage merchant**: Nice and shady. Nothing can go wrong here.  
_The wind blows. Swish, swish, go the leaves._  
_Up high, a big fruit wobbles on its stalk._  
**The cabbage merchant**: Is that tree looking at me?  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): things fall down  
> **The cabbage merchant**: If that fruit falls, which way will it go?  
> - ✅ Down → Yes! Things fall down to the ground.
> - Up → Let’s drop a leaf and watch. Down it goes! Things always fall down.
> _For grown-ups: Your child predicted that things fall down to the ground._

_The fruit drops. Down, down, down. SPLAT!_  
_It lands on the cart. The cart folds in half._  
**The cabbage merchant**: Oh dear. I need planks and rope, please.  
**The cabbage merchant**: And someone to shake that tree first!  

#### `q46-fruit-done`

**The cabbage merchant**: Good as new! Better than new!  
**The cabbage merchant**: Now I will park in the middle of the meadow.  
_High up, a bird looks down. Hmm._  
_There is one big fruit. There are two Nubbins._  
_Both Nubbins want the fruit._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): sharing  
> _What can the Nubbins do?_  
> _(Wait for an answer.)_  
> - ✅ Share it → Yes! Half for you, half for me. Sharing is kind.
> - Grab it → Grabbing makes someone sad. Let’s share it, half each. That feels better!
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about how two friends can share one thing._

_They share it. Munch, munch. Everyone is happy._  

#### `q46-glider`

_Look up! A boy flies by on a wooden glider. Whoosh!_  
**Pemba**: Hi! Hello! Is that a cabbage cart? I love cabbages!  
_He waves. The glider dips. Uh-oh._  
_Pemba lands. He bounces into the cart. CRASH!_  
**The cabbage merchant**: Oh, my poor leafy darlings! Not again!  
**Pemba**: Oh no. That was me. I’m Pemba. I’m so sorry.  
**Pemba**: Can I help you fix it?  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): saying sorry  
> _Pemba said sorry. Then he asked to help._  
> _That is a really good sorry._  
> _(Ask: when did you last say sorry?)_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard what a good sorry sounds like: say sorry, then offer to help._

**Tuk**: Hi. I’m Tuk. I make plans. With drawings!  
**The cabbage merchant**: Three crashes today! I need a cart that cannot tip.  
**Tuk**: Then let’s build one!  

**Choices**

- **Make it wide**
    - **Tuk**: Wide, like a duck sitting down. Ducks don’t tip!
- **Make it low**
    - **Tuk**: Low and heavy at the bottom. Hard to knock over!
- **Give it a bell**
    - **The cabbage merchant**: A bell! Ding-ding! They will hear me coming!
    - **Tuk**: And we’ll make it low and wide too.

#### `q46-glider-done`

_You build the new cart with Tuk._  
_It sits low and wide, like a duck sitting down._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): low, wide things are steady  
> **Tuk**: Which cart is harder to tip over?  
> _(Hold up a hand tall and thin. Now lay it flat.)_  
> - ✅ Low and wide → Yes! Low and wide stays put. Like a duck.
> - Tall and thin → Tall and thin wobbles and tips. Low and wide stays put!
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that a low, wide base is steadier than a tall, thin one._

**Pemba**: Can I test it? Please, please, please?  
**Tuk**: Go on, then. Push it!  

#### `q46-complete`

_Pemba runs at the new cart. Run, run, JUMP!_  
_Boing! He bounces off. The cart does not move._  
**The cabbage merchant**: It stays up! It stays up! Hooray!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds  
> **The cabbage merchant**: Cabbage starts with C. C-c-cabbage!  
> **The cabbage merchant**: What else starts like that?  
> - ✅ Cart → Yes! C-c-cart! Cabbage and cart!
> - Pemba → Pemba starts with P. P-p-Pemba! Cart starts like cabbage. C-c-cart!
> _For grown-ups: Your child listened for the first sound in “cabbage” and “cart”._

**Pemba**: Do you get sad when your cabbages fall?  
**The cabbage merchant**: Yes. But someone always stops to help.  
**The cabbage merchant**: That is how I make new friends.  
**The cabbage merchant**: Please keep my old cart. Grow cabbages in it!  
**Pemba**: And do the cry! Not the cabbages!  

## Q47. The Crystal Tunnel

- `q47-s1`: Follow the singers into the tunnel. · _The tunnel is behind the waterfall. Bring a Nubbin friend._
- `q47-s2`: Find the way out. Keep your Nubbin beside you. · _If your Nubbin goes too far, the light goes out. Call it back._

#### `q47-nomads`

_A painted wagon comes along. Jingle, jingle!_  
**Wandering Juno**: Hello, friends! I’m Juno. We go where the wind goes.  
**Wandering Juno**: Today the wind goes under that hill!  
**Tuk**: But there’s a path over the hill.  
**Wandering Juno**: The path has a grumpy goat on it.  
**Wandering Juno**: ♪ Under the hill, under the hill,  
**Wandering Juno**: ♪ The rocks are cold and the bats keep still.  
**Wandering Juno**: ♪ Doo-be-doo, the moss is blue,  
**Wandering Juno**: ♪ Under the hill with you!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): rhymes  
> **Wandering Juno**: Blue and you sound the same at the end.  
> **Wandering Juno**: What else rhymes with blue?  
> - ✅ Doo → Yes! Blue, you, doo. They rhyme!
> - Hat → Hat and blue sound different. Blue, doo! Those rhyme. Doo-be-doo!
> _For grown-ups: Your child listened for rhyming words in a silly song._

_Pemba hums it. Your Nubbin hums it. Hum, hum!_  

**Choices**

- **Sing the doo-be-doo**
    - **Wandering Juno**: What a good singer! The moss loves it.
- **Ask how long the tunnel is**
    - **Wandering Juno**: Long enough for fun. Short enough to get out.
    - **Tuk**: I hope so!
- **Ask about the goat**
    - **Wandering Juno**: He nibbled my lute once. Nibble, nibble!

#### `q47-nomads-done`

_In you go. The tunnel gets dark._  
_Tuk’s torch flickers. Flicker, flicker. Then it goes out._  
**Tuk**: Oh no. Now I can’t see a thing!  

> **Lesson** (science, 1-PS4-2, ask): we need light to see  
> **Tuk**: Why can’t we see anything?  
> - ✅ There’s no light → Yes. We need light to see. No light, no seeing!
> - Our eyes are shut → Open your eyes wide. Still dark! We need light to see. The torch went out.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that we need light to see things._

**Wandering Juno**: Hmm. This part of the song isn’t written yet.  

#### `q47-crystals`

_It is very dark. Drip, drip, goes the water._  
_Flap, flap! Some bats fly by._  
**Nalu**: Everyone stay close.  
_Your Nubbin snuggles next to you. The wall glows green!_  
_Step apart. The glow goes out. Step together. It glows!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, do): position: beside  
> **Nalu**: Keep your Nubbin beside you. Right next to you.  
> _(Tap your Nubbin. Move it beside you.)_  
> _Beside you, it glows!_  
> Check: `edu:place:nubbin:beside`
> _For grown-ups: Your child practised the position word “beside”._

**Nalu**: The crystals glow when friends stay close!  
**Tuk**: Um. I have to tell you something. I’m scared of the dark.  
**Wandering Juno**: That was brave to say.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, ask): what helps when we are scared  
> **Tuk**: What can help me feel less scared?  
> - ✅ Hold a friend’s hand → Oh. That does help. Thank you.
> - Run away fast → Running in the dark could make you bump. Holding hands helps more. Here, take mine.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named a feeling (scared) and something that helps (a friend’s hand)._


**Choices**

- **Pick up your Nubbin**
    - You hug your Nubbin close. The whole wall lights up!
- **Hold its paw**
    - Your Nubbin holds your finger. The crystals glow green.
- **Tuk holds Pemba’s hand**
    - **Tuk**: Okay. Just this once.
    - **Pemba**: I like holding hands!

#### `q47-crystals-done`

_Look! A crack of light. Squeeze through!_  
_You come out into the warm air. Blink, blink._  
**Wandering Juno**: See? We made it out!  
**Tuk**: Bye, cave. Bye, bats.  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS3-1, notice): animal homes: bats  
> **Wandering Juno**: Lots of bats live in caves like that.  
> **Wandering Juno**: They sleep in the day, hanging upside down.  
> **Wandering Juno**: At night, they wake up and fly out.  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that many bats live in caves and come out at night._


#### `q47-complete`

_The singers wave goodbye. Doo-be-doo!_  
**Nalu**: Here. Some little crystals for you.  
_They glow when two friends sit close._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5  
> **Nalu**: How many crystals? Let’s count.  
> _(Point and count together, one to five.)_  
> **Nalu**: One, two, three, four, five crystals!  
> Check: `edu:count:crystals:5`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted five crystals, touching each one once._

**Tuk**: And now there’s a cave path in your Grove.  
**Pemba**: You hummed the whole way, Tuk!  
**Tuk**: I hummed to be brave.  
**Wandering Juno**: The dark is not so scary with a friend!  

## Q48. The Great Divide

- `q48-s1`: Lead both groups down the canyon. No fighting! · _Walk in the middle. Stop for a rest when they get grumpy._
- `q48-s2`: Don’t laugh! Keep a straight face for Pemba’s story. · _Tap when the giggle meter is green. Don’t look at your Nubbin!_

#### `q48-canyon`

_Two groups stand at the top of a big canyon._  
_They frown at each other. Hmph!_  
**Elder Maud**: We are the Hollins. We go first!  
**Chief Brack**: We are the Drays. WE go first!  
**Tuk**: Their guide went home. Who will lead them down?  
**Pemba**: Me! It’s just walking downhill!  
**Nalu**: Both groups want to go first. Hmm.  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.Civ.8.K-2, ask): taking turns is fair  
> **Nalu**: What is a fair way to choose?  
> - ✅ Take turns → Yes! One group now, the other next time.
> - The loudest goes first → Then quiet people never get a turn. Taking turns is fair.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about fairness and taking turns._


**Choices**

- **Hollins go first**
    - **Chief Brack**: Drays next time, then!
    - The Drays follow. Stomp, stomp.
- **Drays go first**
    - **Elder Maud**: Hollins next time, then.
    - The Hollins follow. Sniff, sniff.
- **Walk side by side**
    - **Elder Maud**: Hmph!
    - **Chief Brack**: Hmph!
    - But they walk side by side. The path is narrow!

#### `q48-canyon-done`

_Down, down, down you go. Now you are at the bottom._  

> **Lesson** (science, 2-ESS1-1, notice): how canyons form  
> **Nalu**: Look how deep this canyon is!  
> **Nalu**: A river made it. The water cut the rock, bit by bit.  
> **Nalu**: It took a very, very long time.  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that rivers slowly cut deep canyons into rock._

**Tuk**: Nobody pushed anybody. Hooray!  
**Elder Maud**: Hollins sleep on the left.  
**Chief Brack**: Drays sleep on the left of the left!  
**Nalu**: That’s the same place, silly. You’re all together.  

#### `q48-story`

_Night. Everyone sits by the campfire._  
_Two children reach for the last dumpling._  
_Grab! Pull! Oh no, a food fight!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, 1.G.A.3, ask): halves: one thing into two equal parts  
> **Nalu**: Oh dear. One dumpling, two children.  
> **Nalu**: How could they both have some?  
> - ✅ Cut it in half → Yes! Two halves. One for each. (Draw a circle in the air. Cut it down the middle.)
> - Throw it → Then nobody gets any! Cut it in half. Two halves, one each.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that cutting one thing into two equal halves lets two people share._

**Elder Maud**: Your great-great-grandpa took our ball!  
**Chief Brack**: Your great-great-grandma kicked it in the river!  
**Pemba**: Wait! I know a story about them!  
**Pemba**: They were twins! They played ball together.  
**Pemba**: They laughed so hard, the canyon cracked open!  
_Pemba looks at you. Don’t laugh! Keep a straight face!_  

**Choices**

- **Add: “They both sang badly!”**
    - **Chief Brack**: Drays sing badly!
    - **Elder Maud**: So do Hollins!
- **Nod slowly**
    - You nod. You don’t laugh. Well done!
- **Cough to hide a giggle**
    - Cough, cough! Pemba coughs too. Phew!

#### `q48-story-done`

**Elder Maud**: Twins! All this time.  
**Chief Brack**: Pass the dumplings, cousin.  
**Nalu**: (whispering) Pemba, that story isn’t true.  
**Pemba**: (whispering) But look. They’re sharing a blanket.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, ask): real or make-believe  
> **Nalu**: Can laughing really crack a canyon?  
> - ✅ No, that’s pretend → Right. It’s a make-believe story. Make-believe is fun. It’s good to say so.
> - Yes → Laughing is fun. But it can’t crack rock. That part is make-believe.
> _For grown-ups: Your child told the difference between something real and something make-believe._


**Choices**

- **Tell them it was pretend**
    - **Elder Maud**: We guessed! But it’s a lovely story.
    - **Chief Brack**: We’ll keep it anyway.
- **Keep Pemba’s secret**
    - **Nalu**: Okay. But you’ll tell them one day, Pemba.
    - **Pemba**: I promise. After the dumplings.

#### `q48-complete`

_In the morning, both groups climb out together._  
_They talk and laugh. Which twin was taller?_  
**Pemba**: Look! They’re sharing socks now!  
_Chief Brack wears socks. He looks surprised. He likes them._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): making up after a fight  
> **Nalu**: The groups were cross. Now they are friends.  
> **Nalu**: How can we make up after a fight?  
> - ✅ Say sorry and share → Yes! Sorry, then share. Like the socks!
> - Stay cross forever → Staying cross feels heavy. Saying sorry and sharing feels better.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about ways to make up after falling out._

**Elder Maud**: Thank you, guide. Here is a hat for you.  
**Elder Maud**: And we’ll build a bridge. No more fighting!  
_By night, a rope bridge swings over the canyon._  

## Q49. The Secret Dance Party

- `q49-s1`: Give out 5 secret party notes. Don’t get caught! · _Wait until the headmaster looks at the board._
- `q49-s2`: Keep the party going! Tap on the beat. · _Tap when the drum ring closes. Stomp, clap, stomp, clap!_

#### `q49-school`

_Ding, ding! The school bell rings._  
_The children sit very still. Nobody smiles._  
**Kaito**: I came to this school once. The rules are the same.  
**Kaito**: No running. No humming. No being too happy.  
_A new boy comes in. He has a red headband._  
**Pemba**: Hi! I’m new! I’m, um, Kuzo!  
**The Headmaster**: In this school, we do not dance. We do not sing.  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.Civ.8.K-2, ask): good rules keep us safe  
> **Kaito**: Some rules help us. Which rule helps?  
> - ✅ “Walk inside, so nobody trips.” → Yes. That rule keeps us safe.
> - “Never, ever smile.” → That rule doesn’t help anyone. Good rules keep us safe and kind.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about why we have rules and what makes a good one._

**Rin**: (whispering) What is dancing?  
**Pemba**: Oh! Then we will have a party!  

**Choices**

- **Hide notes in lunch boxes**
    - **Kaito**: Sneaky! I like it. Am I allowed to like it?
- **Fold paper birds**
    - The paper birds fly over the headmaster. Swoosh!
- **Kaito can hand them out**
    - **Kaito**: Hi. Party. Cave. Tonight. Shh. Bye.
    - Everybody says yes!

#### `q49-school-done`

_You pass the notes out, one at a time._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.4, do): count to 5  
> **Pemba**: Five notes for five friends. Count with me!  
> _(Count on one hand together.)_  
> **Pemba**: One, two, three, four, five!  
> Check: `edu:count:invites:5`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted five party notes on one hand._

**Rin**: A party in a cave? At night?  
**Pemba**: Yes! Bring your dancing feet!  
_The headmaster turns round. Everyone sits very still._  

#### `q49-party`

_The cave is full of children. They stand by the walls._  
_Your Nubbins bang on buckets. Bang! Bang! Bang!_  
**Pemba**: Dancing is easy. Feel the beat!  
**Rin**: My feet feel shy.  
**Kaito**: I don’t dance. I don’t think I can.  
**Pemba**: Everyone can dance. Watch the Nubbins!  
_The Nubbins stomp and clap. The children copy them._  

> **Lesson** (music, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): keep a beat and copy a pattern  
> **Pemba**: Copy the Nubbins: stomp, clap, stomp, clap!  
> _(Stomp and clap together. Keep the beat.)_  
> **Pemba**: What comes next? Stomp!  
> Check: `edu:clap:pattern:stomp-clap`
> _For grown-ups: Your child kept a steady beat and continued a stomp-clap pattern._

**Kaito**: Am I doing it? I think I’m doing it!  

#### `q49-party-done`

_Then the headmaster bursts in with a lantern._  
**The Headmaster**: WHAT is going on here?  
_The buckets stop. Everyone freezes._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): reading feelings on faces  
> _Look at the children’s faces. How do they feel?_  
> _(Wait for an answer.)_  
> - ✅ Worried → Yes. They are worried. Their eyes are wide.
> - Sleepy → Their eyes are wide open. Not sleepy! They feel worried. That’s okay to feel.
> _For grown-ups: Your child read other people’s faces to name a feeling._

_Kaito slowly puts his arms down._  

**Choices**

- **Everyone dance in front of Pemba**
    - Everyone dances at once. Pemba slips out the back.
- **Let Kaito speak**
    - **Kaito**: Sir, I followed every rule here. I was sad.
    - **Kaito**: Rules should help us. Not stop us being us.
    - **The Headmaster**: Hmm. Ten more minutes. Then home.
    - His foot taps along. Tap, tap. Pemba slips out.
- **Ask the headmaster to dance**
    - **The Headmaster**: I will not!
    - But his shoulders wiggle, just a bit. Pemba slips out.

#### `q49-complete`

_Outside, under the stars, the children laugh and laugh._  
**Rin**: We danced! We really danced!  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, notice): moving makes your heart beat faster  
> **Pemba**: Put your hand on your chest.  
> **Pemba**: Feel that thump-thump? That’s your heart.  
> **Pemba**: Dancing makes it beat faster. Moving keeps it strong!  
> _For grown-ups: Your child felt their heartbeat and heard that moving our bodies keeps our hearts strong._

**Pemba**: You were amazing. All of you!  
**Pemba**: You danced like shooting stars!  
**Kaito**: I don’t know what that means. But thank you.  
_Pemba ties the red headband round your wrist._  

## Q50. Uncle Tenzo’s Tea Stall

- `q50-s1`: Make 3 perfect cups of tea. · _Lift the kettle when the steam curls. Count slowly while the leaves soak._
- `q50-s2`: Give Mr Pell a cup of tea. · _Watch his face. A grumble means too hot. A sigh means just right._

#### `q50-open`

_A little tea stall stands by the pond. The kettle sings._  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Welcome, Keeper! I used to be a general. Now I make tea.  
**Kaito**: He got up at four o’clock. He painted the sign twice.  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Good tea is easy. Hot water. Then wait.  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Not too short. Not too long. Just right.  

> **Lesson** (maths, ELOF: Mathematics Development, do): count slowly to 5 to time something  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: The leaves are in. Now we wait and count.  
> _(Count slowly together, one to five.)_  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: One… two… three… four… five. Ready!  
> Check: `edu:count:steep:5`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted slowly to five to time the tea._

_Your Nubbin sniffs the tea tins. Sniff, sniff. Achoo!_  

**Choices**

- **Make jasmine tea first**
    - **Uncle Tenzo**: Lovely! Jasmine smells like flowers.
- **Make ginger tea first**
    - **Uncle Tenzo**: Bold! Ginger tea is spicy.
- **Ask Kaito which tea he likes**
    - **Kaito**: I don’t really like tea.
    - Uncle Tenzo gasps and holds the counter. Oh no!

#### `q50-open-done`

**Uncle Tenzo**: Three perfect cups! My heart is warm.  
**Kaito**: You burnt your tongue again, Uncle.  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Ow. Yes. A little.  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, ask): hot drinks: blow and wait  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: Tea is very hot. What should I do first?  
> - ✅ Blow on it and wait → Yes! Blow, blow. Wait a bit. Then sip.
> - Gulp it fast → Ow! Hot drinks can hurt your mouth. Blow on it and wait. Much better.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned to blow on a hot drink and wait before sipping._

**Uncle Tenzo**: I should have waited.  

#### `q50-grump`

_Here comes the first customer. He looks very grumpy._  
**Old Mr Pell**: Tea. Not fancy. Not flowery. No talking.  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Of course. Please sit down.  
**Old Mr Pell**: The last tea I had tasted like old socks.  
**Kaito**: Then go back there!  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Nephew. A grumpy guest just needs a kind welcome.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): understanding a grumpy face  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: Why might Mr Pell be grumpy?  
> - ✅ He’s tired or sad → Maybe. Grumpy faces can hide sad feelings. A kind hello can help.
> - He hates everyone → Hmm. Maybe he’s just having a hard day. A kind hello can help.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about the feelings that can hide behind a grumpy face._

**Kaito**: Is that a real saying?  
**Uncle Tenzo**: It is now.  

**Choices**

- **Give him jasmine tea**
    - **Old Mr Pell**: Flowery. I said no flowery.
    - He drinks it all. He asks for more!
- **Give him strong black tea**
    - **Old Mr Pell**: Hm. This tea is good and strong.
- **Ask what tea he drank as a boy**
    - **Old Mr Pell**: My mum made barley tea.
    - **Uncle Tenzo**: I have barley! I have everything!

#### `q50-grump-done`

**Old Mr Pell**: Hm. Not bad.  
_For Mr Pell, that is a great big smile!_  
_He counts his coins. He does not have enough._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.2, ask): how many more (adding on)  
> _Tea costs 5 coins. He has 3 coins._  
> _How many more does he need?_  
> _(Count on together: four, five.)_  
> - ✅ 2 more → Yes! Three… four, five. Two more.
> - 1 more → Let’s count on: three… four. Then five. That’s two more!
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted on from 3 to 5 to find how many more coins were needed._

**Uncle Tenzo**: (quietly) It’s your stall today, Keeper. You choose.  

**Choices**

- **This cup is free today**
    - **Old Mr Pell**: I don’t want a present.
    - **Uncle Tenzo**: It is not a present. It is a hello for tomorrow.
    - **Old Mr Pell**: Hm. Tomorrow, then.
- **Kindly ask for the rest tomorrow**
    - **Old Mr Pell**: Fair. I’ll bring it tomorrow.
    - **Uncle Tenzo**: Asking kindly is kind too.
- **Ask him to fix the wobbly stool**
    - **Old Mr Pell**: That stool wobbles. I noticed.
    - He fixes it with a folded leaf. No more wobble!

#### `q50-complete`

_By lunchtime the stall is full. Busy, busy!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): steam comes from very hot water  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: See the little cloud from the kettle?  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: That is steam. It comes from very hot water.  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: Steam is hot too. Only grown-ups touch the kettle.  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that very hot water makes steam, and that kettles are for grown-ups._

_Your Nubbins nap under the counter. Snore, snore._  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Tea is just leaves and water. The rest is caring.  
**Uncle Tenzo**: The stall is yours, Keeper. I will visit a lot.  
**Kaito**: He’ll drink all the tea.  
_Kaito fills Mr Pell’s cup. Nobody asked him to!_  
_Uncle Tenzo smiles and smiles._  
**Old Mr Pell**: Same time tomorrow. Don’t be late.  

## Q51. Cactus Juice

- `q51-s1`: Keep Tuk in the shade until Nalu comes back. · _Stand in his way. Your shiny Nubbin can lead him back._
- `q51-s2`: Take 3 little cactus pieces home to plant. · _Cut the small side pieces. Wear gloves. Spikes are sharp!_

#### `q51-desert`

_The desert is hot and sandy. Sand, sand, sand!_  
_Uh-oh. The water bottles are empty._  
**Nalu**: I’ll go and find water. Nobody eat anything strange!  
**Tuk**: Me? I never eat strange things.  
_Nalu goes. Tuk looks at a fat green cactus._  
_He cuts it open. He drinks the juice. Slurp!_  
**Tuk**: Ooh! Best. Drink. EVER!  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, ask): never eat or drink plants you don’t know  
> **Pemba**: Should we eat or drink plants we don’t know?  
> - ✅ No. Ask a grown-up first → Yes! Some plants can make us poorly. Always ask a grown-up first.
> - Yes, if it looks tasty → Lots of plants look tasty but aren’t safe. Always ask a grown-up first.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned never to eat or drink a plant without asking a grown-up first._

**Pemba**: Tuk, why are you smiling at that rock?  
**Tuk**: It smiled first!  
_Tuk wobbles off across the sand. Wibble, wobble!_  

**Choices**

- **Say the cloud looks friendly**
    - **Tuk**: Thank you! Finally, someone who listens.
- **Take the cactus away**
    - **Tuk**: You can take the cactus. I still feel happy!
- _(left out in this band; plays as `cup`)_

#### `q51-desert-done`

_Nalu comes back with lots of water._  
**Nalu**: I was gone ten minutes!  
**Tuk**: Nalu! You look like a big talking fish!  
**Nalu**: Tuk! You made the rule! You wrote it down!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): worry can feel like cross  
> _Nalu is cross. Why?_  
> _(Wait for an answer.)_  
> - ✅ She was worried about Tuk → Yes. She was worried. Worry can come out as cross.
> - She doesn’t like fish → Ha! Maybe. But she was worried about Tuk. Worry can make us feel cross.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about the worry that can sit underneath a cross feeling._


**Choices**

- **Stick up for Tuk: he was thirsty**
    - **Nalu**: We were all thirsty. That’s why we had a rule.
    - **Tuk**: Thank you. Your face is a melon. A kind melon.
- **Agree with Nalu: he broke his rule**
    - **Tuk**: She’s right. I don’t like it when the fish is right.

#### `q51-cuttings`

**Nalu**: Drink this water, Tuk. Slowly.  
**Tuk**: This water isn’t fizzy. It’s just wet.  
**Nalu**: Water is just what you need, silly.  
_Look at the cactus. It is fat and spiky._  
**Nalu**: Let’s take some little pieces home. They grow easily!  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, ask): what plants need: a cactus  
> **Nalu**: What does a little cactus need to grow?  
> - ✅ Sun and a little water → Yes! Cactus plants love sun. They keep water inside their fat stems.
> - Lots of snow → Brr! Too cold for a cactus. Cactus plants need sun and a little water.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that plants need sun and water, and that a cactus stores water in its stem._

**Nalu**: Keep them far away from Tuk!  

#### `q51-cuttings-done`

_You wrap the cactus pieces in cloth. Careful, careful!_  
_Your Nubbin carries one, like treasure._  
_You carry two._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): add 1 and 2  
> _Nubbin has 1. You have 2. How many altogether?_  
> _(Hold up 1 finger. Then 2 more.)_  
> - ✅ 3 → Yes! One and two make three.
> - 2 → Let’s count them all: one, two, three. Three pieces!
> _For grown-ups: Your child added 1 and 2 to make 3._

**Nalu**: Off we go, back to the Grove.  

#### `q51-complete`

_Back at the Grove, you plant them in the sun._  
_In the morning, they have little pink flowers!_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, do): clap syllables  
> **Pemba**: Let’s clap “cactus”. Cac-tus!  
> _(Clap once for each part.)_  
> **Pemba**: Two claps! Cac-tus!  
> Check: `edu:clap:syllables:cactus`
> _For grown-ups: Your child clapped the two syllables in “cactus”._

**Tuk**: I don’t remember yesterday at all.  
**Pemba**: You asked a rock to be your best friend!  
**Tuk**: I did not.  
**Pemba**: “Best drink ever!” You said it forty times!  
**Tuk**: Rules are hard when you’re thirsty.  
**Tuk**: But that’s when they help the most.  

## Q52. Puffin Is Lost

- `q52-s1`: Follow Puffin’s clues to find him. · _Look for white fur, giant footprints and squashed bushes._
- `q52-s2`: Feed Puffin 5 round fruits, gently. · _Move slowly. If he backs away, sit still and wait._
- `q52-s3`: Ride Puffin home to the Grove. · _Say “hup-hup”. Tilt to steer. Puffin lands by himself._

#### `q52-gone`

_Pemba sits by the gate. He holds a big, empty saddle._  
**Pemba**: Puffin is gone. The storm scared him. He flew away.  
**Tuk**: He’s a giant sky bison. We’ll spot him.  
**Pemba**: But he’s scared on his own.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): helping a sad friend  
> _Pemba is sad and worried. What can we do?_  
> - ✅ Help him look → Yes. Friends help each other. Thank you. I feel a bit better.
> - Tell him to stop being sad → Sad feelings don’t stop when we say so. Helping him look is kinder.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about how to help a friend who is sad and worried._

_Your Nubbin finds white fur on the fence._  
**Tuk**: A clue! Puffin went that way.  
**Pemba**: Will you help me find him?  

**Choices**

- **Promise to find him**
    - **Pemba**: Thank you. Puffin likes people who keep promises.
- **Ask how you lose a giant bison**
    - **Tuk**: I asked that too!
    - **Pemba**: I didn’t lose him. He got lost.
- **Let your Nubbin lead**
    - Your Nubbin sniffs. Achoo! Wrong way. Then it turns round.

#### `q52-gone-done`

_You follow the clues. Fur. Footprints. Squashed bushes._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): compare sizes: big and small  
> **Tuk**: Look at these footprints. Which one is Puffin’s?  
> - ✅ The giant one → Yes! A big animal makes big footprints.
> - The tiny one → That tiny one is a bird’s. Puffin is huge. His footprints are huge!
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared big and small footprints and matched them to animal size._

_Here is a hill with a big bison-shaped dent!_  
_In a cave, two big eyes blink._  
**Puffin**: Mrrrrrooooh.  

#### `q52-trust`

_Puffin is in the cave. He is shaking._  
**Pemba**: Puffin! It’s me!  
_The loud voice scares Puffin. He backs away._  
**Pemba**: He’s never been scared of me before.  
**Tuk**: He’s scared of everything right now. Give him time.  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS3-3, ask): caring for a scared animal  
> **Tuk**: How should we be with a scared animal?  
> - ✅ Quiet and slow → Yes. Soft voices. Slow hands. Lots of waiting.
> - Loud and fast → Loud and fast makes him more scared. Quiet and slow helps him feel safe.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that scared animals need quiet voices, slow movements and patience._

_Your Nubbin sits down quietly. It holds up a fruit._  
**Pemba**: He loves big round fruits.  

**Choices**

- **Sit down and wait**
    - You sit. You wait. A big wet nose comes closer.
- **Whistle Pemba’s bedtime tune**
    - **Pemba**: His bedtime whistle! How did you know?
    - Puffin’s ears flick up.
- **Roll a fruit to him gently**
    - Roll, roll. Slurp! Puffin looks at you. He likes you!

#### `q52-trust-done`

**Puffin**: Mrrrrrf!  
_Puffin comes out. He licks Pemba. Slurp!_  
**Pemba**: I missed you too! Ow, my ribs!  
_Puffin ate every fruit you gave him._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5  
> **Pemba**: How many fruits did he eat? Let’s count.  
> _(Count the fruits as Puffin eats them, one at a time.)_  
> **Pemba**: One, two, three, four, five. Munch!  
> Check: `edu:count:fruit:5`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted five fruits, one number for each._


#### `q52-home`

**Pemba**: Puffin wants to go home. He wants you to come too!  
**Tuk**: Climb on a giant bison. Totally normal day.  
_Puffin rumbles like a warm mountain._  
**Pemba**: Say “hup-hup!” Say it loud!  
_Up, up, up you go! The wind blows hard. Whoosh!_  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS2-1, notice): wind: we can feel it but not see it  
> **Pemba**: We can’t see the wind.  
> **Pemba**: But we can feel it on our faces.  
> **Pemba**: And we can see it move the trees!  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that wind is moving air we can feel but cannot see._

_Your Nubbin holds on tight._  

#### `q52-home-done`

_The Grove is far below. It looks tiny and green._  
_Puffin lands at the gate. THUMP!_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds  
> **Pemba**: Puffin and Pemba start the same way. P-p-p!  
> **Pemba**: What else starts with P?  
> - ✅ Pond → Yes! P-p-pond!
> - Tree → Tree starts with T. T-t-tree. Pond starts with P. P-p-pond!
> _For grown-ups: Your child listened for the P sound at the start of words._

_Fruit falls from every tree._  
_Pemba’s eyes are wet. He says it’s the wind._  
**Tuk**: Yes. Very windy today.  

#### `q52-complete`

**Pemba**: Thank you. I don’t know what I’d do without him.  
**Pemba**: I think it was my fault. I didn’t hold on.  
**Tuk**: You can’t hold on to a storm, Pemba.  
**Tuk**: But you came looking for him. That’s what counts.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): kind words help  
> _Pemba felt it was his fault._  
> _Tuk said kind words. That helped._  
> _(Ask: what kind words help you feel better?)_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard how kind words can help a friend who blames themselves._

_Puffin rests his big head on your shoulder._  
**Pemba**: He likes you now. He’ll come when you call!  
_A tuft of white fur lands on your Nubbin’s head._  

## Q53. The Fan Warriors

- `q53-s1`: Join the fan drill. Don’t drop your fan! · _Copy the warrior at the front. Open, sweep, close._
- `q53-s2`: Practise fan-blocks with Sumi. · _Block just as she moves. Let her come to you._

#### `q53-flattened`

_In the yard, warriors in green swish their fans._  
_Their faces are painted. They move all together._  
**Tuk**: Ha! Fan dancing! How cute!  
**Sumi**: We’re the fan-warriors. I’m Sumi. That was your last joke.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): teasing hurts feelings  
> _Tuk laughed at the fans. How might Sumi feel?_  
> - ✅ Not happy → Right. Teasing can hurt feelings. Tuk will learn that soon!
> - Very happy → Look at her face. She is not smiling. Teasing can hurt feelings.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about how teasing makes other people feel._

**Tuk**: Fans? Come on. I have a boomerang!  
**Sumi**: Want to try me?  
_Whoosh! Tuk is flat on his back. Bump!_  
**Tuk**: What just happened?  
**Sumi**: You fought me. For one second.  

**Choices**

- **Help Tuk up**
    - **Tuk**: Thanks. My pride is still down there.
- **Ask Sumi to teach you**
    - **Sumi**: You I like. Him, we’ll see.
    - **Tuk**: Him is right here.
- _(left out in this band; plays as `help`)_

#### `q53-flattened-done`

**Sumi**: Open. Sweep. Close. Again!  

> **Lesson** (maths, ELOF: Mathematics Development, do): order: first, next, last  
> **Sumi**: First, open the fan. Next, sweep. Last, close it.  
> _(Do it with your hand: open, sweep, close.)_  
> **Sumi**: First, next, last. Well done!  
> Check: `edu:order:fan-moves`
> _For grown-ups: Your child put three moves in order using first, next and last._

**Sumi**: Not bad. You are patient.  
**Tuk**: I want to train too. Properly.  
**Sumi**: Then wear the uniform. All of it.  
**Tuk**: All of it?  

#### `q53-uniform`

_Tuk comes out in a green robe. His face is painted._  
_White face. Red around the eyes. He looks great!_  

> **Lesson** (art, ask): mixing colours: red and white make pink  
> **Sumi**: Mix red paint and white paint. What colour do we get?  
> - ✅ Pink → Yes! Red and white make pink.
> - Blue → Let’s mix and look. Swirl, swirl. Pink! Red and white make pink.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that mixing red and white paint makes pink._

**Tuk**: Not. One. Word.  
**Sumi**: The paint says: I won’t hide who I am.  
_Your Nubbin is painted too. It holds a tiny fan._  
**Sumi**: Last lesson. Don’t block it. Step aside.  
**Sumi**: Spar with me, and see.  

**Choices**

- **Let Tuk go first**
    - Tuk runs. Sumi steps aside. Tuk keeps running. And running.
- **Have a go yourself**
    - **Sumi**: Good. Breathe. Watch my shoulder, not my fan.
- **Send your painted Nubbin**
    - **Sumi**: Ha! So brave. Come on, little warrior!

#### `q53-uniform-done`

**Sumi**: Look at you! Nearly a fan-warrior!  

> **Lesson** (health, CASEL: Self-management, do): calm breathing  
> **Sumi**: Warriors breathe slowly to stay calm.  
> _(Breathe in through your nose. Out through your mouth. Three times.)_  
> **Sumi**: In… and out. Calm and strong.  
> Check: `edu:breathe:slow:3`
> _For grown-ups: Your child practised three slow, calming breaths._

**Tuk**: Nearly? I’m wearing face paint!  
**Sumi**: The paint helps your friends know it’s you.  
**Sumi**: That part you’ve got.  

#### `q53-complete`

**Tuk**: Sumi, I was wrong. I’m sorry.  
**Tuk**: Anyone can be a warrior. Boys and girls.  
**Tuk**: It’s about practice. And helping each other.  
**Sumi**: That is a very good sorry.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): what makes a good sorry  
> **Sumi**: What makes a good sorry?  
> - ✅ Say what you did wrong → Yes. Tuk said “I was wrong”. Then sorry. That’s a real sorry.
> - Say it really fast → A fast sorry can feel empty. Say what you did. Then say sorry.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about what makes an apology real._

**Sumi**: Keep the fan, Keeper. And the paint!  
_Your Nubbin won’t wash its face for three days._  

## Q54. The Sword Master

- `q54-s1`: Do Master Pian’s three lessons: brush, sit still, look. · _Go slow and steady. Slow is better than fast here._
- `q54-s2`: Bring the fallen star rock to Master Pian. · _Tap when the metal glows orange. Rest in between._

#### `q54-lessons`

_Master Pian lives at the top of the hill._  
**Tuk**: Master Pian, I want to learn the sword.  
**Tuk**: Pemba can fly. Nalu does water. I just have plans.  
**Master Pian**: Plans are good. A sword with no plan is just a stick.  
**Master Pian**: First, write your name with a brush.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): write the letter T  
> **Master Pian**: Tuk starts with T. Let’s write it.  
> _(Draw a big T in the air with your finger.)_  
> **Master Pian**: A tall line with a hat on top. T for Tuk!  
> Check: `edu:trace:letter:t`
> _For grown-ups: Your child traced the letter T, the first letter of Tuk’s name._

**Master Pian**: Then sit still. Then look at the garden.  
**Tuk**: That’s not sword stuff!  
**Master Pian**: It is all sword stuff.  
_Your Nubbin sits very still. Master Pian nods._  

**Choices**

- **Start with the brush**
    - **Master Pian**: Good. A brush stroke can’t be taken back. Nor a sword stroke.
- **Start with sitting still**
    - **Tuk**: I can sit still. Watch!
    - Tuk sits still for nine seconds.
- **Start with the garden**
    - **Master Pian**: What do you see?
    - **Tuk**: Rocks. More rocks.
    - **Master Pian**: Look longer.

#### `q54-lessons-done`

**Master Pian**: Today you learned three things.  
**Master Pian**: Your hand. Your breath. Your eyes.  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): body awareness: hands, breath, eyes  
> **Master Pian**: Let’s try all three.  
> _(Wiggle your fingers. Take a big breath. Blink your eyes.)_  
> **Master Pian**: Hand, breath, eyes. Very good.  
> Check: `edu:move:body:hand-breath-eyes`
> _For grown-ups: Your child moved their fingers, breathed deeply and blinked, noticing three parts of the body._

**Tuk**: And my knees. My knees learned a lot.  
**Master Pian**: Knees always learn first.  

#### `q54-meteor`

_That night, a bright light falls from the sky. Whoosh!_  
_The ground shakes. Rumble!_  
**Tuk**: A shooting star! It landed!  
**Master Pian**: A sword from a fallen star. That is rare.  
_In a hole lies a black rock. It is heavy and warm._  
**Tuk**: What if I make a mistake? There’s only one star.  
**Master Pian**: Then you learn something. Every master has a mistake.  
**Master Pian**: Mine is a very wobbly spoon.  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Approaches to Learning, notice): mistakes help us learn  
> _Everyone makes mistakes. Even masters._  
> _Mistakes help us learn._  
> _(Ask: what did you learn by trying again?)_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that mistakes are part of learning, even for experts._


#### `q54-meteor-done`

_Clang, clang! Tuk hammers the hot metal._  
_Hiss! He dips it in water. Steam puffs up._  
_The blade is black, with tiny silver dots._  
**Tuk**: Whoa.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): shooting stars are space rocks  
> **Master Pian**: A shooting star is not really a star.  
> **Master Pian**: It is a little rock from space.  
> **Master Pian**: It glows hot as it falls through the sky.  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that a shooting star is a small space rock glowing as it falls._


#### `q54-complete`

**Master Pian**: A sword becomes part of how you stand and breathe.  
**Master Pian**: Friends are like that too.  
**Tuk**: Can I name it?  
**Master Pian**: You may.  
**Tuk**: Star Stabber!  
**Master Pian**: You may also think about it for longer.  
_Master Pian makes a little wooden practice sword for you too._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): compare sizes  
> **Master Pian**: Two swords. Which one is bigger?  
> _(Point to the big one. Then the small one.)_  
> - ✅ Tuk’s sword → Yes. Tuk’s is big. Yours is small. Small, and just right for you.
> - Your sword → Yours is the small one. Tuk’s is bigger. Small, and just right for you.
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared two objects and named the bigger and the smaller one._


## Q55. The Ember Bay Players

- `q55-s1`: Watch the first part of the play. · _Sit and watch. Tap to throw a peanut, if you like._
- `q55-s2`: Watch the end of the play. Show what you think! · _The actors will remember what you did._

#### `q55-act1`

_A beach stage! The sign says: THE KEEPER AND FRIENDS._  
**Tuk**: A play about us! Hooray!  
**Bao**: I can’t see the stage. This will be great.  
_An actor in orange does cartwheels. He falls over._  
**The Ember Bay Players**: I am Pemba! I love fun! And fun!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.K.3, ask): characters: actors pretend to be someone  
> **Pemba**: That actor is pretending. Who is he pretending to be?  
> - ✅ Pemba → Yes! He’s pretending to be me. Actors pretend.
> - A fish → Ha! No. He said “I am Pemba”. Actors pretend to be someone else.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named the character an actor was playing._

**Pemba**: Is that what I’m like?  
_An actor playing Tuk only talks about meat._  
**Tuk**: I am more than meat!  
_A big actor with a beard plays Bao._  
**Bao**: I love him. Ten out of ten!  

#### `q55-act1-done`

_Ding! Time for a break._  
**Kaito**: They gave me a mop for hair.  
**Kaito**: And I was the baddie. That is how I was.  
**Kaito**: But it is not how I am now.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, ask): people can change  
> **Kaito**: I used to be grumpy and unkind. Now I try to be kind.  
> **Kaito**: Can people change?  
> - ✅ Yes! → Thank you. I’m trying every day.
> - No → Sure they can. Look at him. He’s trying. I’m trying every day.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about whether people can change and grow kinder._

**Bao**: Look at that. You’ve grown. Pass the peanuts.  

#### `q55-finale`

_An actor in a shiny cape walks on. That’s you!_  
**The Ember Bay Players**: The Keeper is here! With lots of Nubbins!  
_The Nubbins are socks on sticks. Five sock Nubbins!_  
_Oh! One falls off. Plop! It rolls in the sand._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): take away 1  
> _Five sock Nubbins. One fell off. How many now?_  
> _(Hold up five fingers. Fold one down.)_  
> - ✅ 4 → Yes! Five take away one is four.
> - 6 → Let’s count the fingers still up. One, two, three, four. Four!
> _For grown-ups: Your child took one away from five and found four._

_The pretend Keeper slips on a banana. The play ends!_  
**Tuk**: They can’t end it like that!  
**Bao**: Your move, Keeper.  

**Choices**

- **Cheer anyway**
    - You cheer! The actors smile. The sock Nubbins bow.
    - **The Ember Bay Players**: A fan! We have a fan!
- **Climb up and fix the ending**
    - You save the sock Nubbin. You help the Keeper up.
    - **The Ember Bay Players**: A new ending! Everybody, make it up!
- _(left out in this band; plays as `cheer`)_

#### `q55-finale-done`

**Kaito**: I’m glad we came.  
**Tuk**: I’m not. I’m writing a letter.  
**Bao**: I’m writing a fan letter. To the big guy with the beard.  
**Pemba**: I liked the start. And the middle. Not the end.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.K.3, ask): stories have a start, a middle and an end  
> **Pemba**: Every story has a start, a middle and an…?  
> - ✅ End → Yes! Start, middle, end.
> - Snack → Ha! Snacks are good. But stories have an end. Start, middle, end!
> _For grown-ups: Your child named the three parts of a story: start, middle and end._


#### `q55-complete`

_After the show, the actors give out masks._  
**The Ember Bay Players**: Take a mask! We do the show again next week!  
**Bao**: Great. I’ll come every week.  
**Pemba**: Will they ever do a play where we win?  
**Kaito**: Nobody would believe it.  
_You go home with two masks. One happy. One sad._  

> **Lesson** (art, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, do): faces show feelings  
> _One mask smiles. One mask frowns._  
> _(Make a happy face. Now make a sad face.)_  
> _Faces show how we feel!_  
> Check: `edu:make:face:happy-sad`
> _For grown-ups: Your child made happy and sad faces, like the theatre masks._


## Q56. Rumble Night

- `q56-s1`: Find the secret arena under the big boulder. · _The big boulder has a crack. Knock twice._
- `q56-s2`: Your Power Nubbin has a match. Win or lose, play fair! · _She feels footsteps. Stand very still, then push._

#### `q56-flyer`

_A paper flyer blows by. Flap! It sticks to your Nubbin._  
**Tuk**: “Rumble Night! Wrestling under the big boulder! Bring snacks!”  
**Tuk**: Under a boulder? Of course it is.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): rocks come in all sizes  
> **Tuk**: A boulder is a very, very big rock.  
> **Tuk**: Rocks come in all sizes.  
> **Tuk**: Tiny sand. Little pebbles. Giant boulders!  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that rocks come in many sizes, from grains of sand to boulders._

**Pemba**: Let’s go! Is wrestling like hugging, but grumpier?  
**Tuk**: That’s a very good way to say it.  
_Your strongest Nubbin shows off its tiny arms._  

**Choices**

- **Pack snacks, like the flyer says**
    - **Tuk**: Finally! Someone who reads the instructions.
- **Bring your Power Nubbin**
    - Your Power Nubbin marches ahead. Stomp, stomp!
- **Ask if this is a good idea**
    - **Tuk**: Hmm. I just want to see it.
    - **Tuk**: We’ll stay together. That’s the good idea.

#### `q56-flyer-done`

_You knock twice on the boulder. Knock, knock!_  
_The big boulder slides away. Rumble, rumble!_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds  
> **Tuk**: Boulder starts with B. B-b-boulder!  
> **Tuk**: What else starts with B?  
> - ✅ Ball → Yes! B-b-ball! Boulder and ball!
> - Sand → Sand starts with S. S-s-sand. Ball starts with B. B-b-ball!
> _For grown-ups: Your child listened for the B sound at the start of words._

_Down below, a crowd cheers by torchlight._  
**Tuk**: Okay. That’s a cool door.  

#### `q56-bout`

**Rumble Master Gorro**: WELCOME TO RUMBLE NIGHT!  
**The Big Slab**: The Big Slab is BIG! The Big Slab is STRONG!  
**Tuk**: Why does he talk about himself like that?  
_In walks the champion. She is tiny. Her feet are bare._  
**Rumble Master Gorro**: THE LITTLE LANDSLIDE!  
_The Big Slab runs at her. Stomp, stomp, stomp!_  
_She stamps her foot. Whoosh! He flies into the noodle stand._  
**Bao**: Next! Anyone? I can feel you all wriggling.  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): senses: feeling with our feet  
> _She can’t see with her eyes._  
> _How does she know where everyone is?_  
> - ✅ She feels it with her feet → Yep. Footsteps make the ground shake a little. My feet feel it. Easy.
> - She guesses → Guesses? Ha! I feel it with my feet. Footsteps make the ground shake a little.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that we can sense things through touch, like feeling footsteps through the ground._

_Your Power Nubbin steps up. Everyone goes quiet._  
**Bao**: Small and heavy. I like it already.  

**Choices**

- **Ask her to go easy**
    - **Bao**: Nope. Your Nubbin deserves a real match.
- **Tell her to do her best**
    - **Bao**: Oh, I will. I like that you asked.
- **Bow to her**
    - **Bao**: You know I can’t see that, right?
    - She grins. She felt the floor creak.

#### `q56-bout-done`

**Bao**: Not bad! Hardly anyone lasts that long.  
_Your Nubbin is covered in sand. And glory!_  
**Bao**: And you didn’t run away. Most people run.  
_Your Nubbin bows. She bows back._  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, ask): being a good sport  
> _Win or lose, what do we say after a game?_  
> - ✅ “Good game!” → Yes! “Good game!” and a high five.
> - “No fair!” → Losing can feel bad. That’s okay. But we still say “Good game!”
> _For grown-ups: Your child practised what a good sport says, win or lose._


#### `q56-complete`

_After the show, the champion finds you._  
**Bao**: My name’s Bao. Don’t tell anyone.  
**Bao**: Up there, everyone helps me. Even when I don’t need it.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): ask before you help  
> **Bao**: What should people do before they help me?  
> - ✅ Ask “Do you want help?” → Yes! Just ask. Then listen to the answer.
> - Just grab my arm → Nope. Grabbing isn’t helping. Ask first. Then listen to the answer.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned to ask before helping someone, and to listen to their answer._

**Tuk**: How do you know where everyone is?  
**Bao**: I feel it with my feet. Your feet are noisy, Ponytail!  
**Bao**: Here. A spare belt. Your Nubbin earned it.  
_The belt is much too big. Your Nubbin drags it proudly._  
**Rumble Master Gorro**: And take a ring post! For your own arena!  

## Q57. The Painted Lady

- `q57-s1`: Take food and medicine to 5 houses. · _Grove fruit and the healer’s herbs both help. Knock gently._
- `q57-s2`: Clean the river at night. Don’t be seen! · _Steer to the brown patches. If a lantern turns, hide in the mist._

#### `q57-sick`

_Down the river, the water is brown and yucky._  
_A little village sits on stilts in the water._  
**Ma Reedy**: The fish are gone. Lots of us feel poorly.  
**Ma Reedy**: The works up the river tips muck into it.  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, ask): what animals need: fish need clean water  
> **Nalu**: What do fish need to live?  
> - ✅ Clean water → Yes. Fish need clean water. Dirty water makes the whole river poorly.
> - Sweets → Ha! No. Fish need clean water. Dirty water makes the whole river poorly.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that fish and other river animals need clean water to live._

**Nalu**: That’s not fair! Someone should help.  
**Ma Reedy**: Long ago, a river spirit helped us. A lady with a painted face.  
_A little girl holds up an empty fish basket._  
_Nalu goes quiet. She has an idea._  

**Choices**

- **Promise to help**
    - **Ma Reedy**: You’re kind. But kind can’t clean a river.
    - **Nalu**: Watch us.
- **Give them fruit from the Grove**
    - **Ma Reedy**: Fruit! The children will be so happy!
- **Ask about the river spirit**
    - **Ma Reedy**: She came at night, in the mist. With a lantern.

#### `q57-sick-done`

_You take food and medicine to the houses._  
_Knock, knock. Gently. People are poorly._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.4, do): count to 5  
> **Nalu**: Five houses. Let’s count them.  
> _(Count on your fingers, one house at a time.)_  
> **Nalu**: One, two, three, four, five. All done!  
> Check: `edu:count:houses:5`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted five houses on their fingers._

_At the last house, a boy gives you a drawing._  
_It is a lady with a painted face._  
**Nalu**: That settles it.  

#### `q57-night`

_Night-time. Mist floats on the river._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): mist is tiny drops of water  
> **Nalu**: Mist is like a cloud near the ground.  
> **Nalu**: It is made of tiny drops of water.  
> **Nalu**: Wave your hand in it. Your fingers feel damp!  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that mist is a low cloud made of tiny water drops._

_Nalu wears a white veil and a big straw hat._  
**Nalu**: Do I look like a river spirit?  
**Tuk**: You look like Nalu in a hat. A spooky hat.  
**Pemba**: You look amazing! Amazing and spooky!  
**Nalu**: I lift the muck out. You scoop it into barrels.  
_Your Swim Nubbin dives in. Splash! It finds an old can._  

**Choices**

- **Clean the river first**
    - **Nalu**: Yes. Clean first. The rest can wait.
- **Block the muddy pipe**
    - **Tuk**: I can help with that. I brought a giant cork!
- **Leave a note at the works**
    - **Nalu**: “Please stop. Love, the river.” Perfect!

#### `q57-night-done`

_By morning, the water is clear. You can see the stones!_  
_A fish jumps up. Splash!_  
**Ma Reedy**: The Painted Lady came back!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): reading feelings on faces  
> _Look at Ma Reedy. How does she feel?_  
> - ✅ Happy and surprised → Yes! Big eyes, big smile. Happy and surprised!
> - Cross → Look again. She is smiling. She feels happy and surprised!
> _For grown-ups: Your child read someone’s face to name two feelings at once._

_Ma Reedy looks at your boat. Nalu freezes._  
**Nalu**: (whispering) Should we tell her?  

**Choices**

- **Come out and tell the truth**
    - **Ma Reedy**: I know, dear. Spirits don’t sneeze.
    - **Ma Reedy**: The Painted Lady is anyone who helps. Now it’s you.
- **Float back into the mist**
    - You float away. The village tells the story again and again.
    - **Nalu**: Maybe they just need someone to help.

#### `q57-complete`

_The village wakes up. The water is clean!_  
_The fish baskets are full._  
_The works puts up a sign: PIPE CLOSED. SORRY._  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, K-ESS3-3, ask): caring for shared places: keep rivers clean  
> **Nalu**: How can we help keep rivers clean?  
> - ✅ Put rubbish in the bin → Yes! Rubbish goes in the bin. Never in the river.
> - Throw rubbish in the water → Then the fish get poorly. Rubbish goes in the bin. Never in the river.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about how everyone can help look after rivers._

**Ma Reedy**: She came back! After all these years!  
**Tuk**: She was very wet. She owes me new trousers.  
**Ma Reedy**: Take this hat. And these little fish.  
**Ma Reedy**: Put them in your stream. They help keep it clear.  

## Q58. Aunt Wen and the Clouds

- `q58-s1`: Visit Aunt Wen. She will read the bones. · _Find the house with stars on the door. Bring a fruit to be polite._
- `q58-s2`: Climb Mount Grumble and peek inside. · _Take the zigzag path, or call Puffin. Keep your Nubbin away from the edge._
- `q58-s3`: Help Pemba push the clouds into a shape. · _Drag the puffs into the outline. The wind blows them one way._

#### `q58-reading`

_In Hillstead, people line up at a starry door._  
**Aunt Wen**: Come in! Aunt Wen sees everything. Well, most things.  
**Aunt Wen**: Your Nubbin will find something lovely today. Probably a snack.  
**Tuk**: Nubbins always find snacks. That’s not magic.  
_Outside, a man carries an umbrella. The sky is blue and sunny._  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS2-1, ask): weather: rain comes from clouds  
> **Tuk**: The sky is blue. No clouds. Does he need an umbrella?  
> - ✅ No, it’s sunny → Right! No rain clouds, no umbrella. Maybe a sun hat instead!
> - Yes → Look up. No clouds at all. Rain comes from clouds. No clouds, no umbrella!
> _For grown-ups: Your child used the sky to decide what the weather will be._

**Pemba**: She says I’ll have a big adventure!  
**Nalu**: She says that to everyone, Pemba.  

**Choices**

- **Believe her**
    - **Aunt Wen**: Wise Keeper! Your Nubbin agrees. It’s nodding.
    - Your Nubbin is nodding off to sleep.
- **Agree with Tuk**
    - **Tuk**: Finally! Someone with a brain.
- **Say you’ll wait and see**
    - **Aunt Wen**: Fair. The bones can wait. So can I.

#### `q58-reading-done`

**Aunt Wen**: Oh! And the mountain won’t go BOOM this year.  
**Aunt Wen**: I read the clouds. Nice and fluffy.  
**Tuk**: Wait. That mountain can go BOOM?  
_Its name is Mount Grumble. It rumbles._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): rhymes  
> **Tuk**: Grumble and rumble sound the same at the end.  
> **Tuk**: What else rhymes with grumble?  
> - ✅ Tumble → Yes! Grumble, rumble, tumble!
> - Cake → Cake doesn’t sound like grumble. Grumble, rumble, tumble. Those rhyme!
> _For grown-ups: Your child listened for words that rhyme with “grumble”._


#### `q58-mountain`

_Above Hillstead, Mount Grumble puffs out grey smoke._  
**Tuk**: I’m going up there to see for myself.  
**Pemba**: I’ll come! Puffin can fly us up.  
**Nalu**: I’ll stay and talk to the people. Just in case.  
_At the top, it is hot. It smells like eggs._  
_Inside, red rock bubbles. Blub, blub, hiss!_  

> **Lesson** (science, 2-ESS1-1, notice): volcanoes and lava  
> **Tuk**: This mountain is a volcano.  
> **Tuk**: Deep inside, the rock is so hot it melts.  
> **Tuk**: When melted rock comes out, it is called lava.  
> **Tuk**: Lava is very hot. We stay far away.  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that a volcano has melted rock inside, called lava when it comes out._

**Tuk**: That is NOT nice and fluffy!  
**Tuk**: But they only listen to Aunt Wen. And she only listens to clouds.  

#### `q58-mountain-done`

**Pemba**: Wait! They only listen to clouds…  
**Tuk**: Then we make them a cloud!  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, ask): tell a grown-up about danger  
> _If you see something dangerous, what should you do?_  
> - ✅ Tell a grown-up → Yes! Tell a grown-up straight away.
> - Keep it a secret → Danger is never a secret to keep. Tell a grown-up straight away.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned to tell a grown-up straight away when something is dangerous._

**Pemba**: A danger cloud! Big and clear!  
**Tuk**: Pemba, you clever little weather boy.  

#### `q58-clouds`

_Everyone stands in the square. Aunt Wen looks up._  
**Aunt Wen**: The clouds will speak! Hmm. That one looks like a duck.  
_Up high, Pemba and Puffin push the clouds. Whoosh!_  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS2-1, notice): wind moves clouds  
> **Pemba**: Wind is moving air.  
> **Pemba**: It pushes the clouds across the sky.  
> **Pemba**: Blow on your hand. That’s a tiny wind!  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that wind is moving air that pushes clouds along._

**Tuk**: We need one shape. A big, pointy mountain.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.2, do): shapes: triangle  
> **Tuk**: A mountain shape is a triangle. Three sides, three points.  
> _(Draw a triangle in the air. Count the sides.)_  
> **Tuk**: One, two, three. A triangle!  
> Check: `edu:tap:shape:triangle`
> _For grown-ups: Your child drew a triangle and counted its three sides._

**Nalu**: Tuk, we’re tricking them. Is that okay?  
**Tuk**: Hmm. You always ask good questions when I’m busy.  

**Choices**

- **Make a frowning volcano cloud**
    - **Tuk**: Tonight we get them safe. Tomorrow we show them why.
    - **Nalu**: Promise?
    - **Tuk**: Promise. With drawings.
- **Make a giant arrow at the mountain**
    - **Nalu**: Yes! Don’t trick them. Show them where to look.
    - Everyone turns to look at the smoking mountain.

#### `q58-clouds-done`

_A giant cloud hangs over Hillstead._  
_Your Nubbins float down out of it. Damp and proud!_  
**Aunt Wen**: Oh my! The clouds have never been this clear!  
**Aunt Wen**: Everyone, get your shovels!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, notice): changing your mind  
> _Aunt Wen changed her mind._  
> _That is a brave thing to do._  
> _(Ask: have you ever changed your mind?)_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that changing your mind when you learn something new is brave and wise._


#### `q58-complete`

_The people dig a long ditch, all night long._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.2, ask): add 2  
> _Five people dig. Two more come to help._  
> _How many are digging now?_  
> _(Hold up five fingers. Add two more.)_  
> - ✅ 7 → Yes! Five and two more make seven.
> - 6 → Count on: five… six, seven. Seven diggers!
> _For grown-ups: Your child added two more to five by counting on._

_Mount Grumble pops. The lava runs down the ditch._  
_It goes away from the houses. Everyone is safe!_  
**Aunt Wen**: Thank you, young man. Even if you don’t believe me.  
**Tuk**: I believe one word. “Shovels.” Great idea.  
**Aunt Wen**: Bring your Nubbins to me. I’ll read the bones for them.  
_Tuk says he’ll never go. He goes twice a week._  

## Q59. The Water Scroll

- `q59-s1`: Keep Captain Hake talking. · _Ask about everything on his ship. The bison whistle takes ages._
- `q59-s2`: Get away from the pirates down the river. · _Steer round the rocks. Your Swim Nubbins can push._

#### `q59-market`

_A pirate ship sits at the river market._  
**Captain Hake**: Step up! Treasures! No questions!  
**Pemba**: A whistle shaped like a bison! I want it!  
**Tuk**: It’s a stick with a hole in it.  
_Nalu goes still. She sees an old scroll._  
_It has pictures of water. Waves, drips and splashes._  

> **Lesson** (science, 2-ESS2-3, ask): water can freeze into ice and melt back  
> **Nalu**: Water can change. Very cold water freezes into…?  
> - ✅ Ice → Yes! Very cold water turns into hard ice. Warm the ice, and it melts back to water.
> - Sand → Not sand. It turns into ice. Warm the ice, and it melts back to water.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that water freezes into ice when very cold and melts again when warm._

**Nalu**: It’s a water scroll. It could teach me so much.  
**Captain Hake**: Two hundred gold coins, lady. Or your bison.  

**Choices**

- **Ask for a lower price**
    - **Captain Hake**: Two hundred! Final offer! Two hundred and ten!
    - **Tuk**: That’s not how it works.
- **Pretend to walk away**
    - **Captain Hake**: Fine! Nobody wants it! …Two hundred?
- **Ask where he got it**
    - **Captain Hake**: I found it. In a temple. On a shelf.

#### `q59-market-done`

_You turn round. Nalu walks fast to your boat._  
_The glass case is empty!_  
**Tuk**: Nalu! Did you just…?  
**Nalu**: Walk. Normally. Now.  
**Captain Hake**: Stop! After them!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, notice): taking what isn’t ours is not okay  
> _Nalu took the scroll without paying._  
> _Taking things that aren’t ours is not okay._  
> _Even when we want them a lot._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that taking things that belong to someone else is wrong, even when we want them._


#### `q59-chase`

_You jump in your boat. The pirates come after you!_  
**Tuk**: You took it, Nalu!  
**Nalu**: The pirates took it first. I’ll give it back.  
**Pemba**: Are we pirates now? Can I have a hat?  
**Captain Hake**: And he never paid for the whistle!  
**Pemba**: Oh. Oops.  
_Your Swim Nubbins jump in. They push the boat. Splash, splash!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): a push makes things move  
> **Pemba**: How do the Nubbins make the boat go?  
> - ✅ They push it → Yes! A push makes things move. A big push makes them go fast!
> - They sing to it → Singing is nice. But it won’t move a boat. A push makes things move!
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that a push makes things move, and a bigger push makes them go faster._


**Choices**

- **Tell Nalu taking it was wrong**
    - **Nalu**: I know. I’ll make it right.
- **Ask Pemba to blow the whistle**
    - Toot! Far away, a sky bison turns his head.
- _(left out in this band; plays as `wrong`)_

#### `q59-chase-done`

_The pirate ship gets stuck. Crunch!_  
_It is stuck between two big rocks._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): position: between  
> _Where is the pirate ship stuck?_  
> - ✅ Between two rocks → Yes! A rock on this side. A rock on that side. The ship is between!
> - On top of a cloud → Ha! Look again. One rock here, one rock there. The ship is between them!
> _For grown-ups: Your child used the position word “between”._

**Captain Hake**: This isn’t over! Has anyone got a long stick?  
_Your boat sails on. Bye-bye, pirates!_  

#### `q59-complete`

_That night, Nalu practises with the scroll._  
_Swish! A ribbon of water snaps out and back._  
**Nalu**: I did it! I really did it!  
**Tuk**: Great. Now do the move where we don’t take things.  
**Nalu**: I’ll give the scroll back to the water villages. I promise.  
**Pemba**: And I’ll go back and pay for my whistle!  
**Nalu**: My tummy felt funny when I took it.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, ask): listening to your feelings  
> **Nalu**: Why did my tummy feel funny?  
> - ✅ You knew it wasn’t right → Yes. That feeling was telling me something. Listen to that feeling.
> - You ate too many snacks → Ha! Not this time. I knew it wasn’t right. Listen to that feeling.
> _For grown-ups: Your child noticed that our bodies can tell us when we have done something wrong._

_Your Swim Nubbin flicks its tail. A ribbon of water snaps!_  

## Q60. The Winged Lemur

- `q60-s1`: Follow the little winged lemur to the old temple. · _It glides from high place to high place. Watch its tail!_
- `q60-s2`: Make friends: give the lemur 3 fruits. · _Hold the fruit out. Stay still. Don’t grab._

#### `q60-thief`

_Your fruit basket is empty. Again!_  
_There are tiny footprints. And peach stones in a neat pile._  
**Pemba**: That’s no bird. Birds don’t make neat piles.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 3  
> **Pemba**: How many peach stones? Let’s count.  
> _(Point to each stone and count.)_  
> **Pemba**: One, two, three. Three peaches gone!  
> Check: `edu:count:peach-stones:3`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted three peach stones, pointing to each one._

_Up in a tree sits a furry thing. Big ears. Wings!_  
**Lumi**: Chirrup!  
**Pemba**: A winged lemur! They used to live at the old temple.  
_It grabs a peach and glides away. Whee!_  
**Pemba**: Let’s follow it. Gently!  

**Choices**

- **Run after it**
    - You run. It looks back. It thinks this is a game!
- **Leave a fruit out**
    - **Pemba**: Clever! It will come back for more.
- **Ask what to call it**
    - **Pemba**: Let’s get to know it first. Then we’ll pick a name.
    - Chirrup! The lemur seems to like that idea.

#### `q60-thief-done`

_Up in an old temple, under the broken roof…_  
_…you find a nest! Fruit stones. Buttons. Your spoon!_  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS3-1, ask): animal homes  
> **Pemba**: Where does the lemur live?  
> - ✅ In a nest, up high → Yes! A cosy nest up high, where it feels safe.
> - In the pond → Fish live in ponds. The lemur lives in a nest. Up high, where it feels safe.
> _For grown-ups: Your child matched an animal to its home._

**Pemba**: Oh. It lives up here all alone.  
**Lumi**: Chirr.  

#### `q60-friends`

_The temple is quiet and cold._  
_Old paintings show people laughing with lemurs._  
**Pemba**: I learned to fly here. It used to be full of friends.  
**Pemba**: The lemur isn’t really a thief. It’s hungry and lonely.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): what helps lonely feelings  
> **Pemba**: The lemur is lonely. What helps lonely feelings?  
> - ✅ A friend → Yes. A friend. And a little kindness.
> - Hiding → Hiding can make lonely feel bigger. A friend helps. Like us!
> _For grown-ups: Your child named loneliness and thought about what helps._

_Your Nubbin sits in the middle. It holds up a fruit._  
**Lumi**: …Chirr?  

#### `q60-friends-done`

_The lemur lands on your shoulder. It wraps its tail round your neck._  
_It falls asleep. Zzz._  
**Pemba**: It likes you!  
**Pemba**: Should we take it home? Or is this its home?  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, ask): what animals need  
> **Pemba**: What does the lemur need to be well?  
> - ✅ Food, water and a safe home → Yes! And kind friends.
> - A hat → A hat would be funny! But it needs food, water and a safe home.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named what animals need: food, water and a safe home._


**Choices**

- **Carry it home to the Grove**
    - **Pemba**: The Grove has fruit and friends. A good home!
- **Leave the door open. Let it choose**
    - You walk away slowly. Flap, flap! It lands on your shoulder.
    - **Pemba**: It chose us!

#### `q60-complete`

**Pemba**: It loves shiny things. And peaches. I know! Lumi!  
**Pemba**: Welcome home, Lumi!  
**Lumi**: Chirrup chirrup!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RF.K.2, ask): first sounds  
> **Pemba**: Lumi starts with L. L-l-Lumi!  
> **Pemba**: What else starts with L?  
> - ✅ Lemur → Yes! L-l-lemur! Lumi the lemur!
> - Fruit → Fruit starts with F. F-f-fruit. Lemur starts with L. L-l-lemur!
> _For grown-ups: Your child listened for the L sound at the start of words._

_Lumi rides home on your shoulder. Nibble, nibble._  
_That night, Lumi sleeps in the fruit basket!_  
**Pemba**: That explains a lot. Count your spoons!  

## Q61. Pemba Day

- `q61-s1`: Search the old records room for clues. · _The oldest papers are at the back. Your Nubbin can squeeze behind shelves._
- `q61-s2`: Show your clues at Pemba’s trial. · _Pick your best clue. Any clue will do!_

#### `q61-trial`

_Kettleby is having a party. Flags! Sweets!_  
_There is a big straw statue of a long-ago hero._  
_People bonk the statue with sticks. Bonk!_  
**Pemba**: That’s Avatar Kesh! She was like me, long ago.  
**Mayor Tibbs**: Long ago, Kesh made our Marshal Brag fall in the sea!  
**Mayor Tibbs**: So YOU are in trouble now!  
**Tuk**: He wasn’t even born then!  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.Civ.8.K-2, ask): fairness: blame only for what you did  
> _Is it fair to blame Pemba for something from long ago?_  
> - ✅ No, he didn’t do it → Right. It’s not fair to blame someone for what they didn’t do.
> - Yes → Hmm. Pemba wasn’t even born then. Blaming him isn’t fair.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about whether it is fair to blame someone for something they did not do._

_Two guards put Pemba in a cage. It has flags on it._  
**Nalu**: Let’s find out what really happened. Where are the old papers?  

**Choices**

- **Ask for a fair trial**
    - **Mayor Tibbs**: Of course! Fair, fast, and done by lunch.
- **Let Tuk do the talking**
    - **Tuk**: Mr Mayor. I will speak for Pemba.
    - **Mayor Tibbs**: Have you done this before?
    - **Tuk**: I’ve read a book.
- **Buy a party sweet**
    - It tastes of peppermint. Yum!

#### `q61-trial-done`

_You search the old records room. Dusty, dusty!_  
_Behind the oldest shelf, your Nubbin finds a scroll._  
_It has Marshal Brag’s own stamp on it._  
**Nalu**: It’s his diary! Look at this!  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.His.2.K-2, notice): diaries tell us about long ago  
> **Nalu**: A diary is a book about your days.  
> **Nalu**: People write down what they did and thought.  
> **Nalu**: Old diaries tell us about long ago.  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard how old diaries and records tell us about the past._


#### `q61-evidence`

_The trial is in the square. Mayor Tibbs is the judge._  
_Mayor Tibbs is also everyone else. Hmm._  
**Nalu**: Brag wrote this: “I will take Kesh’s island.”  
**Tuk**: And this: “Note to self: get a bigger hat.”  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.A.2, ask): compare sizes  
> **Tuk**: Brag had a big hat. He wanted a bigger one.  
> **Tuk**: Which is bigger: a hat or a house?  
> - ✅ A house → Yes! A house is much bigger. Even Brag’s hat wasn’t that big.
> - A hat → A hat fits on your head. A house fits you inside! The house is bigger.
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared the sizes of two familiar things._

_Someone takes off a Brag hat. They put it in the bin._  
**Mayor Tibbs**: He does sound a bit like a bully.  
**Nalu**: Kesh didn’t hurt anyone. She looked after her home.  
**Mayor Tibbs**: One more clue and I’ll be sure. Keeper?  

**Choices**

- **Show the stone that thanks Kesh**
    - **Mayor Tibbs**: “Thank you, Kesh, for our peace.” It was here all along!
- **Show the old map**
    - **Mayor Tibbs**: We were on her island! She was looking after us!
- **Say Pemba was kind all day**
    - **Mayor Tibbs**: That isn’t a clue. But it is very true.

#### `q61-evidence-done`

**Mayor Tibbs**: Pemba is not guilty! Let him out!  
**Pemba**: Hooray! Can I keep the flags?  
**Pemba**: Keeper? Should I say sorry for what Kesh did?  
**Pemba**: It wasn’t me. But it sort of was.
