> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, notice): sorry is for what we did; helping is for everyone  
> _We say sorry for things we did._  
> _We don’t have to say sorry for others._  
> _But we can always help make things better._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard the difference between saying sorry for our own actions and helping to fix things._


**Choices**

- **“It’s not your fault.”**
    - **Pemba**: Okay. I’ll just try to be kind.
- **“Not your fault. But you can help fix things.”**
    - **Pemba**: I like that! Fixing is better than blaming.
    - **Tuk**: Put that on a sign!

#### `q61-complete`

_That evening, the old statue comes down._  
_The people make a new one. It looks like Pemba!_  
**Mayor Tibbs**: From now on, it’s Pemba Day! Fewer sticks, more sweets!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.C.6, ask): more and fewer  
> **Mayor Tibbs**: Here are 5 sweets and 2 sticks.  
> **Mayor Tibbs**: Are there more sweets or more sticks?  
> - ✅ More sweets → Yes! Five is more than two. Hooray for sweets!
> - More sticks → Let’s count. Sweets: five. Sticks: two. Five is more. More sweets!
> _For grown-ups: Your child compared two groups to find which has more._

**Pemba**: Best day ever!  
**Tuk**: You’re a party now. Don’t let it go to your head.  
**Mayor Tibbs**: And next time, we’ll read our old papers first.  
_The mayor gives you a painted party mask._  

## Q62. Penguin Sledding

- `q62-s1`: Get Pemba out of the ice. · _Tap the glowing cracks. Cuddly Nubbins warm the ice._
- `q62-s2`: Make friends with a penguin. Slide down the hill! · _Give a fish first. Lean left and right to steer._

#### `q62-iceberg`

_Snow covers the Grove. Everything is white!_  
_By the frozen pond sits a giant block of ice._  
**Tuk**: Glowing ice? Not good news. Let’s leave it.  
**Nalu**: It’s Pemba! He sat so still all night, he froze!  
_Your Nubbin puts its paws on the ice._  

> **Lesson** (science, 2-PS1-4, ask): warmth melts ice  
> **Nalu**: How can we melt the ice?  
> - ✅ Make it warm → Yes! Warmth melts ice. Ice melts back into water. Drip, drip!
> - Make it colder → Colder keeps ice frozen. Brr! Warmth melts ice. Drip, drip!
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that warmth melts ice back into water._

_Under the ice, a blue arrow glows._  
**Nalu**: Please. Let’s help him.  

**Choices**

- **Tap it with a stone**
    - Tap! A thin crack zigzags across the ice.
- **Get your Nubbins to cuddle the ice**
    - The Nubbins snuggle close. They are warm. Drip, drip!
- **Hand Tuk his boomerang**
    - **Tuk**: Fine. But Pemba owes me a snack for this.

#### `q62-iceberg-done`

_Crack! Whoosh! The ice splits open._  
_Out pops Pemba! His eyebrows are white with frost._  
**Pemba**: Brr! Morning, everyone! Is there breakfast?  
**Pemba**: Do you have any penguins?  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): helping a friend who feels cold  
> _Pemba is cold and shivery. What would help him?_  
> - ✅ A warm blanket and a hug → Yes! Warm and snug. Pemba stops shivering. Ahh. Much better.
> - A big handful of snow → Brr! Snow would make him colder. A warm blanket and a hug will help.
> _For grown-ups: Your child noticed that a friend was cold and thought about what would help him._


#### `q62-sled`

**Nalu**: Do we have… what?  
**Pemba**: Penguins! Big ones. Slidey ones!  
**Tuk**: He was frozen all night. And he wants penguins!  
**Nalu**: Pemba, how long were you in there?  
**Pemba**: All night! So quiet. Then so cold. Brr!  
**Pemba**: Penguins look warm. Can I ride one?  
_On the snowy hill, penguins waddle past. Waddle, waddle!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): penguins are birds that swim and slide  
> **Nalu**: Penguins are birds. But they can’t fly.  
> **Nalu**: They swim very fast in the sea.  
> **Nalu**: And they slide on their tummies on the snow!  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that penguins are birds that cannot fly but swim and slide._

**Pemba**: Give them a fish. Then they give you a ride!  
_Your Nubbin holds out a fish to the smallest penguin._  

#### `q62-sled-done`

_Down the hill you go! Whee!_  
_Over a frozen puddle. Into a big snowdrift. Flump!_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.G.A.1, ask): position words: up and down  
> _Did we slide up the hill or down the hill?_  
> - ✅ Down → Yes! Down, down, down. Whee!
> - Up → Sliding up is very hard! We slid down the hill.
> _For grown-ups: Your child used the position words up and down._

_Pemba lands next to you. He is laughing so hard._  
**Pemba**: Again! Again! AGAIN!  

#### `q62-complete`

**Nalu**: I haven’t seen anyone laugh like that in ages.  
**Tuk**: I haven’t seen anyone eat that much snow.  

> **Lesson** (science, K-ESS2-1, notice): snow is frozen water  
> **Nalu**: Snow is frozen water.  
> **Nalu**: It falls when the air is very cold.  
> **Nalu**: Water freezes at 0 °C (32 °F). Brr!  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that snow is frozen water and falls when it is very cold._

**Pemba**: The penguins say come back on snow days!  
**Pemba**: Thank you for the rescue. And for the penguin ride!  
_The penguins waddle home over the hill._  
_The smallest one waves at your Nubbin._  

## Q63. King Rumbold’s Challenges

- `q63-s1`: Get the king’s key from the waterfall pool. · _A Swim Nubbin can dive for it. Or wait until noon._
- `q63-s2`: Find Biscuit the goat-yak. · _Listen for “baa”. Goat-yaks love high places and cabbages._
- `q63-s3`: Ride the stone slides down with King Rumbold. · _Lean into the bends. Duck under the letter slots!_

#### `q63-throne`

_Stonereach is a city cut into a cliff._  
_The king is very old. He has a huge grin._  
**King Rumbold**: Visitors! You will do three challenges!  
**King Rumbold**: If you fail, no pudding!  
**Tuk**: That’s actually quite scary.  
_The king laughs: HEE-HAW! Like a donkey!_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): animal sounds  
> **Pemba**: Which animal says “hee-haw”?  
> - ✅ A donkey → Yes! Donkeys say hee-haw. Just like the king!
> - A cat → Cats say meow. Donkeys say hee-haw!
> _For grown-ups: Your child matched an animal to the sound it makes._

**King Rumbold**: Challenge one! My snack key is in the waterfall. Fetch it!  

**Choices**

- **Say yes to the challenge**
    - **King Rumbold**: Wonderful! Off you go! The fish nibble.
- **Ask why he needs the key**
    - **King Rumbold**: Because I am hungry! Why else?
- **Laugh with him**
    - You laugh. He laughs more. Your Nubbin brays too!

#### `q63-throne-done`

**King Rumbold**: My key! My snacks are safe!  
_He eats a whole cake in one bite. Gulp!_  
**King Rumbold**: Challenge one is done!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.OA.A.1, ask): take away 1  
> **King Rumbold**: Three challenges. One is done. How many are left?  
> _(Hold up three fingers. Fold one down.)_  
> - ✅ 2 → Yes! Two left! Three take away one is two.
> - 3 → Let’s count the fingers still up: one, two. Two left!
> _For grown-ups: Your child took one away from three and found two._

**Tuk**: He didn’t even blink.  

#### `q63-pet`

**King Rumbold**: Challenge two! My pet Biscuit ran away. Find him!  
**Tuk**: What kind of animal is Biscuit?  
**King Rumbold**: A goat-yak. Like a goat, and also like a yak.  
_Somewhere in the city, something goes BAA._  
**Tuk**: Let’s look from the top down. Pemba, take the roofs.  
**Pemba**: I love roofs!  
**King Rumbold**: Or think like Biscuit! Where would a goat go?  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): goats: climbers, and their babies are kids  
> **Pemba**: Goats are great climbers.  
> **Pemba**: They love high, rocky places.  
> **Pemba**: And a baby goat is called a kid!  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that goats are good climbers and that a baby goat is called a kid._

_Your Nubbin climbs the highest wall. It chews a leaf._  

#### `q63-pet-done`

_Biscuit is on a roof! He’s eating a cabbage._  
_Down below, a voice wails. It sounds familiar._  
**King Rumbold**: Biscuit! That’s two challenges!  
**King Rumbold**: Oh dear. Biscuit ate someone else’s cabbage.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Responsible decision-making, ask): making things right  
> **King Rumbold**: What should we do now?  
> - ✅ Say sorry and pay for it → Yes. Sorry, and here are some coins. Far below, a happy voice says thank you.
> - Run away → Hmm. That wouldn’t be fair. We say sorry and pay for it.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about how to make things right when something gets spoilt._


#### `q63-opponent`

**King Rumbold**: Challenge three! The last one. Pick who you face!  
_He points at a big guard. Then a rock beast. Then himself._  

> **Lesson** (maths, ELOF: Mathematics Development, ask): first, second, third  
> **King Rumbold**: First the guard. Second the beast. Who is third?  
> - ✅ The king → Yes! I am third! Third and best!
> - The guard → The guard was first. I am third!
> _For grown-ups: Your child used the order words first, second and third._

**Tuk**: Easy. Don’t pick the beast. Pick the old man.  
**Pemba**: Tuk, I think the old man is the tricky one.  
**King Rumbold**: Choose! I love surprises!  
_Your Nubbin shakes its head at all three._  

**Choices**

- **Pick the guard**
    - **King Rumbold**: Wrong! You face… the MAIL CHUTES!
- **Pick the rock beast**
    - **King Rumbold**: Wrong! You face… the MAIL CHUTES!
- **Pick King Rumbold**
    - **King Rumbold**: Close! But wrong!
    - **King Rumbold**: You face the MAIL CHUTES! And I’m coming too!

#### `q63-opponent-done`

_Whoosh! Down the stone slide you go!_  
_Round and round. Faster and faster!_  
_You land in a pile of letters. Flump!_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, RL.1.4, do): sound words  
> **King Rumbold**: Say the sounds with me! Whoosh!  
> _(Say “whoosh!” and “flump!” in your biggest voice.)_  
> **King Rumbold**: Whoosh! Flump! Great sound words!  
> Check: `edu:say:sound-words:whoosh`
> _For grown-ups: Your child said sound words that show what something sounds like._

**King Rumbold**: Again! Oh wait, I’m the king. I can go any time!  

#### `q63-complete`

**King Rumbold**: You did all three! Do you know why I set them?  
**Pemba**: To see if we were brave?  
**King Rumbold**: No. To see if you would have fun.  
**King Rumbold**: Lots of people forget how to play.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): playing together matters  
> **King Rumbold**: Playing is important. For grown-ups too!  
> **King Rumbold**: We play, we share, we laugh together.  
> _(Ask: what is your favourite game to play?)_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that play matters for everyone and talked about a favourite game._

**Tuk**: That is surprisingly wise.  
**King Rumbold**: I know! Take a bit of my slide. And my laugh!  
_Biscuit says thank you. By eating your hat._  

## Q64. Kaito Says Hello

- `q64-s1`: Help Kaito practise what to say, three times. · _Pick the tip you think helps. Each try gets better._
- `q64-s2`: Stand next to Kaito while he asks to join. · _Stay by the campfire. Kaito feels braver with a friend._

#### `q64-rehearse`

_Behind a pillar, Kaito walks in circles. He talks to a rock._  
**Kaito**: Oh! Keeper. I wasn’t talking to a rock.  
**Kaito**: Fine. I was. I’m practising.  
**Kaito**: I want to ask Pemba if I can join his group.  
**Kaito**: But I get nervous. I get it all wrong.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, ask): what helps when we feel nervous  
> _Kaito feels nervous. What can help?_  
> - ✅ Practise and take slow breaths → Yes! Practice and slow breaths help. I’ll try both.
> - Never try at all → Then he can’t join his friends. Practice and slow breaths help.
> _For grown-ups: Your child named feeling nervous and two things that help: practice and slow breaths._

_Your Nubbin sits on the rock. It listens._  
**Kaito**: Pemba. Hello. I am a person. Now a good one. Probably.  

**Choices**

- **Say it’s a bit stiff**
    - **Kaito**: Stiff. Right. I can try less stiff.
- **Say it’s a good start**
    - **Kaito**: Really? Thank you.
    - It is a start. Starts are good.
- **Say: try smiling**
    - Kaito smiles. It looks a bit wobbly.
    - **Kaito**: Too much?

#### `q64-rehearse-done`

**Kaito**: Try two: “Pemba, I brought you a gift. The gift is me.”  
**Kaito**: No. Try three: “I’m sorry. I want to help.”  
_It is the shortest one. It is the best one._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, SL.K.1, ask): speaking clearly: short and kind  
> **Kaito**: Which is better: a long, muddled speech or a short, kind one?  
> - ✅ Short and kind → Short and kind. Got it.
> - Long and muddled → Long and muddled is hard to follow. Short and kind. Got it.
> _For grown-ups: Your child thought about how to say something clearly and kindly._

**Kaito**: Short and friendly. Short and friendly.  
**Kaito**: Or maybe I just wave? Waving is friendly.  

#### `q64-ask`

_Pemba, Nalu, Tuk and Bao sit by a campfire._  
_Kaito steps out. Everyone jumps!_  
**Bao**: Relax. His heart is beating fast. He’s scared.  

> **Lesson** (health, CASEL: Self-management, notice): feelings and our bodies: a fast heartbeat  
> **Bao**: When we feel scared, our hearts beat fast.  
> **Bao**: Slow breaths help them calm down.  
> _(Take one slow breath together.)_  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that fear can make our hearts beat fast, and that slow breaths help._

_Kaito does a stiff little wave. His hand stays up._  
**Kaito**: Um. Hello. It’s me. Kaito.  
**Kaito**: That was not the speech. Not any of them.  
**Nalu**: What do you want, Kaito?  
_Everyone looks at you. Even your Nubbin._  

**Choices**

- **Say he practised for hours**
    - **Pemba**: Practising? For us? That’s so nice!
- **Nudge him to try again**
    - **Kaito**: I’m sorry. I want to help. Can I be on your side?
- **Wave back**
    - You wave back. Pemba waves too. Bao waves the wrong way, as a joke.

#### `q64-ask-done`

**Pemba**: You can join! Welcome to the team!  
**Kaito**: Really? Just like that?  
**Tuk**: Not just like that. There will be rules. On paper.  
**Nalu**: I haven’t forgotten what you did, Kaito.  
**Kaito**: That’s fair. I want the chance to do better.  
**Nalu**: …One chance.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): sorry, then showing you mean it  
> _Kaito said sorry. Nalu gave him a chance._  
> _Saying sorry is the start._  
> _Doing kind things shows you mean it._  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that an apology starts with words and is shown by kind actions._


#### `q64-complete`

_Later, Kaito finds you by the pillar._  
**Kaito**: Thank you. For listening. And for the rock.  
**Kaito**: I’m still not good at talking to people.  
**Kaito**: But I got a bit better today. I tried three times.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.4, do): count to 3  
> **Kaito**: Count my tries with me.  
> _(Hold up a finger for each try.)_  
> **Kaito**: One, two, three. Each one got better!  
> Check: `edu:count:tries:3`
> _For grown-ups: Your child counted three tries on their fingers._

_He waves. Stiff and wobbly. Your Nubbin copies him!_  

## Q65. The White Lotus

- `q65-s1`: Learn the tile game with Uncle Tenzo. · _Flowers in the middle. Rocks at the edge. He will help you._
- `q65-s2`: Play a game of tiles with Uncle Tenzo. · _Save the lotus tile for last. Nothing can block it._

#### `q65-tiles`

_Rain pitters and patters on the leaves._  
_Under the big tree, Uncle Tenzo has a round board._  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Keeper! Will you play with me? It’s no fun alone.  
_The tiles show flowers, rocks and wind._  
_One special tile shows a white lotus flower._  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Flowers grow. Rocks block. Wind moves things.  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.B.3, do): sort into groups  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: Let’s sort the tiles into three piles.  
> _(Put flowers together, rocks together, wind together.)_  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: Flowers here. Rocks there. Wind over there. Lovely!  
> Check: `edu:sort:tiles:flower-rock-wind`
> _For grown-ups: Your child sorted tiles into three groups by what they show._

**Uncle Tenzo**: The lotus does nothing. That’s why it matters most.  
**Kaito**: Uncle, that makes it more confusing.  
_Your Nubbin sniffs a flower tile. It’s wood. Oh._  

**Choices**

- **Ask him to teach you**
    - **Uncle Tenzo**: Gladly. Lesson one: don’t rush a tile.
- **Ask what the lotus does**
    - **Uncle Tenzo**: Patience. The quiet ones often matter most.
- **Ask if there are snacks**
    - **Uncle Tenzo**: Always! A game with no snacks is no fun.

#### `q65-tiles-done`

**Uncle Tenzo**: Good. You don’t rush. You look at the whole board.  
**Kaito**: He says that to everyone.  
**Uncle Tenzo**: I have never said it to you, nephew.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, ask): taking turns  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: In a game, how do we play fairly?  
> - ✅ Take turns → Yes. My turn, then your turn. Waiting for your turn takes patience.
> - Everyone goes at once → Then the tiles get very muddled! We take turns. My turn, then your turn.
> _For grown-ups: Your child practised the idea of taking turns and waiting patiently._

**Kaito**: …Hm.  

#### `q65-game`

**Uncle Tenzo**: Now a real game. I won’t go easy. I’ll go medium.  
_The rain falls harder. Splish, splash!_  

> **Lesson** (science, 2-LS2-1, ask): plants need water  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: Rain is falling. Who is happy about the rain?  
> - ✅ The plants → Yes! Plants need water to grow. Rain falls from clouds and gives them a drink.
> - The kettle → Ha! The kettle has enough water. Plants need rain to grow.
> _For grown-ups: Your child learned that plants need water, and that rain gives it to them._

_Kaito falls asleep against the tree. Snore._  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Every tile matters. But no tile wins alone.  
**Uncle Tenzo**: If you lose, don’t sulk. You learned something.  
_Your Nubbins watch every move._  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Hmm. Clever move. Did you mean it?  

**Choices**

- **Say you meant it**
    - **Uncle Tenzo**: Then I’m in trouble!
- **Say it was luck**
    - **Uncle Tenzo**: Honest! I like honest players.
- **Point at your Nubbin**
    - **Uncle Tenzo**: A clever Nubbin! I should have known!

#### `q65-game-done`

_You put down your last tile._  
_Flowers bloom all over the board._  
**Uncle Tenzo**: You win! Fair and square! What a happy day!  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Look at the pattern you made.  

> **Lesson** (maths, ELOF: Mathematics Development, ask): patterns  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: Flower, rock, flower, rock, flower… what comes next?  
> - ✅ Rock → Yes! Flower, rock, flower, rock. A pattern!
> - Flower → Let’s say it again: flower, rock, flower… Rock comes next!
> _For grown-ups: Your child continued an AB pattern._


#### `q65-complete`

**Uncle Tenzo**: Keep this. The white lotus tile.  
_It is old and smooth. And a little warm._  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): the lotus: a pond plant  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: A lotus is a flower that grows in ponds.  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: Its roots hold on in the mud.  
> **Uncle Tenzo**: Its flowers open up above the water.  
> _For grown-ups: Your child heard that a lotus is a pond plant rooted in mud, with flowers above the water._

**Uncle Tenzo**: If you see this tile on a door, you are among friends.  
**Uncle Tenzo**: Old friends who play slow games. And help.  
**Kaito**: Uncle. Are you in a secret club?  
**Uncle Tenzo**: A tea club. Mostly. Go back to sleep, nephew.  
_Your Nubbin carries the board home on its head._  

## LH1. Shalom, Ori!

- `lh1-step1`: Say shalom to Ori at the gate. · _Go to the gate and tap Ori. Wave and say shalom!_
- `lh1-step2`: Teach three Nubbins to wave at shalom. · _Stand by a Nubbin. Say shalom and wave. When it waves back, say toda!_
- `lh1-step3`: Say laila tov to everyone. · _Find Fern, Clover and Nova. Tap each one and say laila tov._

#### `lh1-s1`

_Rattle, rattle! Someone is stuck at the gate._  
**Nova**: Lift and push, Ori! Lift and push!  
_Creak! The gate opens. In tumbles a boy with a big tin._  
**Ori**: שָׁלוֹם (shalom, “hello”)! Your gate is very strong.  
_(Say it: sha-LOME. Wave as you say it!)_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): say hello in Hebrew: call and response  
> **Ori**: I say shalom. You say shalom!  
> **Ori**: Shalom!  
> _(Your turn. Wave and say: sha-LOME!)_  
> **Ori**: Perfect! A big wave and a big shalom.  
> Check: `edu:say:he:shalom`
> _For grown-ups: Said “shalom” (hello) back to Ori, with a wave, in a call-and-response game._

**Nova**: This is Ori. He lives in Israel. He is ten.  
**Ori**: Shalom means hello. It means goodbye too!  
_A round Nubbin waves at Ori. Wave, wave… bump! It falls over._  
**Ori**: I can ask “How are you?” two ways. Which way?  

**Choices**

- **Ask me “ma shlomkha?” (the boy way).**
    - **Ori**: מָה שְׁלוֹמְךָ? (ma shlomkha?, “how are you?”)
    - (Say it: mah shlom-KHA.)
    - **You**: בְּסֵדֶר (beseder, “fine”)!
    - (Say it: beh-SEH-der.)
    - **Ori**: Beseder! That means OK. Fine!
- **Ask me “ma shlomekh?” (the girl way).**
    - **Ori**: מָה שְׁלוֹמֵךְ? (ma shlomekh?, “how are you?”)
    - (Say it: mah shlo-MEKH.)
    - **You**: בְּסֵדֶר (beseder, “fine”)!
    - (Say it: beh-SEH-der.)
    - **Ori**: Beseder! That means OK. Fine!
- **Either way. Just say shalom!**
    - **Ori**: Shalom, then! When I ask how you are, say בְּסֵדֶר (beseder, “fine”).
    - (Say it: beh-SEH-der.)
    - **You**: Beseder!
    - **Ori**: Perfect! You sound like my uncle.

#### `lh1-s1-done`

**Ori**: You said it! A big shalom!  
_The round Nubbin waves again. Bump! Down it goes._  
_It lies on the grass. It looks very pleased._  
**Ori**: I will call it the Wobbler. It is my favourite.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Social awareness, ask): helping someone new feel welcome  
> **Ori**: I’m new here. Everything is new!  
> _What helps a new friend feel welcome?_  
> - ✅ Smile and say shalom → Shalom! That feels nice. Thank you.
> - Run away and hide → Feeling shy is OK. A little wave helps too!
> _For grown-ups: Talked about how to help someone new feel welcome: a smile and a hello._

**Ori**: Shh. Don’t tell the other Nubbins.  

#### `lh1-s2`

**Ori**: Nubbin school! I’m the coach. You help!  
_Three Nubbins: the Wobbler, a shy one, a sleepy one._  
**Ori**: Rule one: say thank you. תּוֹדָה (toda, “thank you”)!  
_(Say it: toe-DAH.)_  
**Ori**: Rule two: say please. בְּבַקָּשָׁה (bevakasha, “please”)!  
_(Say it: beh-vah-kah-SHAH. It’s a long one!)_  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, do): saying please and thank you  
> **Ori**: I say toda. You say toda!  
> _(Your turn: toe-DAH!)_  
> **Ori**: Now bevakasha. Please!  
> _(Your turn: beh-vah-kah-SHAH!)_  
> **Ori**: Toda! You have very good manners.  
> Check: `edu:say:he:toda`
> _For grown-ups: Said “toda” (thank you) and “bevakasha” (please) back to Ori, and talked about good manners._

**Ori**: When they wave, we cheer טוֹב (tov, “good”)!  
_(Say it: TOVE. Cheer it loud!)_  
_The shy Nubbin hides behind a dandelion. Peek… gone._  
_The Wobbler waves at nobody. Bump! Up again._  

**Choices**

- **Sit by the shy one and wait.**
    - You sit. You wait. Very quietly.
    - A tiny paw lifts. A little wave!
    - **Ori**: כֵּן (ken, “yes”)! She waved! Toda!
    - (Say it: KEN. A happy yes!)
- **Whisper shalom very softly.**
    - You whisper: shalom.
    - The shy one tilts her head. A tiny paw lifts!
    - **Ori**: כֵּן (ken, “yes”)! Quiet shalom for a quiet Nubbin.
    - (Say it: KEN. A happy yes!)

#### `lh1-s2-done`

_The shy one waves. The sleepy one waves in its sleep._  
_The Wobbler waves. Bump! It waves from the ground._  
**Ori**: Three Nubbins! Toda, toda, toda!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 3  
> _(Count the waving Nubbins together. Touch each one.)_  
> **Ori**: One, two, three! Three Nubbins waving. Tov!  
> Check: `edu:count:nubbins:3`
> _For grown-ups: Counted three Nubbins, touching each one once._

**You**: Bevakasha!  
**Ori**: Bevakasha means please. And you’re welcome! A busy word.  
**Ori**: Can the Wobbler stand up yet? לֹא (lo, “no”)!  
_(Say it: LOW. Shake your head as you say it.)_  

#### `lh1-s3`

_The sun goes down. The lanterns blink on._  
**Ori**: Hebrew has a “good” for each time of day.  
**Ori**: Morning is בֹּקֶר טוֹב (boker tov, “good morning”).  
_(Say it: BO-ker TOVE.)_  
**Ori**: Evening is עֶרֶב טוֹב (erev tov, “good evening”). Like now!  
_(Say it: EH-rev TOVE.)_  
**Ori**: Bedtime is לַיְלָה טוֹב (laila tov, “good night”).  
_(Say it: LIE-la TOVE. Give a big yawn!)_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): the order of the day: morning, evening, night  
> **Ori**: First morning. Then evening. What comes next?  
> - ✅ Night! Laila tov! → Yes! Night comes after evening. Laila tov!
> - Morning again → Morning comes back after the night. Evening, then night, then morning!
> _For grown-ups: Put the parts of the day in order: morning, evening, night._

**Nova**: Say laila tov to everyone. Fern is in the orchard.  
_The Wobbler yawns, waves, and falls asleep where it lands._  

#### `lh1-s3-done`

**Fern**: Laila tov! Did I say it right?  
**Ori**: Perfect, Fern! Toda!  
_Clover and Nova say laila tov too._  
_Three Nubbins sleep in a heap by Ori’s tent._  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, notice): why we sleep  
> **Ori**: Look. The Nubbins are fast asleep.  
> **Ori**: Sleep helps our bodies rest and grow. Nubbins too!  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that sleep helps our bodies rest and grow._

**Ori**: Laila tov, everybody. Tomorrow: shapes!  

#### `lh1-complete`

_Morning! A new sign hangs on the gate._  
**Ori**: Boker tov! I made you a sign. It says shalom!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): trace the shape ש (shin)  
> **Ori**: Look at this shape: ש. It’s called shin.  
> **Ori**: This shape says “sh”, like shalom.  
> **Ori**: Three arms up, like a goalie making a save!  
> _(Trace it with a finger. Start on the right side.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:he:shin`
> _For grown-ups: Traced the Hebrew letter shape ש (shin) and heard that it says “sh”, as in shalom. Tracing only, not reading._

**Ori**: I made up a chant too. Say it after me!  
**Ori**: “Boker tov, the sun is out!  
**Ori**: Shalom, shalom, wave and shout!  
**Ori**: Erev tov, the lanterns glow,  
**Ori**: laila tov, and off we go!”  

> **Lesson** (music, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): a call-and-response chant with actions  
> _(Say the chant line by line. Your child says each line back.)_  
> _(Stretch up on “boker tov”. Wave on “shalom”. Yawn on “laila tov”.)_  
> **Ori**: Again! Louder! The Nubbins are joining in!  
> Check: `edu:chant:he:lh1`
> _For grown-ups: Joined in an original Hebrew greetings chant, line by line, with actions._

_The Wobbler waves with both arms. Wobble, wobble… it stays up!_  
**Ori**: It stayed up! Ken! Toda, Wobbler!  

## LH2. Right to Left

- `lh2-step1`: Find four painted pebbles round the garden. · _Look by the pond, the gate, the orchard and Ori’s tent. Line them up from the right._
- `lh2-step2`: Trace a shape in the sand with Ori. · _Ori draws it first. Go over it with a stick. Start on the right side._
- `lh2-step3`: Help Ori paint his name on his tent. · _Tap the paintbrush on the tent. Ori helps. Start on the right side._

#### `lh2-s1`

**Ori**: שָׁלוֹם (shalom, “hello”)! Today: Hebrew letters!  
_(Say it: sha-LOME. Wave!)_  
**Ori**: Hebrew goes the other way. Right to left!  
_(Hold up your right hand. Hebrew starts on that side.)_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): Hebrew goes right to left  
> _(Run a finger across the page, from right to left.)_  
> **Ori**: Whoosh! That’s the Hebrew way. Right to left.  
> _(Now left to right. That’s the English way.)_  
> Check: `edu:point:he:right-to-left`
> _For grown-ups: Showed with a finger that Hebrew is written right to left, and English left to right._

_The Wobbler hears “right to left”. It walks backwards!_  
**Ori**: I painted shapes on four pebbles. Together they make shalom.  
**Ori**: I hid them. By the pond, the gate, the orchard and my tent.  
_The Wobbler sets off to help. Backwards. Bump!_  

#### `lh2-s1-done`

_Four pebbles sit on the path. Clink, clink, clink, clink._  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 4  
> _(Count the pebbles together on your fingers.)_  
> **Ori**: One, two, three, four! Four shapes make shalom.  
> Check: `edu:count:pebbles:4`
> _For grown-ups: Counted four pebbles, one finger for each._

**Ori**: You found them all! תּוֹדָה (toda, “thank you”)!  
_(Say it: toe-DAH.)_  
**Ori**: Now line them up. Start on the right!  
_The Wobbler arrives, backwards. It sits on a pebble._  
**Ori**: Off, Wobbler! That pebble is part of shalom.  

#### `lh2-s2`

_By the pond, the mud is dry and smooth. Like sand._  
**Ori**: Hebrew letters are called the alef-bet.  
**Ori**: The first shape is alef. Here it is: א.  
**Ori**: Alef is the quiet shape. It makes no sound!  
**Ori**: It holds a sound for its friends. Like a cup holds tea.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): trace the shape א (alef)  
> _(Trace א with a finger. Start on the right side.)_  
> **Ori**: A quiet shape. Shh!  
> _(Put a finger on your lips. Alef is quiet.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:he:alef`
> _For grown-ups: Traced the Hebrew letter shape א (alef), the first letter, which makes no sound of its own. Tracing only, not reading._

**Ori**: The next letter is called bet. I’ll draw that one.  
_The Wobbler walks backwards. Plop! Into the pond._  
_It climbs out, dripping and delighted._  
**Ori**: Your stick is ready. Alef first, or bet first?  

**Choices**

- **Alef first. The quiet one.**
    - **Ori**: Alef first. The quiet ones go first today.
- **Bet first!**
    - **Ori**: Bet first! I’ll draw bet. Then you trace alef.

#### `lh2-s2-done`

_In the sand: a wobbly alef. It looks very proud._  
**Ori**: Alef! The first letter. And the quiet one.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, notice): first tries can be wobbly, and that is fine  
> **Ori**: Your alef is a bit wobbly. That’s fine!  
> **Ori**: First tries are wobbly. Mine were too. You keep going.  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that first tries are often wobbly, and that practice helps._

**Ori**: Next, the best word in the world. My name!  
_The Wobbler shakes itself dry. All over Ori._  
**Ori**: Thank you, Wobbler. Very refreshing.  

#### `lh2-s3`

_Ori’s tent is green and a bit crooked._  
_A football scarf hangs on the pole._  
**Ori**: My name in Hebrew is אוֹרִי (Ori, “my light”).  
_(Say it: OH-ree.)_  
**Ori**: אוֹר (or) means light. Ori means “my light”.  
_(Say it: ORE. Like the oar on a rowing boat.)_  
**Ori**: I love light. Stars are my favourite!  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): things that give light  
> **Ori**: Or means light. What gives us light?  
> - ✅ A star → Yes! Stars shine. So do the sun and lanterns.
> - A stone → A stone doesn’t shine. Stars, the sun and lanterns do!
> _For grown-ups: Talked about things that give light, like stars, the sun and lanterns._

_The Wobbler watches closely. It is facing the wrong way._  
**Ori**: Help me paint my name. Start on the right!  

**Choices**

- **Paint it big!**
    - **Ori**: Big! You can see it from the hill!
- **Paint it small and neat.**
    - **Ori**: Small and neat. Like a famous person’s name.
- **Paint it, then add a star.**
    - **Ori**: A star! A light next to “my light”!

#### `lh2-s3-done`

_On the tent, in fresh paint: אוֹרִי._  
**Ori**: That’s me! You helped paint my name.  
**Ori**: Nobody here has done that before.  

> **Lesson** (sel, ELOF: Social and Emotional Development, ask): noticing how someone feels  
> _Look at Ori’s face. How does he feel?_  
> - ✅ Happy and proud → Very happy. My name, on my tent, by my friend!
> - Cross → Look at his big smile. Ori feels happy!
> _For grown-ups: Looked at Ori’s face and named his feeling: happy and proud._

_The Wobbler turns round at last. It waves at the name._  

#### `lh2-complete`

_At dusk, Ori lays flat stones on the path._  
**Ori**: Stepping stones! Each one has a letter.  
**Ori**: Hebrew has twenty-two letters. You met alef!  
**Ori**: Hop along them, right to left!  

> **Lesson** (health, ELOF: Perceptual, Motor, and Physical Development, do): hopping in one direction  
> _(Stand up! Hop from right to left together. Hop, hop, hop!)_  
> **Ori**: Right to left. The Hebrew way!  
> Check: `edu:hop:stones:right-to-left`
> _For grown-ups: Hopped from right to left, the way Hebrew is written._

_The Wobbler hops too. Backwards, of course._  
_It does not fall in the pond. Not once!_  
**Ori**: Shalom, stones! See you tomorrow.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, ask): review: which way Hebrew goes  
> _The Wobbler is walking backwards again._  
> **Ori**: Which way does Hebrew go?  
> - ✅ Right to left → Right to left! Whoosh!
> - Left to right → That’s English! Hebrew goes right to left. Whoosh!
> _For grown-ups: Remembered that Hebrew goes right to left._


## LH3. Counting Plums

- `lh3-step1`: Help Fern count plums in Hebrew. · _Tap each plum as it goes in the basket. Say its number with Ori._
- `lh3-step2`: Play Ori’s “How Many?” football game. · _Watch each goal. Then tap the number. Ori helps if you get stuck._
- `lh3-step3`: Count ten stars with Ori. · _Go up the hill after dark. Tap a star to count it._

#### `lh3-s1`

_Fern’s plum trees are full. Plop! Plop!_  
**Fern**: I need ten plums in each basket. I keep losing count!  
**Ori**: I’ll help. I count in Hebrew!  
**Ori**: אַחַת (achat, “one”). שְׁתַּיִם (shtayim, “two”). שָׁלוֹשׁ (shalosh, “three”).  
_(Say them: ah-KHAT, SHTAI-yim, sha-LOSH.)_  
**Ori**: אַרְבַּע (arba, “four”). חָמֵשׁ (chamesh, “five”).  
_(Say them: AR-bah, kha-MESH. The “kh” is throaty, like “loch”.)_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 5 in Hebrew  
> _(Hold up one finger for each plum.)_  
> **Ori**: Achat, shtayim, shalosh, arba, chamesh!  
> _(Say them again together, with your fingers.)_  
> **Fern**: Five plums! Halfway there!  
> Check: `edu:count:he:plums:5`
> _For grown-ups: Counted five plums in Hebrew (achat to chamesh), one finger for each._

**Fern**: Shtayim… it sounds like a sneeze!  
_The Wobbler catches the falling plums. Mostly with its face._  

**Choices**

- **I’ll count. You check.**
    - **Ori**: Deal! I’ll cheer every number.
- **Let Fern count. We’ll cheer.**
    - **Fern**: No pressure, then!
    - **Ori**: Big cheers! Achat!

#### `lh3-s1-done`

_Plop, plop, plop. The basket fills up._  
**Fern**: Ten plums! What is ten in Hebrew?  
**Ori**: עֶשֶׂר (eser, “ten”)! Ten is my favourite number.  
_(Say it: EH-ser. Hold up ten fingers!)_  
**Ori**: It’s my football shirt number.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): asking for help  
> **Fern**: I lost count, so I asked Ori for help.  
> **Fern**: What can you do when something is tricky?  
> - ✅ Ask a friend for help → Yes! Asking for help is brave and clever.
> - Give up → Let’s not give up. Asking a friend helps!
> _For grown-ups: Talked about asking a friend for help when something is tricky._

_The Wobbler has a plum on its head. Nobody mentions it._  

#### `lh3-s2`

_Ori puts four jumpers on the grass. Two goals!_  
**Ori**: The game is called “How Many?” I shoot. You count!  
**Ori**: Big numbers now. שֵׁשׁ (shesh, “six”). שֶׁבַע (sheva, “seven”).  
_(Say them: SHESH, SHEH-vah.)_  
**Ori**: שְׁמוֹנֶה (shmone, “eight”). תֵּשַׁע (tesha, “nine”). And eser, ten!  
_(Say them: shmo-NEH, TEH-sha, EH-ser.)_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): say the Hebrew numbers 6 to 10: call and response  
> **Ori**: I say shesh. You say shesh!  
> _(Ori says each number. Your child says it back.)_  
> **Ori**: Shesh! Sheva! Shmone! Tesha! Eser!  
> **Ori**: Perfect! You count like a referee.  
> Check: `edu:say:he:numbers:6-10`
> _For grown-ups: Repeated the Hebrew numbers six to ten (shesh to eser) after Ori._

_The Wobbler is in goal. It waves at the ball._  
**Ori**: I am very good at football. And very bad at losing.  
**Ori**: Ready? Achat, shtayim, shalosh, GO!  

**Choices**

- **Go easy on me!**
    - **Ori**: Never! …OK, a little bit. For one shot.
- **Bring it on!**
    - **Ori**: Now you sound like a footballer!

#### `lh3-s2-done`

_Ori scores ten goals. Eser!_  
_Then the ball bounces off the Wobbler’s tummy._  
_It rolls all the way down the grass. Into Ori’s goal!_  
**Ori**: The Wobbler scored one? Achat? ACHAT?  
**Ori**: Best of ten! Best of twenty!  
**Nova**: Ori.  
**Ori**: …בְּסֵדֶר (beseder, “OK”). Well played, Wobbler.  
_(Say it: beh-SEH-der.)_  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-management, ask): being a good sport  
> _Ori let in one goal. He feels cross._  
> _What can we say when a game ends?_  
> - ✅ Well played! → Well played. You’re right. That feels better.
> - I’m the best! → Hmm. That’s what I nearly said. “Well played” is kinder.
> _For grown-ups: Talked about being a good sport: saying “well played” after a game, even when cross._


#### `lh3-s3`

_Night. You and Ori climb the hill with a blanket._  
**Ori**: Look up! So many stars!  
**Ori**: A star is a כּוֹכָב (kochav, “star”).  
_(Say it: ko-KHAV. Point up at the sky!)_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, notice): we see stars at night  
> **Ori**: We see stars at night, when the sky is dark.  
> **Ori**: In the day, the sun is too bright to see them.  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that we see stars at night, when the sky is dark, because the sun is too bright in the day._

**Ori**: And that’s the יָרֵחַ (yareach, “moon”). So bright!  
_(Say it: yah-REH-akh.)_  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): trace the shape י (yod)  
> **Ori**: Here’s a tiny shape: י. It’s called yod.  
> **Ori**: In yareach, this shape says “y”.  
> **Ori**: It’s the smallest letter. Small, like a faraway star.  
> _(Trace it with a finger. Start on the right side.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:he:yod`
> _For grown-ups: Traced the Hebrew letter shape י (yod), the smallest letter, which says “y” in yareach. Tracing only, not reading._

_Something in a tree says “hoo”. Two round eyes blink._  
**Ori**: יַנְשׁוּף (yanshuf, “owl”)! This owl is awake at night. Like me!  
_(Say it: yan-SHOOF. Now hoot like an owl!)_  
**Ori**: Let’s count ten stars. Point at one!  

**Choices**

- **Start with the brightest star.**
    - **Ori**: The bright one first. Achat!
- **Start with the faintest star.**
    - **Ori**: The faint ones are brave. They shine anyway. Achat!

#### `lh3-s3-done`

_One star. Two stars. You point, Ori counts._  
**Ori**: Achat, shtayim, shalosh… all the way up!  
**Ori**: …shmone, tesha, eser! Ten stars!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 10 in Hebrew  
> _(Count ten stars again together. Touch each one.)_  
> **Ori**: Achat, shtayim, shalosh, arba, chamesh!  
> **Ori**: Shesh, sheva, shmone, tesha, eser!  
> **Ori**: Ten stars, all counted!  
> Check: `edu:count:he:stars:10`
> _For grown-ups: Counted ten stars in Hebrew, achat to eser, touching each one once._

_The yanshuf hoots once. Hoo!_  
**Ori**: My grandma counts stars with me at home.  
_The Wobbler lies on its back. It waves at the kochav._  

#### `lh3-complete`

_Morning. Fern pins a chart on the orchard gate._  
_Ten plums in a row, with Hebrew numbers._  
**Fern**: I’ll count in Hebrew for every plum I sell!  
**Ori**: Countdown, like a rocket! Ten to one!  

> **Lesson** (music, do): a countdown chant with actions  
> _(Crouch down low. Count down with Ori, slowly.)_  
> _(On “achat”, jump up like a rocket!)_  
> **Ori**: Again! Faster! Eser… tesha… shmone…  
> Check: `edu:chant:he:countdown`
> _For grown-ups: Chanted the Hebrew numbers from ten down to one, crouching low and jumping up on “achat”._

**Ori**: Eser, tesha, shmone, sheva, shesh!  
**Ori**: Chamesh, arba, shalosh, shtayim, achat!  
_On “achat”, the Wobbler jumps… into the plum basket!_  
**Ori**: Lift-off! Well. Plum-off.  
**Ori**: For you: my star chart, with a yanshuf on it. And my football!  
**Ori**: The Wobbler earned it, really. Don’t tell it.  

## LH4. Colours of the Orchard

- `lh4-step1`: Sort Nova’s fruit by colour. · _Red fruit in one basket, green in one, yellow in one._
- `lh4-step2`: Find something red for Clover’s Nubbin. · _Look in Nova’s crate for something red. Adom!_
- `lh4-step3`: Paint Fern’s fence in three colours. · _Listen to Ori. Dip the brush in the colour he says._

#### `lh4-s1`

_Nova drops a crate on the table. Thud! Fruit!_  
**Nova**: One of everything from the market. I was hungry.  
**Ori**: Sorting game! Colours in Hebrew. Ready?  
**Ori**: אָדֹם (adom, “red”). יָרֹק (yarok, “green”). צָהֹב (tsahov, “yellow”).  
_(Say them: ah-DOME, yah-ROKE, tsa-HOVE.)_  
**Ori**: תַּפּוּחַ (tapuach, “apple”). בָּנָנָה (banana, “banana”). Easy one!  
**Ori**: לִימוֹן (limon, “lemon”). Another easy one!  
_(Say them: tah-POO-akh, bah-NAH-nah, lee-MONE.)_  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.MD.B.3, do): sort by colour and count each group  
> _(Point to the yellow fruit. Now the red. Now the green.)_  
> **Ori**: The banana and the limon go in tsahov!  
> **Ori**: A red tapuach goes in adom!  
> _(How many yellow fruits? Count them together.)_  
> Check: `edu:sort:fruit:colour`
> _For grown-ups: Sorted fruit into red, green and yellow groups, and counted a group._

_The Wobbler hugs a banana. It will not share._  

**Choices**

- **Put the green apple in yarok.**
    - **Ori**: Yarok! Apples can be green too.
- **Put the red apple in adom.**
    - **Ori**: Adom! A red tapuach, like in a picture book.

#### `lh4-s1-done`

_Three piles: adom, yarok, tsahov._  
_And a little pile under the table. The Wobbler guards it._  
**Ori**: The Wobbler made up a new colour. It’s called “mine”!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): sharing  
> **Ori**: The Wobbler won’t share the fruit.  
> **Ori**: What could we say to it?  
> - ✅ Let’s share, please! → Kind and polite. Look, it gives back one banana!
> - Grab it! → Grabbing makes Nubbins sad. Asking nicely works better.
> _For grown-ups: Talked about asking nicely to share instead of grabbing._

**Nova**: A very popular colour. I see it at every market.  

#### `lh4-s2`

_Clover sits by the pond with a little Nubbin._  
**Clover**: This one only eats red things. We all have our ways.  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, notice): animals need food and water  
> **Clover**: She hasn’t eaten all day.  
> **Clover**: Every animal needs food and water. Even fussy ones!  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that every animal needs food and water to live._

**Ori**: Something adom! I’m fast. I play on the wing!  
**Ori**: רִמּוֹן (rimon, “pomegranate”). Red, with red seeds inside!  
_(Say it: ree-MONE.)_  
**Ori**: עֲנָבִים (anavim, “grapes”). These are סָגֹל (sagol, “purple”).  
_(Say them: ah-nah-VEEM, sah-GOLE.)_  
_A צִפּוֹר (tsipor, “bird”) lands on the crate. Peck, peck!_  
_(Say it: tsee-POR. Tweet tweet!)_  
**Ori**: Tsipor! One bite. Then shoo!  

**Choices**

- **Take the rimon.**
    - **Clover**: So very adom! She’s sniffing it already.
- **Take a red tapuach.**
    - **Clover**: A red apple! She’s sniffing it already.
- **Take the anavim.**
    - The Nubbin sniffs the grapes. She turns away, slowly.
    - **Ori**: Sagol, not adom! Here, take the rimon instead.

#### `lh4-s2-done`

_The fussy Nubbin eats the red fruit. Slowly. Then: more!_  
**Clover**: תּוֹדָה (toda, “thank you”)! Did I say it right?  
_(Say it: toe-DAH.)_  
**Ori**: Perfect toda! I’d give you a medal. I’m out of medals.  

> **Lesson** (art, do): find the colour red and name it in Hebrew  
> _(Look around you. Find something red.)_  
> _(Point to it and say: ah-DOME!)_  
> **Ori**: Adom! You found it!  
> Check: `edu:point:colour:adom`
> _For grown-ups: Found something red nearby and named the colour in Hebrew: adom._

_The tsipor flies off with one seed. Tweet!_  

#### `lh4-s3`

_Fern’s fence is grey and peeling. Poor fence._  
**Fern**: Pick three colours. I’m bad at choosing!  
**Ori**: I call a colour. You paint it!  
**Ori**: כָּחֹל (kachol, “blue”). שָׁחֹר (shachor, “black”).  
_(Say them: kah-KHOLE, sha-KHOR.)_  
**Ori**: כָּתֹם (katom, “orange”). וָרֹד (varod, “pink”).  
_(Say them: kah-TOME, vah-RODE.)_  

> **Lesson** (maths, ELOF: Mathematics Development, ask): a colour pattern: what comes next?  
> **Ori**: Stripes! Kachol, varod, kachol, varod…  
> **Ori**: What comes next?  
> - ✅ Kachol (blue) → Kachol! Blue comes next. A pattern!
> - Varod (pink) → Listen again: kachol, varod, kachol, varod… then kachol!
> _For grown-ups: Continued a blue-pink colour pattern._

_A פַּרְפַּר (parpar, “butterfly”) lands on the blue tin._  
_(Say it: par-PAR. Flap your hands like wings!)_  
_Splash! The Wobbler falls in the varod tin. Now it’s pink!_  

**Choices**

- **A sunset fence, please.**
    - **Ori**: Katom, varod, sagol. Like the sky at home.
- **A night-sky fence.**
    - **Ori**: Shachor, kachol, and tsahov for stars!
- **Surprise me, Ori!**
    - **Ori**: Varod, yarok, katom. Art doesn’t argue!

#### `lh4-s3-done`

_The fence has three stripes now. Wobbly ones._  
**Fern**: It’s loud. I love it!  
**Fern**: I’d never have picked it. That’s why I love it.  
_The parpar flutters off. The Wobbler is still varod._  
**Ori**: Pink till the next rain. It looks very pleased.  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): trace the shape ל (lamed)  
> **Ori**: Look at this tall shape: ל. It’s called lamed.  
> **Ori**: In limon, this shape says “l”.  
> **Ori**: It’s the tallest letter. It peeks over the fence!  
> _(Trace it with a finger. Start on the right side.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:he:lamed`
> _For grown-ups: Traced the Hebrew letter shape ל (lamed), the tallest letter, which says “l” in limon. Tracing only, not reading._


#### `lh4-complete`

_That evening, Ori gives you some tiny seeds._  
**Ori**: Rimon seeds! Plant one. One day, a rimon tree!  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, notice): seeds grow into plants  
> **Ori**: A seed is a baby plant, waiting.  
> **Ori**: Soil, water and sunshine help it grow.  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that a seed grows into a plant when it has soil, water and sunshine._

**Ori**: Quick-fire! The Wobbler holds up fruit. You shout the colour!  
_The Wobbler holds up a limon._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, ask): review: colour words in Hebrew  
> **Ori**: The limon! What colour is it?  
> - ✅ Tsahov (yellow) → Tsahov! Yellow, like the sun!
> - Kachol (blue) → A blue lemon? Silly Wobbler! It’s tsahov, yellow.
> _For grown-ups: Named the colour of a lemon in Hebrew: tsahov (yellow)._

_Then a tapuach. Then some anavim, half eaten._  
**Ori**: Tsahov! Adom! Sagol! You’re the colour champion!  
**Ori**: Joint champions. With me. I hate sharing. Fine.  
_The seeds go in your seed tin._  
_A parpar lands on the Wobbler. It is still pink!_  

## LH5. Savta’s Letters

- `lh5-step1`: Look at a letter from Savta’s tin with Ori. · _Sit at the table with Ori. Tap the letter to hear it._
- `lh5-step2`: Draw a family tree with Ori. · _Draw a tree. Put a word sticker on each branch. Any way is right._
- `lh5-step3`: Tell Ori how a Nubbin feels. · _Look at the shy Nubbin by the gate. Is she happy, sad or tired?_

#### `lh5-s1`

_Pitter-patter. Rain. Ori opens his biscuit tin._  
**Ori**: Letters! They belong to my סַבְתָּא (savta, “grandma”).  
_(Say it: SAHV-tah.)_  
**Ori**: Hazel used to write to Savta. For forty years!  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, D2.His.2.K-2, notice): letters, long ago and today  
> **Nova**: Long ago, people sent lots and lots of letters.  
> **Nova**: A letter can take days to arrive. I know. I carried some!  
> _For grown-ups: Heard that people have sent letters for a long time, and that a letter takes days to arrive._

_Ori reads one. “How is the מִשְׁפָּחָה (mishpacha, ‘family’)?”_  
_(Say it: meesh-pah-KHAH.)_  
_“Is the כֶּלֶב (kelev, ‘dog’) still stealing socks?”_  
_(Say it: KEH-lev. Woof!)_  
_In the corner of the letter: a game of noughts and crosses!_  
**Ori**: They played games by post? And Savta is LOSING?  

#### `lh5-s1-done`

**Ori**: Savta, mishpacha, kelev. You know them!  
_The Wobbler climbs onto the letter. It sits there like a cat._  
**Ori**: Just like a חָתוּל (chatul, “cat”)! Off, Wobbler!  
_(Say it: kha-TOOL. Miaow!)_  

> **Lesson** (science, ELOF: Scientific Reasoning, ask): animal sounds  
> **Ori**: A kelev says woof. What does a chatul say?  
> - ✅ Miaow! → Miaow! And purr, when it’s happy.
> - Moo! → Moo is a cow! A chatul says miaow.
> _For grown-ups: Matched animals to their sounds: a dog (kelev) says woof, a cat (chatul) says miaow._

**Ori**: Every letter has a game. Savta is behind. I’m very upset!  

#### `lh5-s2`

_Ori unrolls a long sheet of paper. He draws a tree._  
**Ori**: A family tree. My mishpacha! Savta at the top.  
**Ori**: Mum is אִמָּא (ima, “mum”). Dad is אַבָּא (aba, “dad”).  
_(Say them: EE-mah, AH-bah.)_  
**Ori**: I have an אָחוֹת (achot, “sister”) and an אָח (ach, “brother”).  
_(Say them: ah-KHOTE, AKH. A throaty “kh”!)_  

> **Lesson** (art, do): draw your own family  
> _(Draw your family together. Say each person’s name.)_  
> **Ori**: Your grown-ups, your friends… and maybe a Nubbin or two!  
> Check: `edu:draw:family`
> _For grown-ups: Drew a picture of their own family and named the people in it._

**Ori**: A friend who’s a boy is a חָבֵר (chaver).  
**Ori**: A friend who’s a girl is a חֲבֵרָה (chavera).  
_(Say them: khah-VER, khah-veh-RAH.)_  
**Nova**: Do I go on it? I just carry the letters.  

**Choices**

- **Nova goes on as a chavera.**
    - **Nova**: A chavera! I like that very much.
    - **Ori**: A big branch for Nova!
- **Ori goes on as a chaver.**
    - **Ori**: Me? On your tree?
    - **Ori**: …Chaver. Thank you. You’re on mine too.
- **The Nubbins get a branch!**
    - **Ori**: A Nubbin branch! The Wobbler at the very top.

#### `lh5-s2-done`

_Two trees, side by side. Ori’s family and yours._  
**Ori**: Ima, aba, ach, achot, chaver, chavera. All of them!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): family words in Hebrew: call and response  
> **Ori**: I say it in English. You say it in Hebrew. Mum?  
> _(Wait for: ima!)_  
> **Ori**: Dad?  
> _(Wait for: aba!)_  
> **Ori**: Ima and aba. Easy to say. Hard to argue with!  
> Check: `edu:say:he:family`
> _For grown-ups: Said the Hebrew words for mum (ima) and dad (aba) from memory._

**Ori**: My achot will be cross you learned so fast. Good!  
**Ori**: And my ach borrows my football. Without asking!  
_The Wobbler drew itself on both trees. A circle with arms._  

#### `lh5-s3`

_The rain stops. Drip, drip. The Nubbins come out._  
**Ori**: Feelings! In Hebrew, they change for a boy or a girl.  
**Ori**: Boy: שָׂמֵחַ (sameach, “happy”). Girl: שְׂמֵחָה (smecha, “happy”).  
_(Say them: sah-MEH-akh, smeh-KHAH. Big smile!)_  
**Ori**: Boy: עָצוּב (atsuv, “sad”). Girl: עֲצוּבָה (atsuva, “sad”).  
_(Say them: ah-TSOOV, ah-tsoo-VAH. Sad face.)_  
**Ori**: Boy: עָיֵף (ayef, “tired”). Girl: עֲיֵפָה (ayefa, “tired”).  
_(Say them: ah-YEF, ah-yeh-FAH. Big yawn!)_  
_The shy one sits alone by the gate._  
**Ori**: She’s a girl, I think. מָה שְׁלוֹמֵךְ? (ma shlomekh?, “how are you?”)  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, do): feeling words in Hebrew, with faces  
> _(Say it: mah shlo-MEKH. That’s “how are you?” to a girl.)_  
> _(She’s a girl, so use the girl words.)_  
> _(Make a happy face and say: smeh-KHAH!)_  
> _(Now a sad face: ah-tsoo-VAH. Now a tired face: ah-yeh-FAH.)_  
> **Ori**: Smecha! Atsuva! Ayefa! You’re a great actor.  
> Check: `edu:say:he:feelings`
> _For grown-ups: Said three Hebrew feeling words (happy, sad, tired) in the girl form, for the shy Nubbin, and made a face for each._


**Choices**

- **She’s atsuva. Sad.**
    - **Ori**: Atsuva. Maybe she misses someone. Let’s sit with her.
    - You sit by her. After a while, she leans on your arm.
- **She’s ayefa. Tired.**
    - **Ori**: Ayefa. Waving all day is hard work!
- **She’s smecha. Just quiet.**
    - **Ori**: Smecha and quiet. Some happy is the quiet kind.

#### `lh5-s3-done`

**Ori**: מָה שְׁלוֹמְךָ? (ma shlomkha?, “how are you?”), sleepy one?  
_(Say it: mah shlom-KHA. That’s “how are you?” to a boy.)_  
**Ori**: Ayef. Of course.  
**Ori**: The Wobbler is sameach. Very, very sameach!  
**Ori**: And me? A bit atsuv. I miss Savta.  
**Ori**: And sameach, because I’m here. Both at once!  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Self-awareness, ask): feeling two things at once  
> **Ori**: Sad and happy, both at once. Is that allowed?  
> - ✅ כֵּן (ken, “yes”) → (Say it: KEN.) Ken. Two feelings at once is OK. A cuddle helps too.
> - לֹא (lo, “no”) → (Say it: LOW.) Lots of people feel two things at once. It’s allowed!
> _For grown-ups: Talked about how we can feel two things at once, like sad and happy, and what helps._

_The Wobbler rolls over. It leans on Ori’s foot._  

#### `lh5-complete`

_Ori lifts a little wooden box from the tin._  
**Ori**: Savta sent this for you. A letter box!  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): trace the shape ס (samech)  
> **Ori**: Look at this round shape: ס. It’s called samech.  
> **Ori**: In savta, this shape says “s”.  
> **Ori**: Round, like a hug from Savta.  
> _(Trace it with a finger. Start on the right side.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:he:samech`
> _For grown-ups: Traced the Hebrew letter shape ס (samech), which says “s” in savta. Tracing only, not reading._

**Ori**: For when you write to me. You will write!  
_Quick-fire! The Wobbler points. You say the word._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, ask): review: family words in Hebrew  
> _The Wobbler points at the tin of letters._  
> **Ori**: The letters are about Savta’s… what?  
> - ✅ Mishpacha (family) → Mishpacha! Her whole family.
> - Banana → Ha! Not banana. Mishpacha, family!
> _For grown-ups: Remembered the Hebrew word for family: mishpacha._

_It points at Ori: chaver! At Nova: chavera!_  
_Then it points at itself. A big wobbly smile._  
**Ori**: Sameach! The Wobbler is sameach!  
**Ori**: Oh! The last letter says Savta won the game. Phew!  

## LH6. The New Year of the Trees

- `lh6-step1`: Ask Ori about Tu BiShvat, the trees’ new year. · _Ori is under the old apple tree. Tap him to talk._
- `lh6-step2`: Plant an almond tree with everyone. · _Dig a hole. Put the little tree in. Give it water from the pond._
- `lh6-step3`: Go to Ori’s party and say the Hebrew words. · _Go to the lanterns by the fence. Shout the Hebrew word for each game._

#### `lh6-s1`

_Ori sits under the old apple tree._  
**Ori**: Guess what? Trees get a birthday!  
**Ori**: It’s called ט״ו בִּשְׁבָט (Tu BiShvat, “the New Year of the Trees”).  
_(Say it: too beesh-VAHT.)_  
_The Wobbler climbs the tree. It waves: happy birthday!_  
**Ori**: People plant trees. And eat fruit! Dates, figs, pomegranates.  
**Ori**: A tree is an עֵץ (ets, “tree”).  
_(Say it: ETS. Stretch up tall like a tree!)_  
**Ori**: Let’s plant a שָׁקֵד (shaked, “almond”) tree here!  
_(Say it: shah-KED.)_  

> **Lesson** (social-studies, notice): Tu BiShvat, the New Year of the Trees  
> **Ori**: Tu BiShvat comes in January or February, most years.  
> **Ori**: In Israel, almond trees often flower around then.  
> **Ori**: White and pink flowers, all over the hills.  
> _For grown-ups: Heard about Tu BiShvat, the New Year of the Trees: people plant trees and eat fruit, and in Israel almond trees often flower around then._


**Choices**

- **Plant it by the pond.**
    - **Ori**: By the pond! It can see itself in the water.
- **Plant it where your tent is.**
    - **Ori**: Where my tent is. Something of mine stays here.
- **Plant it by the gate, under the sign.**
    - **Ori**: By the gate! Hello, goodbye, and a tree!

#### `lh6-s1-done`

**Ori**: Tu BiShvat, ets, shaked. You know them all!  
**Ori**: Plus fruit. There’s always fruit.  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, notice): remembering a friend who is far away  
> **Ori**: When I go home, the tree stays here.  
> **Ori**: When you see it, you can think of me.  
> _For grown-ups: Talked about ways to remember a friend who lives far away._

_The Wobbler climbs down the tree. Backwards. Old habits!_  
**Ori**: Hold on tight, Wobbler!  

#### `lh6-s2`

_Everyone comes! Nova, Fern, Clover and Byte._  
**Ori**: This is our גַּן (gan, “garden”). It needs a new tree!  
_(Say it: GAHN.)_  
**Ori**: A tree needs מַיִם (mayim, “water”) and שֶׁמֶשׁ (shemesh, “sun”).  
_(Say them: MY-yim, SHEH-mesh.)_  
**Ori**: And גֶּשֶׁם (geshem, “rain”). Rain is mayim from the sky!  
_(Say it: GEH-shem. Wiggle your fingers like rain!)_  

> **Lesson** (science, K-LS1-1, ask): what plants need to grow  
> **Ori**: What does our tree need to grow?  
> - ✅ Water and sun → Mayim and shemesh! Yes!
> - Sweets → Ha! Trees don’t eat sweets. They need water and sun.
> _For grown-ups: Said what a tree needs to grow: water and sunlight._

**Byte**: Five big steps from anything else. Room for roots.  
**Ori**: One day it will have a פֶּרַח (perach, “flower”). Lots!  
_(Say it: PEH-rakh.)_  

**Choices**

- **I’ll water it first.**
    - **Ori**: Slowly. Mayim in, not mayim everywhere!
- **Ori should water it. It’s his tree.**
    - **Ori**: Our tree. But thank you.
- **Let the Wobbler do it!**
    - The Wobbler tips the can over itself. Splash!
    - **Ori**: Geshem! The Wobbler made rain!

#### `lh6-s2-done`

_A little tree stands in the gan. Drip, drip._  
_The Wobbler pats the soil flat. With its whole body._  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Literacy, do): trace the shape ג (gimel)  
> **Ori**: Look at this shape: ג. It’s called gimel.  
> **Ori**: In gan and geshem, this shape says “g”.  
> **Ori**: It has a little foot, like it’s kicking a ball!  
> _(Trace it with a finger. Start on the right side.)_  
> Check: `edu:trace:he:gimel`
> _For grown-ups: Traced the Hebrew letter shape ג (gimel), which says “g” in gan and geshem. Tracing only, not reading._

**Ori**: מָה שְׁלוֹמֵךְ? (ma shlomekh?, “how are you?”), Fern?  
**Fern**: בְּסֵדֶר (beseder, “fine”)! Muddy, but beseder.  
_(Say them: mah shlo-MEKH, beh-SEH-der.)_  
**Fern**: I’ll look after it. Badly, but with love.  
**Ori**: Is there a perach yet?  
**Ori**: לֹא (lo, “no”). I’ll ask every day until I go!  
_(Say it: LOW. Shake your head!)_  

#### `lh6-s3`

_Ori’s last night. Lanterns glow along the fence._  
**Ori**: This is a פָּנָס (panas, “lantern”). It helps me find the party!  
_(Say it: pah-NAHS.)_  
**Ori**: Party games! I say it in English. You shout it in Hebrew!  
**Ori**: Round one: “Hello!”  

> **Lesson** (literacy, ELOF: Language and Communication, ask): review: saying hello in Hebrew  
> **Ori**: “Hello!” What’s that in Hebrew?  
> - ✅ שָׁלוֹם (shalom, “hello”) → Shalom! Wave, everybody! (Say it: sha-LOME. Wave!)
> - לַיְלָה טוֹב (laila tov, “good night”) → That’s good night! Hello is shalom. (Laila tov is LIE-la TOVE. Hello is sha-LOME.)
> _For grown-ups: Remembered the Hebrew word for hello, shalom, and told it apart from laila tov (good night)._

_Every Nubbin waves. The Wobbler wobbles… and stays up!_  
**Ori**: Round two: count the lanterns with me, up to ten!  

> **Lesson** (maths, K.CC.B.5, do): count to 10 in Hebrew: review  
> _(Point to each lantern. Count together.)_  
> **Ori**: אַחַת (achat), שְׁתַּיִם (shtayim), שָׁלוֹשׁ (shalosh), אַרְבַּע (arba), חָמֵשׁ (chamesh)!  
> _(Say them: ah-KHAT, SHTAI-yim, sha-LOSH, AR-bah, kha-MESH.)_  
> **Ori**: שֵׁשׁ (shesh), שֶׁבַע (sheva), שְׁמוֹנֶה (shmone), תֵּשַׁע (tesha), עֶשֶׂר (eser)!  
> _(Say them: SHESH, SHEH-vah, shmo-NEH, TEH-sha, EH-ser.)_  
> **Ori**: Ten lanterns! Eser!  
> Check: `edu:count:he:lanterns:10`
> _For grown-ups: Counted ten lanterns in Hebrew, achat to eser._

**Ori**: Round three: animals! סוּס (sus, “horse”). אַרְנָב (arnav, “rabbit”).  
_(Say them: SOOS, ar-NAHV. Gallop! Now hop!)_  
**Ori**: Last round. You pick!  

**Choices**

- **Colours!**
    - **Ori**: Point to something אָדֹם (adom, “red”)!
    - (Say it: ah-DOME.)
    - **Ori**: Now something יָרֹק (yarok, “green”)!
    - (Say it: yah-ROKE.)
    - **Ori**: Something שָׁחֹר (shachor, “black”), like the night sky!
    - (Say it: sha-KHOR.)
    - **Ori**: The Wobbler is still a bit וָרֹד (varod, “pink”)!
    - (Say it: vah-RODE.)
- **Family!**
    - **Ori**: Who carries the letters? Nova, a חֲבֵרָה (chavera, “friend”)!
    - (Say it: khah-veh-RAH.)
    - **Ori**: Whose tin is it? My סַבְתָּא (savta, “grandma”)!
    - (Say it: SAHV-tah.)
    - **Ori**: And who’s your חָבֵר (chaver, “friend”)? Me!
    - (Say it: khah-VER.)
- **Feelings!**
    - **Ori**: The sleepy one is עָיֵף (ayef, “tired”).
    - (Say it: ah-YEF. Big yawn!)
    - **Ori**: And me? שָׂמֵחַ (sameach, “happy”). Very sameach!
    - (Say it: sah-MEH-akh. Big smile!)

#### `lh6-s3-done`

**Ori**: You did it! Hello, numbers, animals, and more!  
**Nova**: עֶרֶב טוֹב (erev tov, “good evening”), everyone.  
_(Say it: EH-rev TOVE.)_  
**Nova**: Panas off soon. Then לַיְלָה טוֹב (laila tov, “good night”).  
_(Say it: LIE-la TOVE. Big yawn!)_  

> **Lesson** (sel, CASEL: Relationship skills, ask): saying goodbye and thank you  
> _Ori goes home tomorrow._  
> _What could you say to him?_  
> - ✅ Thank you for teaching me! → Oh. That’s the nicest thing anyone has said.
> - Nothing → Goodbyes can feel hard. A wave is enough.
> _For grown-ups: Thought about how to say goodbye and thank you to a friend who is leaving._

_The Nubbins gallop like a sus, then hop like an arnav. Bedtime!_  
**Ori**: מָה שְׁלוֹמְךָ? (ma shlomkha?, “how are you?”), Wobbler?  
_(Say it: mah shlom-KHA.)_  
_The Wobbler waves, and falls asleep halfway. Shh._

_Morning. Ori’s bag waits by the gate, with his panas tied on._  
**Ori**: בֹּקֶר טוֹב (boker tov, “good morning”). The shemesh is out.  
_(Say it: BO-ker TOVE.)_  
**Ori**: I’m sad to go. And happy I came. Both!  
**Ori**: The chant, one last time. New verse! Everybody!

> _(Say them: ah-DOME, yah-ROKE, ko-KHAV, yah-REH-akh.)_

_By the pond, the little shaked waits. One day: perach!_
